# Simulation notes

Moved from [development.md](../development.md) on 2026-11-07.

## Simulation (M3)

`glasnost-sim` runs the game rules on the typed ruleset and map. Everything in it is integers and the soft-float types of `glasnost-num` (S-0205: IEEE binary32/64 in integers, round toward zero under `quirks.chop_rounding`), with ordered containers only.

- **Rules and data.** `rules::SimRules` is built from a resolved ruleset (`SimRules::new(&resolved.ruleset, &resolved.sim_rules_hash)`): typed per-kind tables, the 443 quirk switches of `rules::Quirks` (every one defaulting to the original; `refused_death_keeps_unit` of S-0107 claim 17 is not consulted, since M3 has no death handling), and engine defaults cited to their specs. A type without a locomotor field has the teleporting locomotor (S-0207 claim 14); vehicles with the driving locomotor drive and infantry with the walking one walk, and every other locomotor is not moved in M3. `consts` holds the spec constants given as decimals, as checked bit patterns.
- **A game.** `load::new_game(rules, map, catalogue, setup)` follows S-0009 and S-0202's table *Draws during map loading*: the first round of house-creation draws (S-0202 claim 14: one R(450, 1800) per house in house order, unused; skirmish and network starts only, so not in our viewer's sandbox, a design choice), the early phase of start positions on blank cells (lists 1 and 2, the assignment against list 2, with the base rules' passive fields, claims 17 to 20 and 33; `starts`, with the start-cell variant of the nearby-cell search, `path::start_cell`), the second round of house-creation draws (the same list again), the initial fill, the overlay type stream placed overlay by overlay (`overlays`: the slope condition of claim 12, high-bridge cells as each overlay is placed, wall connection bits, low-bridge end markers with their pieces' draws), the overlay data stream, the first recalculation pass, the vein rebuild with its draws (claim 14), the tile animations each recalculation starts with their creation draws (S-0010 claim 19, S-0202 claim 10: the first recalculation of a cell before the second pass, then at most one per cell from the second pass on, in play too; `load::recalc_cells`), the pre-placed objects (with the visceroid draw of a vehicle's placement and its follower-car link, claim 41), the second pass, the unused-house cleanup (claim 28 and S-0202 claim 14: only in a game with no AI house, S-0412 claim 12, and fewer human slots than the base rules' maximum player count; a removed house loses its pre-placed objects, takes no position and gets no starting units, and taking its objects out of their ground cells recalculates those cells; `House::removed`), the late phase (list 3), the owners of the map's walls (S-0210 claim 14), the starting units (`starting`, claims 45 and 46: the placement search's acceptance test reads the ground occupants of the ground layer only and takes as the nearest the one whose reference point lies nearest the cell corner by the table root, ties by the ground list's order), the rest of game start (S-0412 claim 14: crates, then the secret-lab draws), the game-start pass of S-0010 claim 12 (a third recalculation of every legal cell, with its tile animations, after those draws by S-0202 claim 14) and the bridge spans of S-0010 claim 25, whose low spans give their bands the bridge-body mark (`Cells::bridge_body`, `glasnost_map::bridges`; the burrowing test of S-0010 claim 17 is to read it). Cell animations (S-0009 claim 12) take their creation draws when their overlay is placed, before the tile animation of the same placement. Terrain recalculation itself is `glasnost-map`'s (S-0010, S-0211). `GameSetup` gives each house a role (player, neutral, special), whether it is an AI house (`HouseSetup::ai`), the unit-count setting and the bases setting. `load::sandbox_setup` is our viewer's setup: one house per object owner of the map, no start positions and no starting units.
- **Wall connection bits.** `walls` keeps a wall's overlay data value as 16 × damage stage + connection bits (S-0112 claim 7), recomputed when a wall is placed from the map's overlay list, when crushing removes one, and when a gate or wall tower is placed or destroyed (`quirks.gate_tower_fixed_cells`). The map viewer draws the cells after loading, so walls show the bits the simulation gives them.
- **A tick.** `tick::step` runs S-0203's parts in their places: the turn pass over the update order (with S-0203's removal skips and second turns), each object's behaviour part (`process`, S-0215), its locomotor (`drive` with `tracks`, S-0207 and S-0206 claim 12: a driving vehicle in the same behaviours stops at the track target where its mobile target comes in range, S-0207 claim 19 a, with the walker's conditions and range test, `walk::arrival_stop_holds`; the hovering locomotor is `hover`, S-0503; a vehicle with the teleport-capable flag drives on every assignment but a qualified docking one, S-0207 claim 28, `refresh::begin`, [m8-claims/intake39-S-0207.md](../m8-claims/intake39-S-0207.md); ships move by the driving code since M6a (section *Simulation (M6a: naval units)*); `walk`, S-0208: a walk ends on its arrival tick in the destination's cell, claim 16, and an approaching walker in *attack*, *area guard*, *hunt* or *rescue* stops at the standing position where its mobile target comes in range, claim 17; the walker's speed fraction of S-0310 is `WalkState::fraction`), its infantry sequence (`infantry`, S-0107), then the orders (`orders`, S-0213), then the tick number. Occupancy is `cells`/`occupancy` (S-0209), crushing `crush` (S-0210), passability and regions `passability`/`regions` (S-0211, S-0212), pathfinding `path` (S-0212) behind the `Pathfinder` trait, over (cell, search layer) pairs, with the corridor of claims 13 to 14 in `corridor` (sectors and connections are derived data, rebuilt with the regions). Since step 1i (S-0212 revision 49), `quirks.corridor_retry_no_fallback` (default on, the original) decides what follows a failed repeated coarse search: on, the computation fails; off, one more cell search without a corridor, final (departure D2's chain-length failure fails whatever the switch; `corridor::coarse_why` tells it apart). Since S-0212 r82 (M8 A3 intake 34) every building gives the corridor exemption to its exemption rectangle and an aircraft round its placement and landing cells, so a unit leaving its base or passing a short line of buildings finds the way on the first cell search; the switch's case is now a strip of ground the unit cannot enter, and `quirks.corridor_free_part_ignores_height` (default on) a ledge's. Each house keeps threat records (claim 16a, module `threat`), read by the coarse search's threat term and the straightening passes for a unit whose coefficient exceeds 0.00001 ([m8-claims/intake34-S-0212.md](../m8-claims/intake34-S-0212.md)). Since r87 (intake 44) a walker's failed gate clears its destination after a fresh search or a re-path, a failed search ends its movement in progress, and a new destination waits out a running retry delay for every locomotor ([m8-claims/intake44-S-0212.md](../m8-claims/intake44-S-0212.md)). Regions and sectors follow the rules of claims 3 and 13b and exact ties the recording order of claim 12a (departures D4 and D7, accepted by the project lead).
- **Bridges in the path search.** Each placed high-bridge overlay gives its four cells the deck-entry mark (three of them) and the bridge axis (S-0212 Terms); once at game start `bridges::find_spans` finds the bridge spans of S-0010 claim 25 from the bridge and wooden-bridge role tiles, makes them S-0212's bridge links (enabled when high) and gives the bands of low spans the bridge-body mark and their axis. Links, marks and axes are state (`Cells`); regions join the end cells of enabled links, sectors gain their connections (with claim 13b's side cells, which the place of the first end's tile in its bridge role decides), decks are read through the links where S-0212 claims 4, 6, 14 and 25 say so (`regions::reach_read`), and the coarse end cells and F8's anchors follow claim 13b. The burrowing test (`path::burrow_ok`) is S-0010 claim 17's, the tileset's allow-burrowing flag carried per tile in the tile catalogue. Links do not change in play yet: no bridge is destroyed or repaired before the bridge specs (S-0212 claim 3's table is a seam).
- **Orders and input.** Every change a player or our AI makes goes through `orders::issue` (pending lists, S-0213). `targeting` turns a click or key command into orders by S-0214 and S-0213 table T2, for people and AI houses alike; the selection is kept by the caller.
- **Saves, replays, hashes.** `save::save`/`load` store the whole state with the rules hash; `save::state_hash` is SHA-256 of the state's bytes (derived data such as regions and the path search's memory is not state). `save::Replay` holds the rules hash, the setup and every order with its tick.
- **Marks of the map load.** The loader marks the high-bridge cells of each high-bridge overlay as it is placed (S-0211 claim 15) and gives the map's walls their owners once the buildings are placed, before any starting unit (S-0210 claim 14). Gates start closed (S-0215 claim 35); a gate's door moves at every turn of the gate, before its per-tick start and processing (claim 35 (d), which answers Q-0030); S-0209's gate rows read `process::gate_open`, and a unit that meets the *closed friendly gate* outcome as it moves (not while it only asks) calls `process::gate_met`.
- **Animation start events.** An animation created with no loop delay (`load::anim_created_at`) takes its creation draws (S-0202 claim 10), then its start event (S-0109 claim 13): with μ = 0 the immediate middle event of S-0109 claim 14 and S-0202 claim 15 (the requested particles' creation draws, then, below 30 leptons above the terrain at its position, the scorch-or-crater choice when the type has both flags and a crater's removal of 6 bales from the containing cell by S-0401 claim 3), then its chain reaction. μ is ⌊n/2⌋ of the sprite of the type's last sprite-found moment (S-0109 claim 25), which the importer reads during the art lookup (`middle_frame`, 0 when no sprite was found; absent without a theater, when the middle event takes no draw and leaves a note). Tile animations stand at S-0010's *Tile animation position* (the tile's `TileNNXOffset`/`TileNNYOffset` through S-0101 F6), cell animations at the overlay cell's centre moved by 384 leptons along +x and +y, at the surface height of the overlay cell's centre (S-0009 claim 12). Seams: the smudge selection (S-0312 claim 26; smudge types are not modelled, so no type fits and it takes no draw, as for destructions; for the same reason `quirks.smudge_footprints_beyond_map_edge` of S-0312 revision 17 is not consulted) and a chain reaction on a resource cell (S-0401 claim 20, the economy topic) take no draw and leave a note; a resource glint (S-0202 claim 14) has no position in any spec, so its middle event takes only its particle draws and notes the rest. Animations created in play with no loop delay (warp, weapon, warhead and death animations) take the same draws through the same function (S-0202 claim 15). A middle event later in playback (μ > 0) comes on the advance that reaches μ, in the animation's turn (`anims`, M8 step A5).
- **Owners that are not human-controlled.** An AI house counts as human-controlled (glossary *AI house*); the neutral and special houses do not, and keep the original's rules for such owners (glossary *Player houses only*): skill level 0 (S-0209 claim 11), starting units in *area guard* (S-0009 claim 45), crates blocking their walkers (S-0209 row WO1), the idle scatter of a frightened fraidycat (S-0107 claim 14), lying down while moving (S-0107 claim 15), the computer-controlled branch of S-0208 claims 11 and 15, *area guard* when a unit gives up after a failed path search (S-0212 claim 23 row 2; its house's centre choice is S-0215 claim 38's, `base::selected_centre`), the resting rows 7 to 10 of S-0215 claim 14, a harvester's harvest wherever it stands (S-0215 claim 15 row 4) and the auto-deploy row of S-0215 claim 31 (with the auto-deploy list's entry for difficulty index 1; without one the row does not apply), *hunt* for the crews and survivors a death releases (S-0312 claim 25), and the attack-target crushing approach and the omni-crusher's opportunity-target exemption (S-0210 claim 11; both crushing approaches replace the approach step of S-0215 claim 37 (h), `approach::step`). Their house rate-of-fire and firepower factors are exactly 1.0 (S-0301 claim 3, S-0302 claim 3, S-0412 claim 5). `House::human` carries the distinction. A player house with `human` clear is a *test-only computer-controlled player house* (glossary revision 58; S-0215 and S-0309 Terms, M7b step 1c): it takes every one of these computer-owner rules too, the acquisition rules of S-0309 claim 25 (`combat::acquire::player_owned` is "a player house with `human` set") and the retaliation of S-0215 claim 18 included, but keeps its player-house setup (row factors, credits). Only the oracle arena makes one, and it sets the oracle house's skill level and difficulty index. The auto-deploy delay reads the list's entry for the owner's difficulty index (index 0 for such a house; `GeneralRules::auto_deploy_delays`). Seams for such a house: S-0309 claim 25 parts g and i read difficulty index 1 only, the automatic repair start uses R(0, 0) where the original computer player uses its difficulty row's repair-start delay (S-0314 claim 15 and F4; the difficulty rows are not imported), and the damage scatter of vehicles (S-0215 claim 19) is not built.
- **Selection.** `targeting::selectable` is S-0214's *can be selected*: a unit or building in play whose type's selection flag (`selectable`, default yes) permits it, but not a building whose type is invisible in the game; a building awaiting removal with 0 health stays selectable (claim 16). Units below the surface and cloaked units are an open question of S-0214; M3 has neither state. The acknowledgement's response (S-0214 claim 14, F5) is `targeting::selection_response` with n from `targeting::selection_responses`, the count of the type's imported selection-response list (`voice_select`, every entry kept). The power, alliance and surrender orders of S-0213 table T1 now point to S-0405 claim 10 and S-0414 claims 11 to 14 and are not implemented (no order variant yet), nor are S-0214 claim 13's sell, repair and power modes. The move-target marker (S-0214 claim 30): `targeting::click` sets `ClickResult::marker` when the first selected object, a unit, handles a cell click with *move* (a force-move included; never an object click, a building's click or a *no-move* click), `targeting::move_marker_at` places it at the cell centre on the terrain surface, 416 leptons higher on any intact high-bridge deck cell (the original's unmarked-deck defect is corrected, PLAN D6), and the session hands it to the live view at once (`Live::move_marker`), on the clicking screen only. Since M6b it plays the move-feedback animation `[General] MoveFlash` (S-0214 Import mapping) unattached at that point, created between ticks so its first update is in the next tick (S-0109 claim 5), with its draws from the view's cosmetic stream (claim 14: no scenario draw); the viewer shows its animation placeholder only when the type has no sprite.
- **Not yet in M3.** Combat (firing, damage, target acquisition) came with M4 (next section); the behaviours other topics own are processed as behaviours without a processing rule and leave a note in `World::notes`. Infantry death (S-0107 claim 17), fear (S-0107 claim 16) and hunt's target search (S-0215 claim 27) are M4's.

The determinism gate: `cargo test` checks the first 300 ticks of `crates/sim-gate`'s three synthetic replays (`testkit::scripted_replay`, movement; `testkit::combat_replay`, two sides fighting under `testkit::combat_rules`; the scripted skirmish of `glasnost_ai::skirmish` on `glasnost_ai::fixture`, two scripted houses that deploy, build, harvest, produce and fight until one is left) against `golden/scripted.txt`, `golden/combat.txt` and `golden/skirmish.txt` in a debug build, and the three AI duels of M5b and M6a (section *Own AI (M5b)*) against `golden/duel.txt`, `golden/duel-diagonal.txt` and `golden/duel-naval.txt`; `cargo xtask gate` checks full runs (3000 ticks each for the first two, the skirmish until its game is over, at most 40000 ticks, keeping every 10th tick's hash) natively in release and as wasm32 under node (CI job *gate*). The skirmish's replay is recorded by running the scripts, then replayed without them; `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/skirmish.rs` also checks that the game ends, that each house went through the M5a loop, and a save and load in mid-game (release: `cargo test --release -p glasnost-ai -- --include-ignored`). `tests/scenario.rs` also saves a combat game while shots are in flight, loads it and checks that both copies fight on with the same hashes. After a deliberate change of behaviour, regenerate the file with `GLASNOST_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate -- --include-ignored` and say why in the commit.

The M6c duel (`duel-m6c`, M6c step 5). The straight, diagonal and naval duels run on `glasnost_ai::fixture`, which has no type of the M6c abilities, so they exercise none of them (counted at the start of step 5: every M6c order counter of `Game::special_uses` is zero in them, and no garrison, capture, infiltration, radiation, erasure, bomb, strike or crate event happens). A fourth duel, `golden/duel-m6c.txt` (`Scenario::DuelM6c`, index 6 of `Scenario::ALL`; `crates/sim-gate/src/m6c.rs`), plays the own AI against itself on the synthetic M6c ruleset and map of `glasnost_ai::fixture_m6c` (the fixture's ruleset plus invented types; stock values nowhere): 12 000 ticks (`M6C_TICKS`; the duel stops earlier if a side wins), every 10th tick's hash, seed `M6C_SEED` = 1 (at M6c step 5 a seed that exercised every ability, one of five of 24; at M7a step 2b every seed of 1 to 32 exercised every ability and seed 1 had the largest smallest counter; since step 2e all but seed 13 do and seed 1 still has it, see *Margins* below; seed 2 since M8 A3 intake 110, when S-0309 claim 30's *guard* request at a tech building's capture made seed 1 end at tick 9605 with no radiation exit ordered, while seed 2 still plays all its ticks with smallest counter 4, ledger `docs/m8-claims/intake110.md`; `S=1,2,3 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate --test m6c try_seeds -- --ignored --nocapture` lists the abilities each seed missed, `GLASNOST_SWEEP=1-16 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate --test m6c m6c_seed_sweep -- --ignored --nocapture` prints every counter's minimum, median and maximum over the seeds and each seed's counters below 3). What the map and the types supply (each house starts with its units as pre-placed map objects; the AI's own rules do the rest):
- **Houses and map.** Alpha at start 0 (west) and Beta at start 1 (east), 32 cells apart; a neutral passive house (country *Neutral*) with, near each start, two garrison buildings (HUT, civilian tech level), two tech buildings that pay (DERR: acquisition bonus 300, income 25 every 21 ticks; S-0515) and two spyable power buildings (SPYB); an islet of 3 × 3 land cells ringed by two cells of water north of each start, with the *other* house's unarmed DECOY on it (a target that no walking unit reaches, so the jumpjet module has one; S-0502); the crate setting on with six crates at the start (a power-up table of money only; S-0520).
- **Starting units (pre-placed, 27 infantry and 5 vehicles per house, two cells apart; counts of M7a step 2b).** Four garrison occupiers (GARRI), three engineers (ENGI), three spies (SPYU), deploy-to-fire infantry (DEPI), two radiation-immune deployers with a radiation weapon in the second slot (RADI; S-0525, S-0215 claim 31), three temporal-beam units (ERAS; S-0514), two electric chargers (ZAPR; S-0518), four bomb planters (PLNT), two suicide units (KAMI), a unit with explosive charges (SABO) and two disarming infantry units (DISA); an in-place deployer (DEPT), a deploy-to-fire vehicle (FIRE), two air-strike designators (DESIG; their aircraft STRK) and a disarming vehicle that deploys into a building (DISV, so that the army builder leaves it at home). No house starts with a jumpjet unit: the barracks builds two armed jumpjet types, JETI and JET2 (alike; its door kind gives each one the ascent report of S-0417 claim 15). Toward the middle from each start stand two HULKs of the *other* house: unarmed, sturdy, costly vehicles whose armour class only the temporal, bomb-planting and disarming warheads touch (every other warhead's versus value there is zero, so no other weapon may fire at them, S-0304 claim 1); pre-placed buildings of a player's house are dropped in a skirmish (S-0009 claim 27), which is why they are vehicles. The barracks also builds GARRI, JETI, JET2, DEPI, RADI, ERAS, ZAPR, PLNT, DISA, KAMI and SABO (the army builder takes them in turn with the rifleman); the AI builds the prism type PRSM (also overpowerable, with a support reach and the general prism rules) by its own rule (S-0518). The refinery, power plant, barracks and war factory are spyable (the refinery stores money, so a spy can steal it).
- **What the full run must show.** `m6c::play` counts the AI's orders per ability (`Uses`, summed over the houses) and the simulation's events (`Exercise`: occupants inside garrisons, captures, infiltrations, income cycles of held tech buildings, ticks with a jumpjet in the air, built jumpjet units that rose from their barracks (S-0417 claim 15), deploy and undeploy sounds, ticks with radiation, erasures begun, support beams, bombs planted, air strikes called, crates picked up before their time), and `Exercise::missing` lists every one that is zero; the gate's full run (native and wasm32) and the test `every_m6c_ability_is_exercised_and_the_replay_reproduces_the_game` (`crates/sim-gate/tests/m6c.rs`) assert that the list is empty, so the gate cannot go on passing after a change that makes an ability drop out of the game. Counts of the run of seed 1 (M7a step 2e, with the memory change of *Orders a unit did not take*): garrison orders 37 (6 occupants at most inside at once), engineer 6 (4 buildings captured), spy 6 (6 infiltrations), tech building 4 (income cycles 2343), jumpjet 34 (a jumpjet in the air for 8688 ticks; 7 built jumpjet units rose from their barracks), deploy 185 (6 changes of form), radiation exits 16 (radiation on the map for 2445 ticks), erasure 88 (6 begun), prism 8 (28 support beams), charger 15, bomb planter 102 (11 bombs planted), disarm 9, suicide 44, sabotage 14, air strike 9 (10 called), crate 130 (7 picked up). At step 2b, before that change, it gave erasure 280, charger 131, bomb planter 289 and suicide 254 orders. At M6c step 5 the same seed gave engineer 2, spy 2, tech 2, radiation exits 2, 1 erasure begun and disarm 4.
- **Margins (M7a step 2b).** With the M6c step 5 scenario the sweep of seeds 1 to 16 gave these minimum / median per counter: garrison orders 16/23, occupants 2/3, engineer 2/2, captures 2/2, spy 2/2, infiltrations 2/2, tech 2/2, income cycles 1176/1178, jumpjet orders 17/36, flight ticks 10492/11983, built jumpjets 2/3, deploy orders 23/56, changes of form 4/4, radiation exits 1/9, radiation ticks 1800/3833, erasure orders 79/264, erasures begun 0/1, prism 6/6, charger 24/247, support beams 12/25, bomb planter 79/238, bombs planted 0/1, disarm 0/1, suicide 72/185, sabotage 4/15, air-strike orders 0/2, strikes called 3/4, crate orders 61/100, crates picked up 4/8; seeds 3, 4, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13 and 15 each had a counter at zero (they would fail the gate). The setup changes above (more engineers, spies, occupiers, erasers, bomb planters, disarmers, deployers and designators at the start; a second DERR and SPYB per side; the HULKs; no starting jumpjet unit and a second buildable jumpjet type, because the army builder takes the type with fewest on the map and a surviving starting jumpjet kept the barracks from building more) give, over seeds 1 to 16: garrison orders 12/26, occupants 1/4, engineer 6/6, captures 3/4, spy 5/6, infiltrations 5/6, tech 4/4, income cycles 1765/2342, jumpjet orders 22/49, flight ticks 8688/8688, built jumpjets 4/7, deploy orders 30/81, changes of form 3/6, radiation exits 5/23, radiation ticks 2445/4463, erasure orders 22/249, erasures begun 3/7, prism 6/6, charger 3/204, support beams 13/31, bomb planter 32/242, bombs planted 7/32, disarm 6/11, suicide 15/144, sabotage 3/13, air-strike orders 7/9, strikes called 6/9, crate orders 57/121, crates picked up 3/8. Every counter's minimum is at least 3 except the most occupants at once (1, seed 10 only; median 4). Over seeds 1 to 32 no counter is ever zero; four seeds have one counter below 3 (10: occupants 1, 18: crates picked up 1, 26: jumpjet orders and built jumpjets 2, 28: sabotage orders 2). The AI was not changed for this: no choice of the AI is forced or scripted, the map and start units only give each ability an occasion. Further tuning was tried and dropped (six occupiers instead of four moved the low counters to other seeds: crates picked up 0 in seed 2; frail jumpjets and higher start credits did not help). After the memory change of step 2e (fewer repeated orders, so the games go differently), seeds 1 to 16 give: garrison orders 10/24, occupants 1/4, engineer 6/6, captures 3/4, spy 5/6, infiltrations 5/6, tech 4/4, income cycles 1765/2342, jumpjet orders 27/46, flight ticks 8688/8688, built jumpjets 3/7, deploy orders 30/85, changes of form 3/6, radiation exits 0/16, radiation ticks 2445/4471, erasure orders 45/109, erasures begun 3/7, prism 6/6, charger 13/27, support beams 18/30, bomb planter 49/109, bombs planted 7/32, disarm 6/11, suicide 26/66, sabotage 3/11, air-strike orders 7/9, strikes called 6/8, crate orders 56/121, crates picked up 3/7. Seed 1, the gate's, still has smallest counter 4; over seeds 1 to 32 one seed has a counter at zero (13: radiation exits; it would fail the gate) and eight have one below 3 (4: radiation exits 2; 10: occupants 1; 18 and 32: crates picked up 2; 20 and 22: crates picked up 1; 26: jumpjet orders and built jumpjets 2; 28: sabotage orders 2).
- **Save and load, replay.** The run saves and loads the game at the halfway tick and at the first tick each kind of M6c state exists (garrison occupants, a jumpjet in the air, radiation, an erasure, a planted bomb, an air strike, a crate); the loaded game must hash alike and the run goes on from it. The test above also replays the finished game (`Game::run_replay`: setup and the persons' orders, the AI runs again) and requires the same final state hash.
- **Not exercised as an event.** The *orders* for the engineer and spy sidebar rules (a house that has none orders one) are not used: ENGI and SPYU are not buildable here (their counters count the orders to the starting ones); the sidebar orders are covered by `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/special.rs`. A disarm *order* happens but the disarm event itself is not counted separately, nor are suicide and sabotage hits (their orders are).
- **Findings from building the scenario (AI), fixed at the merge.** (1) A crate that landed on a water cell was sent a ground unit all the same (`glasnost_ai::crates::same_region` let a goal outside every reach region pass), which then kept its *move* behaviour for ever without moving; now such a goal rules the unit out (test `a_crate_on_water_sends_no_walker`). (2) The prism order was given at every think until three prisms stood or were queued; it now waits for a free building line (test `no_prism_order_while_the_building_line_is_busy`). Erasure, bomb and charger orders came by the hundred in a long game; most were the same unit re-ordered at the same goal (fixed at M7a step 2e, see *Orders a unit did not take*). The counters count orders given, not units used. (3) Starting units next to a deployed radiation-immune unit leave its field (that is what the radiation counter shows).

The M7a duel (`duel-m7a`, M7a step 4). A fifth duel, `golden/duel-m7a.txt` (`Scenario::DuelM7a`, index 7 of `Scenario::ALL` and of the node list in `xtask/src/gate.rs`; `crates/sim-gate/src/m7a.rs`), plays the own AI against itself on the synthetic ruleset and map of `glasnost_ai::fixture_m7a` (the fixture's ruleset plus invented types; stock values nowhere): 12 000 ticks (`M7A_TICKS`), every 10th tick's hash, seed `M7A_SEED` = 17 since M8 step A4 round 5 (note 24: the absorber keeps slaves, so seed 92 absorbs no infantry; seeds 17 and 26 are the only ones of 1 to 40 with every counter at least 3, 17 the lowest), 92 since M7b AI round 3 (the margin setup's step 2a-bis below: no seed of 1 to 16 has every counter at least 3, seed 92 is the only one of 17 to 104 that has); 27 from the economy-defence change at the end of M7b AI round 2, when seed 4 gave no takeover order and no seed of 1 to 16 had every counter at least 3 (seed 27 is the only one of 17 to 40 that has; seeds 1, 11 and 13 miss only by the sensor orders' 2); 4 earlier in that round (16 since M7b step 2a, where seed 16 gave 2 sensor orders and 2 bunker entry orders after the round-2 AI changes; the only seed of 1 to 16 that plays all 12 000 ticks with every counter at least 3, the sensor orders included, on the margin setup of that step, see *Margins (M7b step 2a)* below; before: 1 from M7b step 1c, when Alpha won seed 2 at tick 11 331 and seed 1 was the lowest seed that played all 12 000 ticks with every counter at least 3 but the sensor orders' 2; 1 until M7a step 3.3, 2 until M7a step 1g, when Alpha won seed 2 at tick 10 314; 3 until M7a step 1n, kept at M7a step 3.6, where seed 3 still played the full run with the grinder and Alpha's hulks; back to 1 at step 1n, because since step 1m Alpha wins seed 3 at tick 11 222 and seed 1 is the lowest seed that plays all 12 000 ticks with every counter at least 3 but the sensor orders' 2; 2 again since step 1o, where seed 1 gives only 2 takeover orders and seed 2 is the lowest that plays all 12 000 ticks with every counter at least 3 but the sensor orders' 2). Alpha plays a Yuri-style side and Beta a fixture side: Alpha has no refinery or harvester of the fixture but a slave miner (SMV, three SLAVE walkers, built at the war factory) whose deployed form SMB is also its sidebar refinery (S-0402 claim 14; the required-country lists of S-0407 claim 5 (b) keep each economy to its side, the owner lists stay shared because the factory test of claim 11 reads them). Both sides have the berserk rifleman MADMAN (weapon MADRAY, warhead MAD with the berserk flag; six per house at the start, two cells apart, and the barracks builds more), the psychic sensor EYE (radius 12, built by the AI) and MINDB, which gives the psychic reveal REVEAL (buildable once a war factory stands; a skirmish drops pre-placed buildings of a player's house, S-0009 claim 27). Since M7a step 3.3 Alpha alone (required-country lists) also has the gattling vehicle GATV (six stage slots holding SPIN0 to SPIN2 of damage 6, 9 and 12; 3 stages, spin-up 4, spin-down 2, thresholds 20, 60, 120; built at the war factory as an army type), the gattling defence GATB (same slots and settings, built by the AI's defence rule) and the mind controller TAKER (a rifleman whose weapon THRALL has the takeover warhead MIND, capacity 4, range 2, cost 200); its victims are Beta's units. The lobby gives 15 000 credits (`fixture_m7a::CREDITS`), which is what lets both houses reach MINDB within the run: with the fixture's credits no seed fired a reveal. `m7a::play` saves and loads at the halfway tick and at the first tick each kind of M7a state appears (a reveal fired, a berserk unit, a hostile-intent line seen through a sensor, a slave-miner building, a slave at work, a gattling at its top stage, a gattling building spun up, a controlled object); the full gate run (native and wasm32) and `every_m7a_ability_is_exercised_and_the_replay_reproduces_the_game` (`crates/sim-gate/tests/m7a.rs`) assert that `Exercise::missing` is empty and replay the game to the same final hash. Counters (`Exercise::counters`) and their minimum / median over seeds 1 to 16 (`GLASNOST_SWEEP=1-16 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate --test m7a m7a_seed_sweep -- --ignored --nocapture`), measured at M7a step 3.3 after merging the late start of M7a step 1b: berserk weapon orders 3/21, units made berserk 20/34, psychic sensor orders 2/2, ticks with a hostile-intent line seen by a sensor 1982/3517, psychic reveal orders 6/12, reveals fired 6/12, slave-miner buildings placed or deployed 5/8, ticks with a slave at work 6312/8073, gattling stage rises 9/15, ticks with a gattling at its top stage 1396/2954, takeover orders 6/15, takeovers 10/15. Alpha wins 7 of the 16 games before 12 000 ticks (seeds 4, 5, 7, 11, 12, 15 and 16; before step 3.3 every seed ran the full run, with stronger gattling weapons or a larger controller capacity Alpha won all 16, and with a controller range of 3 or a price of 300 some seed gave only 1 or 2 takeover orders), and seed 1 also ended early with an earlier tuning, so the seed moved to 2, which plays the full run in every tuning tried. Seed 2: berserk orders 23 (16 and 7), units made berserk 53, sensor orders 2, intent-line ticks 3 517, reveal orders 12 (6 and 6), reveals fired 12, slave-miner buildings 7, slave ticks 8 513, gattling stage rises 28, top-stage ticks 2 846, takeover orders 11 (all Alpha), takeovers 10, gattling defence orders 2 and kept spins 337 (Alpha). `Exercise::building_spin_ticks` (ticks with a gattling building spun up) is kept but not asserted: Alpha builds its two defences, but in most seeds no enemy reaches them within the run (0 in seed 2). **Until M7b step 2a the sensor orders were 2 in every seed, below the margin of 3:** the AI orders a sensor only while its house has none (`psychic::build_sensor`), so two houses give two unless one is lost and built again. No setup tried moved it (a sensor of strength 1, starts 22 cells apart instead of 32, 16 000 ticks; with the closer starts games ended before the full run, and a house that lost its sensor at tick 10 451 had not ordered another by 12 000 while it held 850 to 1 900 credits; why was not traced). The counter stays, so that a change which drops the sensor from the game fails the gate; the sensor's margin is in the intent-line counter, which only a built sensor can raise. **Adding a type and a counter:** (1) insert the invented type in `fixture_m7a::base_ruleset` (with `register`; a side's own type gets that side's required-country list; `duel-m7b` builds on `base_ruleset` and `base_map` too, while the gate-only margin setup of step 2a is in `fixture_m7a::margins` and `fixture_m7a::map`); (2) for a start unit, add its name to `fixture_m7a::INFANTRY` or `VEHICLES` (both duels), or to `fixture_m7a::START_VEHICLES` (this duel only); (3) add a field to `m7a::Exercise`; (4) count it in `Exercise::observe` (a new set in `Watch` if it is a beginning, as `berserk`), and call `watch.first(present, "name")` there if its first appearance deserves a save and load; (5) add its line to `Exercise::counters` and raise the array's length; (6) run the sweep above (every minimum at least 3, the median well above) and then regenerate the golden with `GLASNOST_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate -- --include-ignored`, which must change only `duel-m7a.txt` (and `duel-m7b.txt` when the change is in `base_ruleset` or `base_map`).

**Margins (M7b step 2a).** The AI audit's `harvesters_wanted` (one harvester per 20 explored resource cells near the refineries, up to twice the plan's) made the fixture's spreading fields (about 228 resource cells by mid-game) keep up to four harvesters per house, and the armies came late: over seeds 1 to 16 gattling stage rises fell to 0/2 (minimum / median), gas clouds 0/2, bunker entry orders 0/4 and lift orders 0/5, and Alpha won 4 games early (seeds 2, 3, 4 and 12). The step changes only the scenario's setup, in `glasnost_ai::fixture_m7a::margins` (rules) and `fixture_m7a::map` (places), on top of `base_ruleset` and `base_map`, which `duel-m7b` keeps unchanged; no choice of the AI is forced or scripted, the AI's code is untouched. (1) **Ore does not spread** (`ORE` spread 0): each field stays at its 25 cells, so each house keeps the plan's two harvesters and spends the rest. (2) **PREY**, an unarmed, sturdy (2 000), unbuildable vehicle with GUNR's speed and size, of the last armour class (index 10), at which every warhead but the lifter's MAGWH and the controller's MIND has a versus value of 0 (no other weapon may fire at it, S-0304 claim 1): three of each house stand near the *other* house's start, toward the middle (6 cells in at rows −3 and 0, 10 cells in at row −6). They are what a person's lifter and mind controller go for: the lifters lift them (lift orders, seized and dropped vehicles), Alpha's controllers take them, and a taken one is sent to the grinder. (3) **Start vehicles** (`START_VEHICLES`, a row at dy −3): Alpha a GATV and a MAGN (its gattling fights from the first wave, which also gives the gas clouds, and its lifter stays home by the AI's own rule), Beta a second MINR (with Alpha's two extra units Beta's economy broke in some seeds and its sensor and intent counters fell to 0 or 1). (4) **Alpha's HULKs** stand in a column 7 cells behind its start instead of north of it, out of the first raids' way. (5) **The sensor EYE is frail** (strength 10 instead of 1 000), so a raid can destroy it and its house orders another (`psychic::build_sensor` orders one only while the house has none). Tried and dropped: ore spread off alone (gattling and gas still 0 in most seeds); three GUNR for Beta and two GATV and a GUNR for Alpha (10 of 16 games ended early, 6 of them before tick 5 100); one GUNR for Beta (it raided at once and killed Alpha's damaged HULKs: grinder 0 in every seed, and in 15 of 16 with the HULKs moved behind the start); a MAGN for Beta (it lifted Alpha's GATV: gattling stage rises 0 in 4 seeds); a GUNR as Alpha's second unit (Beta collapsed, intent ticks 0 in 5 seeds); all PREY 10 cells in (zeros in 4 seeds); PREY at speed 1 (taken prey never reached the grinder, the controllers stayed full and gave no orders, takeover orders 0 in one seed); a sensor of strength 100 (a rebuild in 1 seed of 16). Counters over seeds 1 to 16 with the final setup (minimum / median): berserk weapon orders 25/48, units made berserk 30/55, sensor orders 1/2 (3 in seeds 7 and 16), intent-line ticks 27/3 121, reveal orders 6/12, reveals fired 6/12, slave-miner buildings 3/12, slave ticks 5 691/7 072, gattling stage rises 9/26, top-stage ticks 1 403/3 850, takeover orders 2/20, takeovers 5/25, gas clouds 64/181, gas hits 132/554, absorber entry orders 5/14, infantry absorbed 5/6, grinder entry orders 8/17, units ground 2/11, bunker build orders 3/4, bunker entry orders 2/7, vehicles bunkered 1/4, bunker exit orders 1/3, lifter build orders 3/4, lift orders 5/13, vehicles seized 11/17, seized units dropped 9/14. No counter is 0 in any seed. Alpha wins 4 games before tick 12 000 (seeds 4 at 10 931, 5 at 11 543, 9 at 11 760, 11 at 11 549), as many as before the step; setups with no early end (before MIND could affect PREY) had a counter at 0 in some seed. Seed 2 shows an order flood: from tick 9 000 Alpha's lifter grips a PREY at height 0 beside it that is never dropped, the brain re-sends the lifter at a GUNR each think and it turns back to the held PREY, about 110 lift orders while the drops stay at 11 (a brain or S-0607 claim 8 question, left to the AI work; seed 16 has no such flood). Seed 16, the gate's, plays all 12 000 ticks: berserk orders 72, units made berserk 54, sensor orders 3, intent-line ticks 3 995, reveal orders 14, reveals fired 14, slave-miner buildings 13, slave ticks 6 970, stage rises 25, top-stage ticks 2 851, takeover orders 35, takeovers 28, gas clouds 121, gas hits 375, absorber entry orders 24, infantry absorbed 6, grinder entry orders 22, units ground 14, bunker build orders 4, bunker entry orders 7, vehicles bunkered 6, bunker exit orders 4, lifter build orders 4, lift orders 12, vehicles seized 17, dropped 13 (gattling building spun up 0, not asserted); the run saves and loads at the halfway tick and at the first tick of each kind of state, and the replay reproduces it. **The sensor orders stay the weak counter:** in a two-house game they reach 3 only when a house loses its sensor and its AI orders another, which needs the house to keep its harvesters (the brain's sensor rule waits for them); in the sweep that happened in 2 of 16 seeds (in the others the sensor was lost, if at all, by the house that was being overrun, which then kept no harvesters and ordered nothing). A third house (one more sensor order per game) was not tried: it would change the duel throughout. Seed 16 is the only seed of 1 to 16 that passes, its sensor orders at exactly 3 from one loss and rebuild, so most changes of the AI or the simulation will move it. Proposal (not made): define the counter over what a person does with the building, e.g. "every AI house ordered a sensor" (per-house orders at least 1) or count sensors placed, and keep its margin in the intent-line counter. The sweep prints, per seed, the ticks played, whether the game ended early, every counter and those below 3, and a line with the early ends.

**Margins (M7b AI round 3, step 2a-bis).** Setup and seed only (`glasnost_ai::fixture_m7a` and `M7A_SEED`); the AI is untouched by this part. With the round's AI the setup above left zeros in 6 of seeds 1 to 16 (takeover orders 0 in seeds 4, 5, 9 and 16; grinder entries and units ground 0 in 5 and 12; the bunker counters 0 in 7 and 8, which ended early). Causes and changes: (1) the raids' berserk hits sent Alpha's damaged HULKs hunting and killed them before the grinder stood, so the HULKs take PREY's armour class (only the lifters and the controllers touch them); (2) five PREY per house instead of three (two more 14 cells in, rows −3 and 2), each worth 1 credit, so a controlled one ground pays next to nothing (S-0603 claim 8); (3) a start TAKER for Alpha (`START_INFANTRY`, row −5), so a controller is out before the first wave; (4) the bunker zeros: a probe of seed 4 showed every tank of the house fighting (start vehicles from tick 150, dead by 2 250; later tanks taking fights near the base before they idle) when the bunkers stood from tick 4 350, so no vehicle was free to go in. **WARD**, an unbuildable GUNR-like turreted vehicle (strength 2 000) whose one gun sits in a multi-weapon slot with no primary weapon, stands 7 cells behind each start: it is bunker-eligible (S-0606 claim 1: turreted, with a current weapon), and the AI, which counts a unit with a primary weapon (or a gattling's slots) as army, leaves it out of its waves and base defence, so it is idle when a bunker stands. (Our AI's `armed` reads only the primary weapon and the gattling slots; a stock multi-weapon unit with no primary would be left at home the same way. Not changed here: setup only.) Tried and dropped: six or eight PREY (slave miners broke, more zeros); two start TAKERs and two GUNRs for Beta (Alpha won 11 of 16 early); one TAKER with two Beta GUNRs (6 early ends); Beta's two GUNRs with the TAKER (2 early ends, bunker zeros in seeds 4 and 5); a bunker of cost 150 (zeros in 5 seeds, 2 early ends); WARD in the start row (killed in the first raids) and a lone PREY 9 cells from the start TAKER (the TAKER's own target search took it before the brain's order: takeover zeros in 3 seeds). **Counters over seeds 1 to 16 with the final setup (minimum / median):** berserk weapon orders 32/84, units made berserk 63/108, sensor orders 2/2, intent ticks 2 709/4 524, reveal orders 12/12, reveals fired 12/12, slave-miner buildings 12/13, slave ticks 5 974/7 443, gattling stage rises 8/18, top-stage ticks 1 058/3 100, takeover orders 1/2, takeovers 6/17, gas clouds 85/258, gas hits 310/2 094, absorber entry orders 7/16, infantry absorbed 5/7, grinder entry orders 5/10, units ground 4/5, bunker build orders 4/4, bunker entry orders 4/8, vehicles bunkered 3/7, bunker exit orders 2/7, lifter build orders 4/4, lift orders 6/15, vehicles seized 11/18, dropped 10/17. **No counter is 0 in any seed of 1 to 16**, and no game ends early. The weak counters are the sensor orders (2 in 15 of 16 seeds: a third needs a lost sensor rebuilt) and the takeover orders (1 or 2 in 10 of 16: a controller's own target search usually takes a victim before the brain looks, so the brain's order counts only victims beyond that search and within its reach). No seed of 1 to 16 has every counter at least 3; of seeds 17 to 104 only seed 92 does (sensor orders 3 also in 5, 50, 59 and 72, each short of takeover or bunker counters), so `M7A_SEED` moves from 27 to 92 (raw lists git-ignored under `local-lane/m7b/ai3/m7a-sweep-14*.txt`). **Seed 92, the gate's:** berserk orders 38, made berserk 77, sensor orders 3, intent ticks 3 590, reveal orders 12, fired 12, slave-miner buildings 13, slave ticks 7 055, stage rises 8, top-stage ticks 1 122, takeover orders 12, takeovers 19, gas clouds 81, gas hits 305, absorber orders 8, absorbed 6, grinder orders 11, ground 4, bunker builds 4, bunker entries 11, bunkered 5, exits 5, lifter builds 4, lift orders 18, seized 22, dropped 21: every counter at least 3. `duel-m7b` uses `base_ruleset` and `base_map` and is unchanged by this setup.

The headless runner: `glasnost-headless synthetic [ticks] [seed]` prints the per-tick hashes of the synthetic replay, `glasnost-headless replay <file>` those of a replay file. The local lane: `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- smoke [ticks] [seed] [index]` loads every map of the install in the sandbox setup, orders every mobile unit to another unit's cell at tick 4, runs the ticks and writes counts and timings (no names, no values) to `local-lane/sim-smoke.txt` (with each map's shots, detonations, kills and most projectiles in flight). A skirmish pass also sets each map up as a skirmish of the tool's design (`headless::skirmish_setup`: as many players as the lobby allows, all on random positions, the neutral and special houses, unit count 10, bases on), loads it without running it and counts the maps loaded and refused (with the reason) and the starting units created. The scripted skirmish (M5a): `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- skirmish [ticks] [seed] [index …]` sets up the first two stock maps with two start positions (or the listed map indices) as skirmishes of two AI houses (`econ::setup::skirmish`, default settings, the first two open countries that own a harvester type the scripts can use, else the first two open countries; the scripts use only harvester and army types the simulation moves: a vehicle or infantry unit with the teleporting locomotor jumps by S-0207 claim 20 and S-0208 claim 19; the scripts use no aircraft), lets `glasnost_ai`'s script play both through orders for at most `ticks` ticks (default 120000, a ceiling; with seed 7 the two default stock maps end after some 11000 and 17000 ticks) or until the game is over, and writes to `local-lane/skirmish.txt` the rules' type counts by the script's roles, the players' country indices, the end tick and the winner's house index, the order count, the objects each house had by role, the ticks in which its credits rose, the credits it gained, its harvesters' ticks by current behaviour and harvest phase, its credits at the end and the types it could build then by role, the game's shots and kills, and whether the replay without the scripts and a save in mid-game, loaded and continued, reproduce the final state.

Provisional rules of the simulation, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `load` | not implemented yet: the removal of a removed house's triggers by the unused-house cleanup (S-0009 claim 28; map triggers are not implemented), header pairs as start position marks (claim 29; the importer keeps an unread component of pairs 9 … 16 unread, for the mark in force at each phase to complete), observers (claim 22), the tags of cell tags and of pre-placed objects with their draws (S-0011 claim 34), wall buildings (S-0112 Terms), a departing wall tower's wall hits (S-0112 claim 7, S-0303 F4), terrain object types that only the map names (S-0009 claim 42 creates them with health −1; our engine places nothing there, with a note), and the bridge-body mark that a destroyed high bridge sets (S-0010 claim 25: bridges are not destroyed yet) | — |
| `objects` | pre-placed objects (S-0009 claims 34 to 43) carry no veterancy value or tag (a vehicle with the bridge flag stands on the deck where its cell has one, else on the ground), start switched on, and aircraft stand landed; follower cars carry their leader (claim 41) but are not sent after it (S-0212 claim 26 is a seam) | — |
| `process` (route row, a seam) | routes are not built. When they are, S-0215 revision 39, claim 13 applies: under our owner-routes departure both repair and reload route-point alliance guards and the non-allied fallback test use the actor owner's house; the simulation has no machine-local house | Q-0663 (answered; not built) |

## Simulation (M4: combat)

Combat lives in `glasnost_sim::combat`, driven from the tick pipeline and the behaviours of M3; every phase runs in S-0203's places (no component picks its own order). Its rules are typed in `combat::data` (`CombatRules`, built inside `SimRules` from the schema's combat fields: weapons, projectiles, warheads with their versus values read at double precision, per-type `TypeCombat`, countries' multipliers, the general values and the difficulty rows). The quirk switches the combat specs name are in `rules::Quirks` (236 switches now, each defaulting to the original), plus `clear_weapons_any_turret_count` and `coefficient_zero_replaced` (importer, `convert::sim::SimQuirks`) and `percent_list_integer_prefix` (`ini::Quirks`); the quirk inventory test checks every switch the simulation, movement, infantry, rendering and combat specs propose.

- **Firing (S-0301, S-0310, S-0313).** `check` is the firing check in S-0301 claim 9's rank order, with S-0310's aiming answers (turret and body facing, the building aim point); `fire` makes a shot (burst counter, the delay kinds and their draws, ammunition and reloading, firing-frame sync, the full delay F1 and the reload F3), `position` the fire point (the 3 × 4 single-precision matrices of S-0313 F1, burst alternation, building pixel shifts F3), `behave` the firing parts of the behaviours (vehicles S-0310 claim 9, infantry S-0107 claim 11, buildings S-0215 claim 32 with the turret snap), and `ballistics` the arc speed and reachability.
- **Weapons and acquisition (S-0308, S-0309).** `weapon` chooses the slot by table P (with table N, gattling and switching types, elite slots), the mean damage of a healer for acquisition and the versus lookup (S-0304, S-0305); `acquire` holds the searches (ring order F2, candidates by table C, threat F4 to F9, early stops, the timer draw first), retaliation, anger, expected remaining health and the per-tick search after an actor's processing, area guard and hunt.
- **Projectiles (S-0306, S-0307).** `projectile` launches (straight and arcing pitch, scatter F5), flies (turning F7, steering F10 to F12, the rows of tables T1 and T2), detonates (detonation point, cluster draws, airburst children) and removes shots; projectiles are kept in `World::projectiles` in update order, with IDs from the object counter. `range` is the range test (elevation bonus F2, measured and source points, the high-bridge refusals) and `lof` the line-of-fire walk.
- **Damage (S-0302, S-0303, S-0304, S-0305).** `damage::apply` runs S-0302's stage table (F1 to F3, F8, thresholds, readiness loss, damage effects and building fires, reactions: anger, fear and retaliation, the killing hit with delayed kills and credit); `area::detonate` the area damage (ring metric, radii, wall hits, exploding overlays).
- **Death, veterancy, healing, suppression (S-0311, S-0312, S-0314, S-0315).** `death` removes killed objects (nearby point F2, debris, death weapons, infantry death sequences, crew and survivors, building destruction, terrain objects), `veterancy` ranks and abilities (and the experience a pre-placed object starts with), `heal` self-healing, `fear` suppression and panic.
- **Aircraft retaliation and the marked-water death (S-0215 claims 10, 16, 18, 19; S-0309 claims 18 and 25 part h; S-0501; S-0312 claims 10 to 12; M8, [air-retaliate.md](../m8-claims/air-retaliate.md)).** `acquire::retaliate` now judges an aircraft as any other actor and overrides it to *attack* (`override_attack`), on its pad too (S-0501 r154 claim 52, intake 114: the Q-2792 interim and `air::on_its_pad` are gone; the override keeps the pad contact and gives no destination). In `air::pad_turn` a settled aircraft with the *attack* of a retaliation current is told to queue *sleep* (T3's last row) unless `quirks.aircraft_pad_sleep_ends_retaliation` is off, which skips it as one in *enter*; servicing ends when no settled aircraft is left after the first-bullet release (claim 26); and claim 28's half-reload test with a target comes before the round and the repair, so at full ammunition with a target a step neither repairs nor releases. `air::fall_back_rows` gives an object of a missile type (the missile-spawn flag) the rows for an aircraft on the ground whatever its height (claim 18, *Missile types*; for a flagged type that is not a rocket type this was the Q-2821 interim, which S-0501 r172 answers the same way: intake 118 replaces the interim with r172's cite). The S-0309 claim 18 row for harvesting slaves, slave masters and spawner types (`seams::is_harvesting_slave`, `seams::is_slave_master`, `spawn::spawn_rules`) now answers `MayNot` for every kind. Case 1 of S-0215 claim 19 (`damage_scatter_sent`) is unchanged, but it no longer reaches an aircraft that retaliates, because that aircraft is answered `Done`. In `death::kill`, a vehicle that carries the bomb mark and dies within 10 leptons above a water cell (`marked_low_over_water`) skips the debris, claim 5's passenger kill and the death weapon. It shows the game-wide wake and the last splash-list entry (`wake_and_splash`, no draws) in place of claim 9's animations, and its attached bomb still goes off. The defeat sweep's repeat (`Repeat::marked_water`) does the same. A marked vessel never sinks (`naval::sinks_at_kill`). The water test (S-0312 r66, Q-2793 answered, intake 114) reads the height above the surface, from the deck on a bridge deck (`World::height_above_surface`), 10 leptons or less with no lower bound, and the land type of the cell at the vehicle's position whatever its layer; `quirks.bomb_mark_water_death_on_deck` off fails the test for any vehicle on a deck. Claim 12's height rule and claim 15's crash start read the same height from the deck, and claim 12's bomb-mark column tests the mark alone. The mark is written by `hover::start_fall` (S-0503 claim 11) and by `fall::start(.., true)` for a vehicle dropped from a collapsing bridge deck (S-0511 claims 9 and 10).
- **Crews and survivors (S-0312 claims 13, 18, 19 (g), 21, 24, 25, F4, F5; S-0408 claim 7; M8 step A7, [a7-S-0312-survivors.md](../m8-claims/a7-S-0312-survivors.md)).** `combat::survivors` holds the shared rules. `crew_type` is F4: the owner's country's side (S-0412) picks the side's crew (`[General]` crew of side 0, 1 or 2) or, for an armed object, the technician on a percent draw below 15; no side, or a side past 2, gives the technician (armed: one discarded percent draw). `count` is F5's *C* (the refund K of S-0408 claim 1 over the side's divisor, doubled for a building that ever changed owner, `combat.captured`; 1 to 5; 0 for no crew, `combat.no_crew`, a type without `Crewed`, a side past 2 or no side, or a divisor of 0). `destruction_round` is claim 18 with claim 19 (g): from the first cell after those the absorbed passengers used (claim 24, `absorb::destroyed` returns the count), per foundation cell the chance R(0, *g*), the type draws (*p*, the engineer below 25 for a building factory that never changed owner, F4), the creation draw, a free standing position (Q-2132's interim: the first free one in table P order), health R(5, S), the scatter request and the order (attack the charge planter when the owner is not allied with it, else *move* for a player house and *hunt* for the neutral and special houses, claim 25); then the cell's smudge step when the cell is S-0211 category *open* (Q-2133's interim). Killing damage that ignores defences sets `combat.no_crew` first (F5). Claim 21's second round (`quirks.building_removal_second_round`) calls the round again from the first cell in `death::removal_turn`, with the source taken before the building leaves play. A vehicle's crew (claim 13, `death::crew`) uses `crew_type` and `place_infantry` with half the type's strength. `sold` is S-0408 claim 7: exactly *C* attempts from the release step of a sale (`econ::sell::release_occupants`, after the absorbed passengers and the garrison), each its type choice (repeated while it gives a second engineer, at most 100 times), the creation draw, R(0, *n* − 1) for the cell and S-0208's centre-class row, full strength, facing 0, the forced scatter request and *move*. Seams: the map's no-crew flag (Q-2131; no map sets it), `Crewed` absent on a building type means no (Q-2135), smudges (claim 26's plain form: no smudge type fits, no draw).
- **Effects.** `combat::effects` lists the cosmetic events of a tick (animations, detonations, shots, deaths) in `World::effects`, which is not state (it is skipped by saves and hashes); the viewer draws them.
- **Repair steps (S-0314).** `heal::repair_step`, `step_size`, `step_cost` (F2 with our engine's departure) and `building_repair_step` are the step rules; nothing asks for a step yet (depots, hospitals and the repair order wait for the structures and economy topics), and a building's step cost is F2 on S-0408's adjusted cost (`seams::building_adjusted_cost`, which calls `econ::sell::adjusted_cost`).
- **Input rules.** S-0214's armed, healing actor, claim 6 (d) and (e), rows I16, I21, GC4, AC8, ACC, BC3, BC4, k1, table R and the idle order read the weapon choice, the range test, the firing check's legality answer and veterancy abilities. Row I8's *can garrison* (S-0214 rev 29) is S-0508 claim 1's occupation test, `targeting::may_garrison`: garrisonable type, occupier actor, the building's owner the actor's own house or a passive house (alliance plays no part), not built up or sold, centre in the usable area, not being erased, occupant count not equal to capacity, not both positive health and badly damaged, actor not mind-controlled (tests in `claims_s0214_garrison.rs`). Occupants are `structures::garrison` (M6c, section *Simulation (M6c: garrisons)*); erasure and mind control are seams that answer no.
- **Seams.** Not modelled yet, each left where its topic plugs in: bunkers, transports and open-topped passengers, cloaking and disguise, berserk, mind control, spawned aircraft and flight (`weapon::in_air` is false), parasites, crates, bridges (deck damage), smudges, particles, animation playback (a weapon that waits for an animation detonates at once), the production cost factors (1.0), the map's inert and harvester-immune flags, turret-locked weapons (not imported), the weapon cursor flags of S-0214 rows I17 and I18, and economy, production, power and shroud (S-04xx, M5). Where a behaviour or combat rule asks an M5a topic (slave miners and the harvesters' guard rules of S-0402, available credits and payment of S-0404, short of power of S-0405, adjusted cost and refund of S-0408, the defeated state of S-0414), it calls `glasnost_sim::seams`. The harvesters' guard rule, short of power, available credits, payment, the adjusted cost, the refund and the defeated state are connected to their M5a topics; the slave group's parts answer from `econ::slaves` (S-0402 claims 14 and 19 to 28).
- **Claim ledgers.** `docs/m4-claims/S-03xx.md` lists every claim of each combat spec as tested, seam, partly or not implemented, with the tests that check it (`crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s03*.rs`, each citing `// spec: S-xxxx claim N`). `testkit::combat_ruleset` and `testkit::game_with` give the invented combat ruleset and an empty-map game for such tests.

Q-0236 is answered by S-0312 claim 16: a killed aircraft with an empty explosion list shows no animation and takes no draw (`combat::death::kill`).

Q-0230 and Q-0237 are answered by S-0307 claims 21 and 25: an outside-area alternative fails a non-winged actor's clear-cell region test, a *winged* actor's clear-cell test accepts without the occupancy, region, land and wall tests (`combat::range::approach_search`), and after the close-range shortcut an actor of any locomotor but walking gets only the destination (`approach::shortcut`).

Q-0661 is answered by S-0610 revision 3, claim 1: a psychic-weapon or psionics immunity that no rules layer gives is *yes* for building types and *no* for infantry, vehicle and aircraft types (an initial default, not a reset by a later layer that omits the key). `building_rules` in `glasnost_sim::rules` applies it, so an otherwise damageable building ignores a psychic-damage hit unless its data turns the immunity off (`claims_s0302::s0610_claim_1_psychic_immunity_defaults_by_kind`). The psionics immunity's other consumers (the berserk-weapon firing check of S-0610 claim 3 and the control effects of S-0601) are not built; berserk hits already exclude buildings by S-0302 claim 10.

Q-0662 is answered by S-0613 revision 3, claim 1 and its Edge cases: a rules set without the shared gas system type is not rejected and draws no warning; the type is created at game start and reads no section, as S-0004 claim 3 says, which is what `convert::layers` (`finish`) already did (`refs_tests::game_start_particle_system`). The created type's values (lifetime −1, no behaviour kind, no held particle type) are for the gas clouds of S-0613, which are not built.

Provisional rules of combat, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `combat::range::approach_search` | the two edges S-0307 leaves open: a vehicle alternative off the map's legal cells never passes the clear-cell test; claim 22 (e) withdraws a close-range attacker's own entry on a copy of the cell's record for the clear-cell test, so the order of the cell's occupants never changes | Q-0172 |
| `combat::damage` (berserk hit), `combat::fire`, `combat::acquire::friendly_exception`, `targeting::berserk` | the berserk code predates S-0610 (now revision 3) and follows only S-0302 claim 10 and F3: a berserk hit sets `berserk_until` = now + *T*, read by the halved rearm and the friendly-target exception; `targeting::berserk` is still a seam (always false). Building types now default to psionics immunity (S-0610 claim 1, Q-0661), but buildings were already excluded from berserk hits. Not built yet (S-0610 is wave 2): the firing refusals of berserk weapons (claim 3), dropping the target and queuing *hunt* on going berserk (claim 4), the countdown in the unit's own updates and the *guard* or *hunt* at expiry (claim 5), the order block and other while-berserk rules (claim 6), including the exemption from S-0215 claim 33a's clearing of allied targets by computer-owned units (that rule is not built either); a hit with *T* ≤ 0 has no effect, which is the off state of `quirks.berserk_zero_duration` rather than its default | none (S-0610, wave 2) |
| (garrisons) | retired by M6c: the garrison occupants, the shared index and its resets are built (section *Simulation (M6c: garrisons)*); the open part, a dead occupant among the firing choices, is Q-0970 there | Q-0970 |

S-0301 revision 16, S-0215 revision 44 and S-0406 revision 9 answer Q-0798, and its row is retired. The weapon providers of a building are its upgrade positions 1 through the used count (at most 3), in increasing order, empty positions skipped (`combat::weapon::installed_upgrades`); a count of 0 supplies none, even with types still recorded, and a count above 3 uses the three valid positions. The attack gate applies only with a nonzero count and an occupied position 1, and tests that type only (`combat::weapon::gate_upgrade`, `combat::behave::upgrade_gate`): an empty position 1 bypasses it, and a level-skipped installation in position 2 imposes none. A passing gate makes its primary and secondary attempts at once, also for an animation-delayed building, and starts no delayed shot. A failed gate starts neither an attempt nor a delayed shot; with `quirks.upgrade_attack_weapon_gate` off it takes the ordinary path, delay included. A secondary slot that resolves to no weapon does nothing (`combat::fire::shot` returns before any change or draw). S-0308 claim 12's own text still says "first installed"; S-0406 claim 12's position order is followed, a pending cross-spec change on the spec side. Ledger: `docs/m6-claims/step2-core.md`.

## Simulation (M5a: economy and base building)

- **Power, shroud and radar (S-0405, S-0409, S-0410).** `econ::power` keeps each house's totals and blackout (`HousePower`), starts a pre-placed building switched off for a map flag of 0 only when its type drains or needs power (`map_off_applies`, claim 18), works them out in the house update when an event has made the house due (placement, removal, owner change, switch, health noticed in the building's own turn, blackout start and end), and answers `low_power` and `fraction` (the double that S-0403 claim 5 reads). `in_operation` is the live test of claim 8 (its needs-engineer row reads the saved *manned* state, `BuildingSwitches::manned`, set by `power::owner_changed` at a building's first ownership change and never cleared, S-0515 claim 2; save format version 16) and `firing_answer` is table T1 at S-0301 rank 4 (`combat::check`); the one-off effects (control-centre count, gap field, super-gap end) run in the building's own turn through `econ::events::turn`. `power::switch` applies the power orders. `econ::shroud` keeps per player house the cover count, hostile gap count and marks of table S1 (plus one shared off-map state), applies sights in the objects' turns and at building placement, sets the discovery mark of every house on a placed object's usable cell (S-0409 claim 20), runs the optional regrowth and then the re-cover sweep before the turn pass (regrowth first on a tick where both are due, S-0409 claim 18), gap fields, the spy satellite's reveal and the whole-map re-cover (taken back and re-applied in object order), the reveal on fire (`combat::fire::shot`), area reveals for other specs, `defeat_reveal`, discovery, the point test of S-0110 claim 8 (which the radar map applies at a cell's surface height at its centre, S-0410 claim 6) and the query `explored`. `econ::radar` rechecks claim 1 in the house update after an event (power first, then radar and the spy satellite); `on_defeat` turns radar on for good and reveals the map. `econ::minimap` holds the radar map's pure functions (point grid F1, scale F2, cell at a pixel F3, object pixel F4, a building's pixel set F8 (`building_pixels`), flash F5, terrain mean F6, tile colour F7, overlay table T1, listing, arrivals and `compose`) for the viewer; nothing there is game state. Seams, each a small function in `econ::power` that answers "no" or 0: pulses and locks, being erased or drained (being drained is built at M7b step 3.3, S-0608), chargers, absorbed passengers, occupants; the superweapon charge hold (claim 15) and the super-gap's deploy behaviour (claim 16). Since M7b step 1c (S-0405 revision 20, Q-1503 answered) a vehicle is disabled on a completed cell arrival (`power::arrival`, from `cloak::machine::cell_movement_completed`) and claim 20 says what disabling does: the guard reset, deselection (`Effect::Deactivated`), the locomotor off, the deactivation and activation sounds (`Sound::Deactivate`, `Sound::Activate`); a disabled vehicle is not movable, is left out of the selection box, and `orders::issue` refuses its behaviour, stop, scatter, deploy and guard orders (`refused_while_disabled`, also for the own AI). Since M7b step 1e (S-0405 revision 21, Q-1505 answered) a vehicle on its own house's repair depot is passed over at the 1-to-0 transition only if it is tethered to that depot and the depot stands in its cell (`power::tethered_in_own_cell`, which reads neither the pad flag nor the repair phase; S-0314 claim 17 decides when depot entry tethers), and a passed-over vehicle is disabled at its first completed arrival in a cell without a building (`claims_s0405_depot.rs`). S-0301 revision 26 (*Deactivated firers within rank 4*) gives the firing check's answer: *illegal* for every target and weapon, after the busy check and the own-gripped-target *rearming*, and yielding to the materialising firer's *rearming*; it outranks every other rank-4 answer (`claim_20_deactivated_illegal_outranks_absent_weapon`). A disabled vehicle with a voxel body is drawn at trunc(b / 2) ‰ (S-0104 revision 26 claim 13; `Mobile::disabled`, `mapview::build`); sprite vehicles stay undimmed (open in S-0405). `production::power_changed` (S-0405 claim 11) works out every line's step period again; S-0409 claim 11 reads `victory::defeated`. Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0405.md`, `S-0409.md`, `S-0410.md`.
- **Production, tech tree and the sidebar's rules (S-0403, S-0404, S-0407, S-0411).** `econ::production` holds every house's lines in creation order (`World::econ.lines`), the build time F1 to F7 (`build_time_of`, `period_of`, on the power fraction `power::fraction`, refreshed by `power_changed`, which S-0405's house update calls), the price with cost bonuses, pad term and free unit (the adjusted cost is `econ::sell::adjusted_cost`, S-0408's), available credits and payment (`available_credits`, `pay`, behind `seams::available_credits` and `seams::pay`), pay-as-you-go steps in tick row 6, holds, cancels and refunds, the queue, the choice of the releasing building (primary, else last acquired), a busy or failed release cancelled at once with the refund, and the factory checks of S-0404 claim 8 (called from `econ::events`). An item's object is created at the start (the creation draw of S-0404 claim 11) and stays off the map until it is released or placed. `econ::release` releases a done unit by S-0404 claim 13's rows (ground vehicle factory with the exit displacement and the hand-off to an idle twin, naval factory toward the rally point, infantry factory and cloning building with the door cells and the exit lists of `quirks.foundation_exit_lists`, aircraft factory with the dock or the edge entry and its draw) and makes claim 14's copies at the house's cloning buildings. The way out is S-0417's (ledger `docs/m5-claims/S-0417.md`): a ground factory with the vehicle-factory flag queues *unload* (current at a per-tick start that finds the ready mark set, `quirks.factory_unload_waits_ready_mark`) and gives the released vehicle a contact and tether (`ObjectEcon::exit`) and the factory's rally point as its saved guard spot; `release::process_unload` runs the stages of table S1 (apron clearing at the apron cell `apron`, from the exit list's eleventh pair with `quirks.factory_apron_short_list`, by scatter requests to E's occupants and then its occupied neighbours; the door through `behaviour::Door` on the gate door time; sending out on the 68-point exit track T1, `drive::begin_exit_track`, with tunnelling and teleporting vehicles on a temporary drive, or to F4's point with `quirks.factory_fixed_hover_exit` for the others, which can be a cell of K's own foundation: a vehicle in contact with a building may enter that building's non-blocking cells, S-0209 claim 5 row VP3 in `occupancy::vehicle_outcome`; when F4's cell holds another building, S-0212 claim 24's substitute replaces the vehicle's destination cell in `path::compute` (departure D3's "replaces"), as it does for every unit whose destination cell is substituted, so the move ends there; waiting; closing, which queues *guard*, started by the ready mark as *unload* is). `release::arrival` is the cell-arrival update (from `drive`, `walk`, `hover` and a `teleport` landing): the clear report ends contact and tether, then the run-away reply of claim 8 (harvest, the saved rally as destination, or a scatter) for a vehicle factory. A naval factory takes two processings. An infantry unit is tethered until its first cell arrival. An infantry unit released by a factory with a rally point gets the rally as its guard spot before it is put in play, so the placement fall-back (S-0215 claim 13's guard-spot row) makes the rally its destination; it then walks to its exit cell with *move* queued and the rally kept as its guard spot for the second leg, unless its type is a jumpjet or teleporting one, which heads straight for the rally (claim 10); without a rally point, or with one in its own cell, the release gives it no destination, and S-0417 claim 17 sends it off a cell that holds the factory (`scatter::resting_turn`, M8 A3 intake 35; *No path*, r68, intake 43: a walker whose destination waits for the retry delay has movement in progress and refuses it, one whose search failed accepts it), so its first arrival frees the contact place and later units come out. An infantry factory has max(*d*, 1) contact places, *d* its docking-place count, and its exit search takes a cell only on the free result of the unit's own cell-entry test (S-0417 claim 14). A docked aircraft appears at S-0404 claim 15's pad position (the foundation centre plus the docking displacement of its contact place for a pad or repair building; `DockingOffsetN` imported into `docking_offsets`), and an edge-entering one without a rally point gets *move* forced with the nearby-cell search's result around the factory's reference cell (S-0417 claim 14). A docked aircraft gets contact and an exit tether when its type need not return to a dock (claim 11; the tether ends on take-off, S-0501 claim 8 in `air`). An idle vehicle factory in guard without the stupid-guard-mode flag makes no apron test at the first processing after the behaviour starts and sends claim 4's clearing requests at every later one, before its delay draw (`release::idle_apron`, claims 12 and 13). `release::holds_back` adds S-0215 claim 11's vehicle-factory exceptions to the readiness test, and `orders` ignores stop, scatter and deploy for a tethered unit and keeps the link on behaviour orders (S-0213 claims 8 and 9, T3 b). `econ::tech` gives the tech-tree answer (override, secret type, tech level, country lists with the index wrap, stolen tech, superweapon setting, prerequisites with groups and upgrades, build limits), the present, owned and ever-built counts, the taken-up set (which is also the icon set) and its rechecks at the house update (`tech::house_update`; a recheck returns the types it removed), the queue-time limit and `assign_secret_labs` for S-0412's game start. `econ::sidebar` has the click tables of S-0411 claims 4 and 5 as pure functions (`click`), the icon states, the legality of production orders (`legal`, which `orders::issue` applies to every house), and, at every player house's update, the cancel and cancel-all orders of S-0411 claim 2 and S-0404 claim 9 for the types a recheck removed (`removal_orders`; the start of a sale removes nothing) and the automatic release order of claim 9. Darkening (claim 3) and the at-limit tests use the build-limit answer with the production allowance (`tech::at_limit`). A build order, the queue's forced start and the factory check of S-0404 claim 8 use the limit-only test `tech::could_make_limit_only` (S-0407 claim 13, S-0404 claims 3, 8 and 10), so a prerequisite loss alone neither cancels nor drops an item; a factory building queued to be sold takes no start, queue request, resume or release (S-0411 claim 14). Each player house keeps its icons per tab in S-0004 claim 11's insertion order (`HouseTech::icons`; the new offers of one recheck in S-0407 claim 17's order: kinds by the acquisition of their earliest still-owned qualifying factory, then registration index), and the removal orders follow the tab order and then that icon order (S-0411 claim 13). The superweapon setting (`setup::superweapons_allowed`) and don't-score vehicle entries (`victory::unscored_vehicle_entries`) come from S-0412 and S-0414. A free dock (`production::dock_free`) is a contact place not held by a released or reserving aircraft (S-0501 claim 23), and the dock-bound queue test uses the house's pad total (S-0407 claim 16, S-0501 claim 30). Seams: vehicle-thief take-overs and infiltration (`tech::infiltrated`, for the special-abilities specs). Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0403.md`, `S-0404.md`, `S-0407.md`, `S-0411.md`, `S-0417.md`.
- **Placement, selling, repair, deploy and pack-up (S-0406, S-0408, S-0415).** `econ::placement` has the cell test (`cell_test` with a `Test`: the placing house, whose walls the exceptions of claim 8 accept, whether place-anywhere counts, and an object to ignore), the foundation cells (the 8-cell 3 × 3 word), adjacency (F1), explored ground, `place_legal` (what `orders::issue` asks of a place order with a cell), the input helpers `click_cell` and `cursor_shroud_result`, and `place_building`, which `production::place` calls: the factory condition of claim 1, then either an upgrade (`upgrade_target`, `add_upgrade`, F5) or the cell test and `put_on_map`. `put_on_map_facing` is the one core for a place order, a deploy and a filling wall: walls underneath removed by the silent wall sale (claim 9), a wall type turned into its overlay (claim 10), otherwise the building put at the cell centre at the surface height in its construction state (`begin_construction`; `objects::place_with` passes `by_order` to the events), then the wall fill for a place order (claim 11). The construction behaviour (`deploy::process_construction`) and the selling behaviour (`sell::process_selling`) are dispatched from `process.rs` and run every tick with no draw. *Construction* is queued and starts at the ready mark: a deployed building has the mark when it enters play, a placed one only once (*n* − 1) × *d_b* ticks (1 for a zero-delay range) have passed since it entered play, so a placed building's build-up runs twice (S-0406 claim 17 and F7, `quirks.placed_buildup_twice`; `placement::construction_turn`, `Construction::entry`). Until then the building's first per-tick start is withheld (`placement::first_start_withheld`), and the tests that ask for its behaviour see the queued *construction* (`placement::seen_behaviour`). Construction records its first processing *C* (`Construction::first`) and hands over at *C* + (*n* − 1) × *d_b*, or *C* + 1 for a zero-delay range (claims 14 and 15, F6; `sell::buildup_range`, S-0108 claim 5, where a gate has *n* = `GateStages` + 1 and *n* is otherwise the frame count of the art's build-up sprite, which the converter imports as `buildup_frames`); a one-stage range with a positive delay never hands over (claim 18, `quirks.single_stage_buildup_never_completes`; the converter warns about such types): the opening effects, then *guard* queued with the ready mark and the target kept. A placed building faces 0 (claim 16), and so does a deployed one (S-0415 claim 6).
