The sale's build-down ends when (*n* − 1) × *d_b* ticks have passed, with *n* capped at 23 for a building that undeploys into a vehicle and has no guard spot (S-0408 claim 17's early report; `sell::builddown_ticks`); a positive-delay range of 1 stage or fewer never reports under `quirks.single_stage_buildup_never_completes` (the sale stays in build-down, no refund) and reports on its first wrap, *D* + *d_b*, with it off (S-0408 claim 17, which answers Q-0225). `econ::sell` has the refund (F1, on `production::cost_factors`) and main's adjusted cost (F2), sellable and repairable, the sell order (building, upgrade, vehicle on a building cell), the wall sale (`wall_sale`, silent or not), the upgrade sale of S-0408 claim 5 at the upgrade count's position (`BuildingSwitches::upgrades` holds the three positions; a turret upgrade resets the count to 0, any other lowers it by 1; an empty position is the Edge-cases departure), the repair switch and the building's repair step in its own turn (`sell::building_turn`, via `econ::events::turn`, on S-0314's `building_repair_step`). `seams::building_adjusted_cost` and `seams::building_refund` now answer. `econ::deploy` has the vehicle's *unload* by the ranks of S-0415 claim 1 (`process_unload`, ranks 1 and 3 handed back; rank 2, deploy fire, chooses 14 + U(0, 2) ticks its shot and the in-place rank are S-0517, section *Simulation (M6c: deployable units)*), the attempt, refusal, turn and completion (claims 2 to 6, state in `DeployState`, reset by every deploy order), `packs_up` (S-0213 claim 12) and the pack-up's end (`pack_up`, claims 10 to 12: the return cell accepts the new vehicle when its S-0209 entry outcome there is clear, with the packing building gone). Sounds are `Effect::Sound` events (deploy, pack-up, sell, cannot-deploy, unit sold) for the viewer. Seams: the theater-refresh build-up range of S-0108 claim 5 (no theater flag imported yet), the to-tile tileset test (no morphable flag in the catalogue), docked units, mind control, deploy-readiness states other than health, map-placed upgrades (still separate objects). A direct call of `place_building` with no done item of the type creates the object (engine callers and tests; orders always come through a done item). Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0406.md`, `S-0408.md`, `S-0415.md`.
- **Slave miners (S-0402 claims 14 and 19 to 28, F7; S-0312 claims 30 and 31; S-0408 claims 6 and 10a; S-0415 claims 10 and 11; S-0203 claim 12; S-0213 claims 5 and 8, T3 h; S-0209 WP2).** `econ::slaves` keeps a master's group (`ObjectEcon::group`: the slave places in initial creation order, each slave inside or out and ready or not with its delay's start tick; the group state (at rest, field seeking, travelling, retrying, working, checking the field, packing), last-rest tick and next update; the remembered destination of claim 28 is the building's guard spot, which an automatic pack-up sets to the old top-left cell) and each slave's own state (`ObjectEcon::slave`: master, phase, load per tiberium, remembered cell, recalled). `objects::create` makes the initial slaves inside and off the map (one creation draw each). A successful conversion moves the group (`slaves::detach` and `slaves::attach`, from `deploy::pack_up` and the deploy); the conversion does not change the group state. A pack-up first creates the return vehicle with its own hidden group (claim 19a, one creation draw each), then on success ends that temporary group as a removal (`slaves::end_removed`, S-0312 claim 31) before the old group is attached; on refusal the vehicle keeps it (`quirks.failed_packup_keeps_vehicle`), or with the quirk off it ends and the vehicle is removed. The group update (`slaves::group_turn`, every 10 ticks) runs for a building master in `econ::events::turn` after its power step and before its paid repair, and for a vehicle master in `tick::object_turn` before its movement (S-0203 claim 12). It first runs each slave place's effects in place order (claim 26): a replacement, a reload completion, or an outside slave's arrival, full or exhausted load, search and delivery. Then the master's decision: a vehicle's packing (recall, then field seeking), checking (to at rest), field seeking, travelling and deploy attempts (`deploy::attempt`); a building's busy-state table of claim 27 (`building_update`): at rest and working go to checking the field outside construction and selling, checking the field runs the short scan and the long scan with claim 25's (0, 0) for a failed search (`quirks.slave_relocation_failed_search_origin`), F7 and an automatic pack-up through `sell::start_pack_up` that sets the guard spot to the old top-left cell; field seeking runs the long scan and site search and offers the site (to travelling) or rests, and travelling or retrying rests, both recording the last-rest tick. Last, when the decision allows it, emergence at the unloading cell in reverse place order, facing 0 (S-0208 claim 3's centre row, one draw). A deployed master's scans centre on its reference-point cell and need only tiberium land. A slave's *harvest* (`slaves::process_slave`, from `process.rs`) only loads one bale every *h* ticks; a non-resource cell, a full load and a capacity of 0 (or a negative one with `quirks.slave_negative_capacity_stalls` off) queue *guard* with a 1-tick delay. The slave miner vehicle's *harvest* (`slaves::process_harvest`) searches for a site near its destination at every processing, or queues *guard* and rests without one, with the standard delay. The first processing of selling or packing runs claim 28's directed site rule (`slaves::first_sale_processing`, from `sell::process_selling`). `death::kill` hands the slaves of a destroyed master to S-0312 claim 30 (`slaves::master_killed`); a true sale takes S-0408 claim 10a's path (`slaves::master_sold`, from `sell::finish`); a refused pack-up (`slaves::end_removed`) and any other removal (`slaves::removing`) take S-0312 claim 31's; a lost slave starts its place's regeneration (`slaves::lost`). Stop and behaviour orders to a vehicle master recall its slaves (`slaves::recall`). The wake-up rule is `harvest::wake_rule` with its inputs from the world (`harvest::slave_wake_rule_holds`); only the neutral and special houses and a test-only computer-controlled player house take its owner alternative, so a person's or an AI house's idle miner far from a field never wakes; claim 13's guard rows follow a processing that did not wake, for a type with the harvester flag only (S-0402 r80, Q-1990, [intake41-S-0402.md](../m8-claims/intake41-S-0402.md)). Our AI sends a resting miner with its slaves home to harvest on the nearest field cell it has explored (`scripted.rs`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0402_slaves.rs`.
- **Skirmish setup, country bonuses, victory and defeat (S-0412, S-0413, S-0414).** A skirmish is started in three steps: `econ::setup::skirmish(rules, players, settings, unit_count, seed)` builds the `GameSetup` (the players in house order, each a `Player` with name, country, start, AI flag and lobby `HouseValues` (team, handicap, colour, observer), then the neutral and special houses; `Settings` holds credits, short game, superweapons, crates, build off ally, redeploy and the all-base-units mode, each `None` for the rules' default); `setup::resolve_lobby` (optional) settles random countries and colours on its own lobby stream, so the game's stream is untouched; then `load::new_game` (or `Replay::new`) refuses a setup S-0412 forbids (`LoadError::Setup`), creates the houses with their credits, difficulty index and row (`house_values`) and, for every non-sandbox game, runs `setup::start_game` after the starting units: crate count, extra credits, start alliances, wall-owner rewrite, colour fields and secret labs. The accessors `credits_setting`, `short_game`, `superweapons_allowed`, `build_off_ally`, `redeploy`, `observer`, `person_played`, `wall_owner` and `colour_field` answer other topics. Country factors go through their owning specs' seams (`house_combat` with the row, `CountryRules.speed_aircraft` through `setup::aircraft_speed`). `econ::victory` keeps each house's on-map counts (hooks in `econ::events`), runs a pending surrender's destruction and the defeat test in the house update after power and the ordinary radar recheck and before the tech-tree recheck (S-0414 claim 16; tick 1 on, contenders only), defeats a house (every object of the four kinds hit in creation order, off the map and in production included, with the cancellation skip of `quirks.defeat_cancellation_skip`; radar on, map revealed), runs the end test, the results and the end delay, commits the pending result at the start of the houses' part of the last tick (`victory::commit`), and applies the surrender and alliance orders. The outcome is `victory::outcome(&World) -> Option<Outcome>` (`Some` once the game is over, with each player house's `Verdict`); `victory::verdict`, `victory::defeated` and `victory::ended` answer per house and game. Seams: crate placement, superweapon granting, the all-base-units mode (refused), observer positions, aircraft flight, mind control, erasure, passengers and the nominal-survivor condition. Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0412.md`, `S-0413.md`, `S-0414.md`.
- **Leftovers and the defeat sweep (S-0203 r53 claim 13, Q-2636, Q-2691 to Q-2693; M8 A3 intakes 105 and 107, [intake105.md](../m8-claims/intake105.md), [intake107.md](../m8-claims/intake107.md)).** `objects::remove` keeps every infantry unit, vehicle, aircraft and building it takes out of the game in `World::leftovers` (`glasnost_sim::leftover`; saved and hashed, save format 109, AI game format 74 since intake 107), off the map with its owner and health. A passenger killed with its transport keeps the health it had (`transport::died_with_transport`, claim 13 (b)). `tick::step` empties the store first, so a leftover lasts to the end of its tick (claim 13 (a)), except a kept one (below). The arena's *Removal before the groups* (`objects::take_out_quietly`) keeps none. Nothing in the simulation finds a leftover but the sweep of S-0414 claim 5 (`econ::victory::destroy_all`), which walks the house's objects and the leftovers together in creation order, so it also reaches the leftovers an earlier sweep of the same house update or a death weapon earlier in the same sweep made (claim 13 (f)); every leftover of the four kinds holds a place for claim 15's cancellation skip in both switch positions (claim 13 (h)). Under `quirks.defeat_sweep_leftovers` (default on, the original) every leftover vehicle of the house, whatever removed it, takes the sweep's hit (`leftover::sweep_hit`, `Effect::LeftoverHit` with the demolition warhead, no attacker and the health before and after). With no health it takes no health and registers nothing; with health left it is a killing hit that registers the destruction once more with no attacker (`damage::credit`: a loss, no kill), with S-0312 claim 29's raises for a killing hit with no attacker for a tag the leftover still carries (S-0011 r29 claim 2, Q-2716 answered at intake 113: `objects::remove` detaches no tag under `quirks.removed_carrier_keeps_tag`; `death::leftover_destruction_raises`, made after the registration and before the repeated death effects, with the leftover out of the world). Then `death::repeat` gives its S-0312 death effects once more with their draws, in claim 1's order (debris, the death weapon of an exploding vehicle, the claim 9 animations, no crew under the no-escape marker, a crate it carries). `death::repeat_plan` reads them with the leftover put back among the objects for the read alone (`leftover::read_restored`). A leftover with health left that meets S-0312 claim 10's sinking conditions takes claim 10's branch instead of the animations: 1 health point, sinking, no crate; of a type that cannot crash it is kept (`Leftover::kept`) to the end of the game with its place, and one that can crash is removed again with its crate and ends with its tick (claim 13 (g)). A leftover with no health starts no sinking. One of a type that can crash above the terrain starts a crash after its other effects (S-0312 claim 16's three tumbling draws) and no fall, so it leaves no crate and ends with its tick. Claim 11's wake and splash of a bomb-marked vehicle are not built (a seam). Off: no leftover takes a hit, none is kept. Tests: `claims_s0203_r50.rs`, `claims_s0203_r53.rs`, `claims_s0414.rs`.
- **Viewer live sandbox (M5a).** The *game on load* option starts either the sandbox game (every owner's objects, as in M3) or a skirmish (`live::skirmish_setup`: one human house and one computer house of the rules' first open countries, no starting units beyond the base units, the fixed sandbox seed), in which `glasnost_ai`'s script plays the computer house through orders every `THINK_PERIOD` ticks. A skirmish load also reads the art of every building, vehicle and infantry type (`build::game_files`), since a game creates types the map does not hold; the game's own buildings are drawn after the cell pass from `Painter::buildings` (`build::LiveBuilding`, damage by S-0108 claim 3). The sidebar is a placeholder layout of our own (`app::mapview::sidebar`, the original-looking one is M5b): credits through S-0411's counter, power output and drain, the radar map (`app::mapview::radar`: S-0410's grid, image, arrivals and `compose` for the controlled house in 5/6/5 bits, black with a "no radar" note while the house has no radar (claim 1), a click jumps the view to the cell of F3; point colours come from the cells' tile radar colours only (claim 9's tile case: the first tile radar colour of the tile cell the shown image variant holds, S-0010 claim 20, also used by the static minimap), and each house's objects take its house display RGB, S-0103 claim 15), tabs by production line kind, cameos with their state (darkened, build stage of 55, ready, on hold, queue count) where a left click and a right click go through `econ::sidebar::click`, and placement: a ready building's cameo enters placement mode, the cursor names the foundation's top-left cell by S-0406 F2 (`placement::click_cell`), the foundation is previewed green or red from `place_legal` and the cell test (or the upgrade target), a left click issues the place order and a right click leaves the mode. The shroud is S-0110's (`app::mapview::cover`, `render::shroud`): in every drawn frame each legal cell whose 60 × 30 canvas meets the view gets its shroud piece from the controlled house's explored states (`econ::shroud::explored`, which answers S-0409 claim 14's shared state for non-legal neighbours), read afresh each frame, so a cell, or the shared state of non-legal neighbours, that a later gap change covers again is shrouded again from the next frame (S-0110 claim 1 and the re-covered-cells and non-legal-neighbour edge cases): 15 when it is not explored, else claim 2's piece for its reduced set of unexplored neighbours (table T1). The pieces are image frames of `SHROUD.SHP` (a missing or unreadable sprite draws no cover, and the load lists a problem; so does a missing `FOG.SHP`), applied by cell in increasing *x* + *y* on the height-0 grid (claim 7, `quirks.shroud_on_flat_grid`; off, each canvas sits at its cell's terrain height) to the view's light-alpha values (127 at the start of each drawn frame, F1: a shroud pixel sets the value unless its index is 254). The pieces are not painted: every drawing shaded through S-0104 claim 15 (`compose::Paint::Lit`, a shade table and ρ; tiles, overlays, smudges, terrain objects, buildings, infantry and vehicles) picks its row from its pixel's value, on the CPU and the GPU (a per-frame `R8Uint` light-alpha texture). Status extras and our placeholders keep their fixed rows and are not darkened (S-0111 claim 2 for health segments and pips). Fog of war is always off in skirmish, so no fog piece is drawn; the fog rules of claims 3, 4 and 10 are in `render::shroud` with tests only. No light-glow images are drawn yet (claim 6). The point test of claim 8 is the simulation's `econ::shroud::point_shrouded`, which keeps the switch's on state; its candidate cells (`point_candidates`) take *u* = trunc(*Z* / 104) for heights below 0 too, with the odd-negative pair of `quirks.shroud_negative_odd_pair` (a simulation switch, default on), and the radar's gap test shares them. The sandbox game and the *shroud* checkbox off draw no cover. `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --lib snapshot_skirmish_view -- --ignored` writes `local-lane/skirmish-view/start.png` and `after.png` (after `GLASNOST_SNAPSHOT_TICKS` ticks, default 600).
- **Ore and gems (S-0401, r17 since M8 intake 42).** `econ::resource`; ledgers [m5-claims/S-0401.md](../m5-claims/S-0401.md) and [m8-claims/intake42-S-0401.md](../m8-claims/intake42-S-0401.md). A germinable cell holding a terrain object takes a new cell's draws and gets nothing (claim 11, M-0013); `quirks.spread_step_lost_on_terrain_object` off makes it not germinable.

- **Building body range (S-0108 claim 6, S-0215 claims 1 and 11).** `econ::body` follows only the *idle* body range of a building (`AnimIdle` and `Normalized`, imported as `anim_idle` and `normalized`): a building placed at game set-up enters it at once (`set_up`, which also queues *guard* with no current behaviour), a construction handover enters it deferred, and entering construction or a sale leaves it. On each building turn, before the first per-tick start, the stage of a building without a turret advances and wraps, and the ready mark is set when it lands on the last stage, or on every turn of a zero-delay range (`quirks.building_wrap_normalisation`). The other body states (*active*, *aux-1*, *aux-2*, *full*) and what enters them are a seam: the range runs on as *idle* through them.

Q-0500 is answered by S-0402 revision 18, claim 27a: a field-seeking building's successful search offers the shifted site cell (`econ::slaves::offer_site`). A building that is not currently *selling* and is a factory of infantry or vehicles, a cloning building, a repair depot or a construction yard takes it as its guard spot (`takes_site_offer`), the one remembered destination a later pack-up uses; any other building keeps its earlier guard spot or its absence, and no building is given a movement destination. *Move* is requested without an immediate start in every case (current *selling* refuses it under S-0215 claim 7), and a building's *move* processing does nothing for 450 ticks (`process`, S-0215 claim 4 and T1), so the group's return to rest leaves it current. Revision 18's other changes need no code change and are now tested: the exact non-wrapping refinery distance with `quirks.refinery_distance_wraps` off keeps the primary and tie rules, a dump rate of 0 or below pays one tiberium per processing, and refinery selection comes no earlier than the processing after the change to returning.

Provisional rules of M5a, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `combat::death::kill`, `econ::production::release`, `econ::production::place` | a production item killed by a destruction hit (S-0414 claim 15) is only marked killed (no other death effect, no draws) and stays on its line with health 0 until a cancellation deletes it; a release order for it fails and cancels it with the refund, a place order does nothing | Q-0193 |

S-0215 revision 42 answers Q-0241 (claim 1): set-up placement runs the building's grand opening after *guard* is queued, and the opening sets the ready mark (`econ::body::set_up`), so *guard* is current from the building's first per-tick start, armed or not, turreted or not, with or without a map-installed upgrade. The opening's free unit stays suppressed at set-up (S-0402 claim 18). Its row is retired; map-placed defences now guard and fire from their first turn.

S-0215 revision 43, S-0308 revision 11 and S-0301 revision 15: upgrade weapons and the upgrade weapon gate. `combat::weapon::slot_weapon` now takes slot *i* of a building from its first installed upgrade (S-0406's upgrade positions) whose type has an ordinary weapon there, never an upgrade's elite slot, and only then the building's own slot (S-0308 claim 12). A non-SAM building with an installed upgrade fires on *can fire* only when its first installed upgrade passes the gate (`combat::behave::upgrade_gate`: a primary, and both slots the same weapon, a primary burst above 1, or a secondary burst above 1). A passing gate makes the primary attempt and then the secondary attempt with no firing check of its own (S-0301 claim 9's exception). A failed gate keeps the target and *attack*, sets the ready mark and chooses 1 tick, with no attempt (`quirks.upgrade_attack_weapon_gate`, on; off, the ordinary selected-weapon attempt). Upgrades the map places are still separate objects, not installed upgrades (S-0009 claim 40 seam), so neither rule applies to them yet. Tests: `s0308_claim_12_upgrades_supply_weapons` and `s0215_claim_32_*upgrade*`, `s0215_claim_32_*gate*` in `claims_s0301.rs`.

## Simulation (M6a: missiles)

S-0506 (missiles and the spawn controller) is built in `glasnost_sim::spawn` (the controller and its slots), `glasnost_sim::rocket` (rocket flight) and `glasnost_sim::combat::flying` (the flying-object registry of S-0303 claim 6 and F7). Its rules are `SimRules::missiles` (`spawn::MissileRules`: each spawner type's slots, count, regeneration and reload, and the three rocket parameter sets A, B and C from the general section), imported by `glasnost_convert` (spawn keys, rocket sets, the aircraft trailer). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0506.md](../m6-claims/S-0506.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0506.rs`.

- **Spawn controller (claims 1 to 13).** `objects::create` calls `spawn::created`, which makes the full count of docked slot objects off the map and sets the first pass at creation + 20. `spawn::turn`, from the tick pipeline after the behaviour step, runs a pass every 10 ticks: the slot steps in index order, then the controller step (`spawn::pass`). A spawner weapon's shot (`combat::fire`) launches nothing and calls `spawn::shot`, which sets the pending target. The pass launches docked slots by claim 6's spacing (9 or 20 ticks), places them at the fire point raised by 10 with F2's facing, and releases them onto the rail (`OnRail`, with the rail time of claim 8) once every slot is preparing or empty; the controller then resets and cools down. Death, removal and owner change go through `spawn::killed`, `spawn::removing` and `spawn::owner_changed` (claim 13).
- **Rocket flight (claims 14 to 21, F1 to F9).** A rocket-type object runs `rocket::tick` in place of a locomotor. Its phases are fixing the aim, pause, tilt or raise, ascent, cruise (plain or lazy) and descent. It moves by F1 only into legal cells and stalls at the map edge under the quirk. It detonates at its nose point F9 through `combat::area::detonate`, with the set's damage and warhead (elite ones for an elite launch), and then leaves play. Trailers appear every 3 ticks on the shared timer. A rocket destroyed above the terrain keeps flying (`rocket::keeps_flying_when_destroyed`, called from `combat::death`).
- **Hooks into other specs.**
  - S-0301 claim 9: rank 2 *busy* while a vehicle launches, rank 4 *rearming* via `spawn::any_alive`; `spawn::firing_answer` from `combat::behave`.
  - S-0309 claim 4: `spawn::impossible` keeps the target (`combat::acquire`).
  - S-0303: flying candidates and the half distance in `combat::area`; S-0312: interception through `combat::death`.
  - S-0311 claim 8: an untrainable missile-spawn object's experience goes to its launcher (`combat::veterancy`, `spawn::launcher_of`).
  - S-0214: `targeting::movable` is false while 0 < launching count < *N* (`spawn::partly_launched`).
  - S-0504 claim 3 / S-0207 claim 6: `drive::begin_track` waits while `spawn::track_held`; `spawn::launching_count(w, spawner) -> i32` is public.
  - In the air: `combat::weapon::in_air` answers by flight phase for the set-A and set-B types and by the 208-lepton test for a distinct set-C type (claim 22).
- **Revision 3 (answers Q-0610 to Q-0616).** The raw arctangent of F6 and F7 is S-0204 F7's exact table `glasnost_num::tables::RAW_ATAN`, generated from the tangent recurrence (`tablegen::raw_atan_table`). The phase timer starts in the aim-fixing tick with zero elapsed ticks and runs that tick's update. Raise adds its rate only before expiry, and the update that finds cruise altitude still accelerates (claim 24). A rocket missile is in no cell list (claim 23). A destroyed missile keeps its phase timers (claim 20). A crossing flying object follows the objects already in its new sector (S-0303 claim 11). Pending targets follow claim 25 under `quirks.spawner_pending_target_survives_idle_clear`. No pass drops pending for a targetless spawner, and the pending target's own loss clears it.
- **Revision 6 (claim 25 with S-0309 rev 14 claims 4, 23 and 24 and S-0215 rev 29 claim 12).** Every place that clears an object's own target goes through `spawn::clear_own_target` or `spawn::set_own_target` (an actual change of a nonempty own target to empty requests the pending clear; a nonempty replacement does not). Orders use `spawn::explicit_clear_own_target`, which also records a clear of an already empty target, so that with the switch off it empties pending. A nonempty assignment that S-0309 claim 24 redirects to none uses `spawn::redirected_to_none`, which requests the clear even when the old own target was empty. Dropping an unusable opportunity target requests it even when the same search picks a new target. The attack-move searches that run as for an object with no target (S-0309 claim 23) discard and restore the own target without any request, under either setting. A request is recorded on the controller (`SpawnController::clear_request`) and settled by `spawn::settle_clear` at the start of the spawner's turn, of every pass, of `spawn::shot` and of `spawn::removing`, before anything reads the pending or controller target, so the outcome equals an immediate clear; a clear and restore before a pass still clears. This replaces the earlier per-turn inference (`watch_own_target`). The save format version is 15.
- **Revision 5 (claim 26, Q-0645 answered): the shared kamikaze re-order cycle.** `spawn::kamikaze_orders` runs once per tick from `tick::step`, after resource growth and the planted-bomb seam (row 2) and before the diving-aircraft seam (row 10) and the turn pass (S-0203 claim 1 row 17). The first cycle is due at tick 1 (`SpawnedObjects::default`), and each cycle schedules the next at its own tick + 30, even with no kamikaze. A normal salvo release, or a reset that actually releases a preparing flagged object, replaces the deadline with the release tick + 2; slot clearing enrolls in descending slot index and keeps the deadline. `SpawnedObjects::kamikaze` is a `Vec` in enrollment order (removal keeps the others' order). At each due cycle every enrolled object, rocket missiles included, gets ammunition 1, its release cell as target and *attack* queued without an immediate start, with no draws. A rocket's flight ignores them (its aim is fixed by its first move order and it has no behaviour processing).
- **Claim 24 and S-0614 claim 18 (local turn order).** `tick::object_turn` already runs behaviour processing (with aircraft attack-run shots) before the spawner's launch pass and the parasite event, and the separate infantry and vehicle firing after both; `claim_24_vehicle_shot_waits_for_next_pass` tests the vehicle case. No test drives an aircraft spawner yet.
- **Revision 19 (claim 27, answering Q-2159 questions 2 and 3; M8 A3 intake 59).** Claim 27 restates what other claims already give for close targets, and nothing in the simulation changed. The spawner weapon's minimum range is part of the firing check's range test and of the idle controller's adoption test (`combat::range::in_range`, S-0307 claim 9; `spawn` claim 5). So a spawner does not fire inside it and takes the approach step as an ordinary weapon would, and a controller whose adopted target is inside it resets. A launching controller never repeats the test. A rocket's hits name the missile as attacker, and the allies row reads the missile's owner (`combat::damage`, S-0302 claim 8), so with a warhead that does not affect allies the spawner and its housemates take no hit. Being berserk changes neither the crush test nor the crush response. Tests: `claims_s0506_close.rs` (scenario R23 parts a to h). Q-2159 question 1 (how the stock launchers leave play) stays open as S-0506's Open question, for scenario R24. Ledger: [m8-claims/intake59-S-0311-S-0506.md](../m8-claims/intake59-S-0311-S-0506.md).
- **Revision 37 (claims 7, 11, 12, 14 and 24; Q-2748 in part and Q-2749 answered; M8 A3 intake 113, [intake113.md](../m8-claims/intake113.md)).**
  - *A controller target in the air at the launch.* `spawn::launch` gives a missile no destination when the controller target's in-the-air answer is true (claim 7). The missile waits at its placement point with no phase, speed 0 and pitch 0, and `rocket::tick` makes no update for it; the r20 first-update fixing (intake 110's Q-2748 interim) is gone. `rocket::give_destination` is the one way a destination reaches a rocket after its launch: a destination in the air is ignored, the first one not in the air fixes the aim from its heading point then, and claim 24's *t*₀ is that tick. Which destination comes first is still Q-2748's questions 2 and 3; our interim (`design: Q-2748 interim`) gives the release cell at the shared re-order (`spawn::kamikaze_orders`), one of the candidates the Open question names.
  - *The loss of the controller target.* `spawn::target_lost` (settle the waiting clears, then the loss) is called at the destroying hit (`spawn::killed`, from `death::kill`, so before a death sequence, a sinking or a crash), at the start of cloaking (`cloak::machine::start`, whatever the spawner's house and sensors), at going inside (`spawn::target_leaves_play`) and at removal (`spawn::removing`). A lost pending target is cleared with no reset; a lost controller target is cleared before the reset, so `release_object` gets no target and gives the F8 cell (claim 12, Q-2749). Claim 8's release, the pulse and the other resets still release with the target. A controller target lost while a pending target waits is cleared too, with no reset at the loss (S-0506 r40 claim 11, Q-2811 answered); the controller's next pass decides. In `controller_step` a launching controller with no controller target resets (releasing its preparing missiles with no target, so to the F8 cell, and clearing the pending target), unless a non-missile slot's `slot_step` adopted the pending target earlier in that pass. A cooling-down controller keeps the pending target for an attacking or returning slot or its first idle pass. `quirks.launching_spawner_resets_after_target_loss` (default on) off: the launching controller adopts the pending target at that check, with no range test, and goes on launching. Since r40 a non-missile slot's preparing rows move its object whether its type is flagged or not (re-sent to the step direction 4 neighbour, or returning with no controller target), so after a double loss a flagged non-rocket object has turned returning before the reset and is not released. Going underground is a seam: no underground locomotor is built.
- **Seams for other lanes.**
  - S-0501 claims 35 and 36: `spawn::aircraft_launched`, `spawn::aircraft_attack` and `spawn::aircraft_return` now route to `air::carrier_move`, `air::carrier_attack` and `air::carrier_return`, so slot aircraft fly, attack and dock end to end (M6a: aircraft, below).
  - `spawn::owner_changed` has no caller until owner change exists.
  - `spawn::magnet_held` answers false (no magnetic beam).
  - Other seams: EMP and locomotor-warhead resets, building docking points and tunnel heading points, and the set-C take-off animation (no import key names it).
- **Naval stand-in (retired).** `set_b_launcher_attacks_in_play` in `claims_s0506.rs` still uses a two-slot launcher on the driving locomotor. Since S-0504 the ship locomotor moves, and `ship_borne_set_b_launcher_attacks` in `claims_s0504.rs` runs set B from a launcher on the ship locomotor on water, so the stand-in no longer replaces a vessel.

## Simulation (M6a: parasites)

- **Parasites (S-0614).** `glasnost_sim::parasite` holds a unit's parasite record (`ParasiteState` in `Object::parasite`). Only a latching weapon creates one, so saves without parasites are unchanged. `combat::fire::shot` calls `parasite::fired`: the firer leaves the map inside the projectile and the latch block is set. `combat::projectile` calls `parasite::carrier_ended` when the projectile ends, which either latches or fails to latch (claims 3 and 4). A parasite-flag detonation makes no area hit but keeps its animation and debris (S-0303 claim 2, S-0306 claim 21). An attached parasite acts late in its host's turn (`parasite::host_turn`, called from `tick::object_turn` after self-healing, behaviour processing and movement, before firing; claim 18):
  - drone deliveries and the hold (claims 6 to 9, F1);
  - the grappling cycle of table G, with strikes, splashes, wakes and the cull (claims 10 and 11, F3, F5, F6). A culled host starts sinking through `naval::start_sinking`.
- **Leaving.** A parasite leaves its host through these hooks:
  - `parasite::host_hit`, from `combat::damage::apply` (sonic hits, healing and suppression, S-0315 claims 6 and 7);
  - `parasite::repair_step`, from `combat::heal::repair_step`;
  - `parasite::host_jumps`, from `teleport`;
  - `parasite::leaving`, from `objects::take_inside` and `objects::remove` (host loss, claim 15).
- **Held units.** A held unit:
  - does not drive or fly (`tick::object_turn`);
  - is not movable (`targeting`);
  - gets rank 4 *impossible* when it is a mobile spawner, and rank 9 *illegal* when it is an organic firer (`combat::check`);
  - cannot deploy (`econ::deploy::deploy_ready`);
  - drops its destination when it is a teleporter.

  A host never retaliates against its own parasite (`combat::acquire`). Returning placement (claims 4, 13, 15, 20 and 23) uses S-0208 claim 3's position choice and S-0209's entry outcomes. A projectile removed without detonating leaves its parasite off the map (claim 21). On host loss a vehicle parasite whose fallback choice fails is put at world position (128, 128, 0) (claim 23). The seven quirk switches from the spec's Edge cases are in `quirks`.
- **Orders a human gives.** Select a drone and click an enemy vehicle. The attack order fires the latching weapon (`targeting::click`, then `orders::issue`).
- **Claim ledger.** `docs/m6-claims/S-0614.md`.

Provisional rules of M6a for missiles and parasites: none at present (the naval ones are listed in the naval units section). Q-0610 to Q-0616 are answered by S-0506 revision 3, S-0204 revision 8 and S-0303 revision 11; Q-0620 to Q-0623 by S-0614 revisions 4 and 5.

Known limits of M6a:

- A culled host is set sinking (`naval::start_sinking`) and goes down and leaves play by S-0504 claim 4 (`naval::sinking_turn`). The tick of the cull within the host's turn is still open (S-0504 claim 4).
- Some hooks have no callers yet because their topics are not built: `parasite::host_unloading`, `parasite::host_consumed`, `parasite::host_curtain_started` (curtain protection, S-0614 rev 6: 50 ticks of suppression just before release), `parasite::superweapon_teleport` and `parasite::locomotor_replaced`.
- A teleporting superweapon's departure (S-0614 rev 6 claim 12): a drone parasite is not released at launch and its fate at departure is unresolved (S-0614 Open questions, P7). `parasite::host_jumps` is only the host's own teleport jump; no departure hook exists, so the superweapon topic must not call it for that path until the spec settles it.
- Not modelled: spark particles, the tank bunker, uncloaking and claim 19's re-selection (presentation). F2's rocking is reported (`Effect::DroneRock`) and drawn by the view (M8 A4 rocking).

## Simulation (M6a: naval units)

S-0504 (naval units) is built in `glasnost_sim::naval`, with small hooks in `drive`, `crush`, `objects`, `tick` and `combat`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0504.md](../m6-claims/S-0504.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0504.rs` and `crates/convert/src/sim_naval_tests.rs`.

- **Ship locomotor (claims 1 and 3).** A vehicle with the ship locomotor carries `DriveState` (`objects::create`) and moves by `drive::tick`, called from `naval::tick` in the tick pipeline. Its heading point, scatter centre, arrival stop (S-0207 claim 19 a), launching hold (`spawn::track_held` in `drive::begin_track`) and destination clearing after a failed search (S-0212 claim 23) are the driving ones. Its crush mode is S-0210 claim 9's ship case (`drive::crush_mode`: a crushable overlay, or a wall for crusher-all, in the first or second cell, never a vehicle), and its crush slowdown is entered by a crusher on a crush-mode track entering a cell with the overlay of registration index 0 (`crush::entered_cell`, `quirks.crush_slowdown_first_overlay`).
- **Wakes (claim 2; S-0207 claim 23).** `drive::wake_check(w, id, period)` is shared: *moving now* is a turning body, or an active track with `drive::progress` above 0 at the check, after the tick's movement. A ship uses period 8 and makes no wake when its type is underwater (`naval::ship_wake`). A drive vehicle uses period 10 (`drive::driving_wake`). This answers Q-0600: the old `DriveState::step` test is gone from the wake, and `DriveState::step` stays for S-0402 claim 5a.
- **Sinking (claims 4 and 5; S-0312 claim 10).** `combat::death::kill` sinks a killed vehicle when `naval::sinks_at_kill` holds (naval, not underwater, not organic, weight at least `combat.general.sinking_weight`, on a water cell). It then skips the explosion and destruction list picks and `leave_play`, keeps 1 health point and calls `naval::start_sinking`. Debris, the death weapon and the crew still run. `naval::sinking_turn` runs at the start of the vessel's own turn (`tick::object_turn`; for a vessel destroyed while sinking it is the whole turn, S-0312 claim 32). It lowers z by 5. Below −400 leptons above the terrain it leaves play with no draws. Otherwise, on ticks divisible by 4, it takes two ranged draws and makes a wake at (x + r₂, y + r₁, terrain height). Its locomotor does not run while it sinks. A sinking firer gets *illegal* at rank 4 and a sinking target *illegal* at rank 9 (`combat::check`), and `combat::acquire::redirect` gives no target for it. The state is `Object::naval` (`naval::NavalState`).
- **Underwater vessels (claim 6).** `naval::submerged` needs an underwater type that can cloak (`cloakable`, imported) and a cloak state. Cloaking (S-0516) is not built, so no vessel is submerged yet and the submerge and surface test waits for S-0516.
- **Docking (claims 7, 8, 12 and 14).** `naval::naval_match_refuses`, `naval::starting_distance`, `naval::depot_distance` (F3, exact integer root) and `naval::waiting_gate`, with `naval::SETTLING_DELAY`, are called by the depot cycle of M6b (below): a damaged ship clicked onto its repairing yard docks at the yard's fixed point and is repaired in play (`claim_14_ship_repaired_at_yard`).
- **Revision 5 (answers Q-0640, Q-0642, Q-0643, Q-0644).** A vehicle type without a weight weighs exactly 1.0 in the sinking test (claim 11, `naval::sinks_at_kill`). Sinking is no immunity: damage reaches a sinking vessel by the ordinary rules and it keeps self-healing (claim 10, S-0314 claim 19; `combat::damage::apply`, `tick::object_turn`); a further killing hit is answered by revision 6. A body turn under way continues while it sinks (claim 10; the locomotor is suppressed, the body turn is not). The naval match's only exception is the plain *amphibious* zone (claim 7, as built). Ships share the driving locomotor's path refresh, retry delay and first-step blocker rules (claim 9, as built through `drive`). The chase cut of S-0212 claim 20 is not built for any locomotor yet. `naval::yard_docking_point` gives a naval vehicle-factory yard's docking point (claim 12); the depot cycle uses the same point (`depot::docking_point`).
- **Revision 6 (answers Q-0646).** A further killing hit on a sinking vessel takes the ordinary kill path: credit and death effects again, and S-0312 claim 10 leaves an eligible vessel at 1 health, still sinking, its descent going on from its current height (claim 13; `combat::damage`, `naval::start_sinking`). A type that fails the sinking condition follows S-0312 claim 32 (next bullet). Claim 14 (yard interruptions and queued entry) rests on S-0314 claims 20 to 23's depot cycle, built in M6b.
- **S-0312 revision 22 (claims 15 and 32, with S-0504 claim 13).** A killing hit on a vessel already sinking (after a killing hit or a cull) that fails claim 10's conditions, for example a culled host that is underwater or below the sinking weight, now ends at 0 health with claim 9's explosion and destruction animations and their two list picks, and the vessel stays in play, still sinking, when its type cannot crash (`combat::death::kill`). A dead sinking vessel takes only its descent in its own turn (`tick::object_turn` calls `naval::sinking_turn` and nothing else: no healing, no behaviour processing), so it leaves play below −400 leptons at the end of the same descent. At 0 health it is out of ordinary damage (S-0302 claim 8), so no later hit kills it again. A crashable type that was sinking cannot start a crash below the surface and is removed at the end of the hit's effects, in either row of claim 32. Destruction is registered again and the raises, debris, death weapon and crew repeat through the ordinary kill path; passengers, the attached bomb, the bomb mark and death image frames are not built.
- **Revision 7 (editorial).** Other sinking triggers belong to their movement specs (the jumpjet drop of S-0502 claim 19, not built), and claim 9's chase cut and last-step refresh are S-0212 claim 20's, which includes ships. No code change.
- **S-0312 revisions 23 and 24 (answer Q-0652).**
  - A crashable vessel that meets claim 10's conditions at its first killing hit cannot start a crash. It gets 1 health and is removed at the end of the hit's effects (`combat::death::kill`).
  - It takes neither of claim 9's list picks and no crash-start draws. Its debris, crew and death weapon still apply.
  - It makes no descent and no sinking wakes (S-0504 claim 4).
  - Revision 23's bomb and crate wording needs no code: bombs and crates are not built.
  - An absent `Crashable` reads as no (`combat::data`). The import note that a later absence keeps the value in force is left to the ordinary rules resolution and is not tested here.
- **Revisions 8 to 10.**
  - Claim 9's ship refresh distance is the table-root distance under `quirks.table_root_distance`, which `drive` already uses.
  - F3's double round-toward-zero root equals the exact integer root that `naval::depot_distance` already takes.
  - Claim 4 removes a crashable vessel at its first killing hit (the S-0312 change above).
  - Claim 13's death-image-frames qualifier is not modelled, because death images are not built.
  - The only code change is the S-0312 one.
- **Import.** `Cloakable` (`combat.cloakable`) is new. `[General] Wake` absent gives an importer warning. `Naval`, `Underwater`, `Organic`, `Weight`, `ShipSinkingWeight`, `UnitRepair`, building `Naval`, `MovementZone` and `Locomotor` were already imported.
- **What play needs.** A naval yard is a building with `Naval=yes` and a vehicle factory. A player places it on flat cells whose tiles are in the tile catalogue's water role range (S-0406 claim 3 d, `econ::placement`), and it needs an empty water cell at its exit (`econ::release::naval_row`); the tests spawn the yard directly and skip placement. A naval unit is a vehicle with `Naval=yes` and the ship locomotor; the tests give it a float locomotion class and the water movement zone. A map needs water cells (land type water) next to the yard, and land rows whose multipliers let the ship's class enter water but not land. Orders are the ordinary ones: build the type on the naval line (`Order::Build`), then move and attack by `targeting::click` and `orders::issue`.

Provisional rules of M6a naval units: none at present. The Q-0641 row (depot cycle not built) is retired: M6b builds it (below).

## Simulation (M6b: depot cycle)

The depot cycle of S-0314 claims 6 and 16 to 24, S-0504 claims 7, 8, 12 and 14 and S-0215 claim 29 is built in `glasnost_sim::depot`. A repair depot (a building with `UnitRepair=yes`) and a naval yard with the repair flag accept an allied vehicle, which drives to the docking point, docks, and is repaired in three phases until the depot sends it away. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/depot-cycle.md](../m6-claims/depot-cycle.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0314_depot.rs` and `claim_14_*` in `claims_s0504.rs`.

- **State.** `Object::depot` (`depot::DepotState`): a vehicle's `link` (depot, place, tethered; valid only while its contact is that depot), `queued` (claim 23's queued entry), `entering` (the depot claim 23's order makes the vehicle remember, step 1j; the step 1h `walked` mark is gone) and `settled` (S-0314 Terms, claims 21 and 22; `loco_off` before intake 6 of M8 A3; since intake 30 a `depot::Settled` of no, at a depot or away, save format 80); a depot's `serviced` vehicle and `due`, the tick its next working step falls due (S-0314 claim 6). The phase is the depot's *repair* progress count. This state came with save format 18.
- **Permission and entry (claims 16 and 17; S-0214 table V value *n*).** `depot::accepts` is claim 16's permission. `targeting` gives *enter* over an accepting allied depot (row n3) and *no-enter* for a vehicle of zone *fly* (row n1); a held one-place depot keeps *select* (row n4). The enter order takes contact at once in the first free place (`depot::order_entered`). The vehicle's *enter* processing (`depot::process_enter`, from `process`) repeats the request; only a depot with the pad flag tethers it there (`depot::pad_exchange`: the foundation centre's cell, and no moving destination or place 0 more than 128 leptons from the centre). Since step 1l (S-0314 revision 29) an ordinary depot's repeated request supplies no completed-entry confirmation, pad flag and tether or not: the tethered vehicle is no longer turned to 16384 and queues nothing from its *enter* processing. Reaching the docking-point cell during movement (`depot::movement_arrival`, from the driving and hovering locomotors' arrivals) queues *repair* and *sleep* without a tether, so an ordinary depot repairs its vehicle untethered. An unarmed depot's guard processing makes the early start of claim 17 (`depot::early_start`, from `combat::behave`). Since intake 55b (S-0314 revision 107, claims 16 and 17) the repeated request (`depot::repeated_request`) also refuses a vehicle at full health, at every repair depot (naval yards and bunker depots included): without a tether it loses its contact and falls back to rest at its first *enter* processing after the order, before it reaches the docking point; a tethered one keeps its contact, as it makes no repeated request. The first acceptance (the click and the order) still makes no damage test (`claim_16_full_health_refused_at_the_repeated_request`, `claim_16_full_health_refusal_and_the_tether`).
- **Docking point (claim 18; S-0504 claim 12).** `depot::docking_point`: the foundation centre plus the place's displacement (`econ::release::pad_position_at`, S-0404 claim 15's rules), or the cell (2, 1) for a vehicle factory. `path::heading_point` heads a vehicle in depot contact for it, and `hover::destination_point` makes it a hovering vehicle's destination *D*, so a hover stops in the docking cell of a depot larger than one cell.
- **Repair (claim 6; S-0504 claim 8).** `depot::process_repair` follows claim 6's processing spacing table (S-0314 revision 73): waiting (the naval waiting gate by the moving-destination test, 3 ticks to settling; not accepted, *D* ticks and the vehicle stops being settled), settling (within 200 leptons the step waits, rechecking after 1 tick, until the vehicle is settled and, hovering, down, and a processing that finds it standing still by its movement report alone settles it; the step empties its order-level destination; at 200 leptons or more a vehicle moving by the moving-destination test stops being settled, no step, *D* ticks; every settling outcome is followed *D* ticks later, the production hold 1 tick), working (processed every tick; the second step due *D* + *K*<sub>u</sub> − 1 ticks after the settling step, then every *K*<sub>u</sub>, kept in `DepotState::due`; only movement defers a step; done or not needed announces, puts the depot back in settling and sends the vehicle away; cannot pay returns to settling and announces low funds at exactly 0 credits; every outcome followed 1 tick later). Release goes to the depot's rally point, else claim 25's departure cell (`depot::exit_cell`: door kinds, the naval rows, S-0404's exit list or the perimeter, the first giving free); a neutral or special house's vehicle with a guard spot goes there. With no cell the vehicle keeps contact: from working it returns to settling with a 1-tick retry, in settling it retries after *D*. The announcements are `Sound::UnitRepaired` (audio: F7 kind *unit repaired*, 2 cells, 400 ticks) and `Sound::LowFunds`, the depot owner's only.
- **Orders, sale, destruction, power (claims 20 to 23).** `depot::order_applied` (from `orders::apply_behaviour`) and `depot::stop_order` give claim 20's and claim 23's order rules; a docked vehicle is tethered (`econ::tethered`) and keeps its contact, while an untethered one, at an ordinary depot in any repair phase, is released by another order. `depot::vehicle_turn` (from `tick::object_turn`, after the locomotor movement) retries a queued entry every tick; permission queues *enter* with an immediate start only when ready. Since step 1j (S-0314 revision 25, answering Q-1156, and revision 26, editorial only; the step 1h walk to a cell near the depot is gone), a damaged vehicle's enter order on a depot holding a contact runs the order's other effects with no destination (`depot::OrderEffect::NoDestination`): *enter* queued without an immediate start, the depot remembered (`DepotState::entering`) and the queued entry set. *Enter* becomes current at the vehicle's next ready start and `depot::process_enter` repeats the request to the remembered depot: with no free place the depot accepts without contact (`depot::repeated_request`, claim 16's free-place refusal governs the click and the retry only) and the vehicle stays where it is with no destination; a free place is taken without a destination, and the same turn's retry grants and assigns the depot; a refusal (switched off) ends contact and falls back to rest, the queued entry kept, and the retry gives up when the place frees (`claim_23_queued_enter_current_no_walk`, `claim_23_switched_off_queued_vehicle`, `claim_23_free_place_taken_by_the_processing`). Since step 1k (S-0314 revision 28, answering Q-1158) a docked vehicle standing on its depot and given an enter order on that depot takes the queued entry in full (*enter* queued, the depot remembered, no destination); its *enter* is current at the next start and processed as the repeated request only, queuing nothing at either side; the same turn's retry refuses it (claim 16) and clears contact, queued entry and destination, and *enter* falls back at its next processing (`claim_20_same_depot_enter_while_docked`); the processing that takes a free place gives no destination, as built. Since S-0314 revision 29 (step 1l, answering Q-1162) movement arrival requests no map-east turn, tethered or not (`claim_17_arrival_requests_no_turn`); only a turn already in progress holds the arrival *sleep*, by S-0215 claim 11's ordinary readiness (`claim_17_existing_turn_holds_the_arrival_sleep`). The step 1k rotation at arrival and the Q-1162 interim are gone. Save format 54 (53 on the step 1j branch; main's S-0606 work took 53 first). The goldens were regenerated at step 1j: their hashes changed only for the new state field of S-0605 revision 7 (`StructState::fate_tick`), and no gate game plays differently. A sale gives the run-away notice (`depot::sale_started`, from `econ::sell`) and releases contacts at the next selling processing; destruction releases without notice (`depot::depot_destroyed`, from `combat::death`, beyond S-0312 claim 17's kill distance). A switched-off depot refuses new acceptance only. The three quirks of S-0314 Edge cases are live. Since S-0314 revision 31 (step 1j, intake 14S) a depot that loses its contact reacts by phase (claim 22): waiting goes to guard at once; working moves to settling and rechecks one tick later; settling goes to guard once special slot 3 is not running. A settling depot that still has its contact waits while a production slot is running (claim 6). Both slot tests ask the seam `depot::slot_running(w, k, slot)` (`DepotSlot::Production`, `DepotSlot::Special3`). It reports no running slot until S-0108 claim 20's simulation slot model is built (intake 14B). No new state is saved, so the save format and the goldens are unchanged (`claim_22_contact_loss_by_phase`, `slot_seam_reports_no_running_slot`). Since intake 55b (S-0314 revision 107 with M-0007) the waiting row with no contact sets the next processing 1 tick later and the depot is in *guard* from that tick, whether or not *special-3* still runs: our fourth phase number, `phase::LEAVING`, marks that tick's processing. Destruction now tells a vehicle before its movement arrival in the docking-point cell (target lost, *guard*) from one after it (`depot::Link::arrived`, set by `depot::movement_arrival`): an arrived vehicle keeps its behaviour, docked or not, and `quirks.depot_loss_leaves_sleep` off gives every arrived vehicle the sale's run-away notice. Save format 91, AI game format 53.
- **Settled vehicles (S-0314 revisions 73 and 88 Terms).** `depot::settled`: made settled by the movement arrival in the docking cell, by a near settling processing that finds it standing still, and by a disabling's switch-off (`depot::disabled`, S-0405 claim 20). `depot::settled_at_depot` (r88): the arrival and the settling processing make it settled at a depot, and so does a disabling while it has contact or a depot occupies its cell; any other disabling makes it settled away, and reaching a depot's cell later changes nothing. Ended by an effective destination received while a depot or tank bunker occupies the cell, by a vehicle whose locomotor counts as switched off (r88: a hover only once it is down, `depot::counts_switched_off`) (`depot::destination_given`, from `infantry::destination_assigned` and the scatter's assignments; S-0207 claim 27's effective), a distant moving settling processing, a waiting processing that does not accept it, an enabling (`depot::enabled`) and, with `quirks.depot_loss_strands_hover` off, the end of contact. Each switches a hovering vehicle off or on (`hover::switched`, `HoverState::off`). A settled hover that is down does not travel (`hover::tick`), so after its depot is sold or destroyed with the quirk on it is stranded. The tank bunker's release switches a hover on without ending its being settled (S-0606). Being placed on the map again is not built as a switch-on (no claim owns it here).
- **Import.** `UnitRepair`, `NumberOfDocks`, `DockingOffset`*i* and the factory kind were already imported. New: an importer warning for `NumberOfDocks` above 1 on a repair depot (S-0314 Edge cases).
- **Our AI.** At each think, a damaged idle army vehicle (at or below half its strength, our tuning) of a house with an accepting depot is given an ordinary enter order on the nearest one (`Brain::send_for_repair`; S-0314 claim 24).
- **What play needs.** A repair depot is a building type with `UnitRepair=yes`; built and placed like any building, or spawned by a map. Its foundation cells must let the vehicle reach the docking point: S-0209 claim 5's row VP12 makes cells beyond `NumberImpassableRows` passable. A ship needs a naval vehicle factory with `UnitRepair=yes` and a water cell at (2, 1). A person selects a damaged vehicle and clicks the depot: `targeting::click` gives *enter*, `orders::issue` applies it, and the rest runs by itself; the owner needs credits for the steps.

S-0207 revision 28 answers Q-0797 (claim 26, with S-0215 claims 22 and 29), and its row is retired. A driving or ship vehicle's order-level goal is removed by `drive::goal_cleanup`, in the first locomotion update that begins without an active track. A cell destination goes while the vehicle occupies that cell, whatever its behaviour and with no search, tolerance or alignment, and the locomotor destination point goes with it; this is also the assignment tick when assignment precedes the update, and the tick after a final track's completion, which clears only the point (`drive::arrive_or_rest` now only rests the vehicle). A building destination is not removed by cell equality; only a vehicle at rest in *guard* whose world point equals the locomotor destination point in x, y and z loses it (the same removal). With approach destinations (`queued_dests`) the goal is abandoned and the vehicle falls back to rest (`process::fall_back`). The cleanup does not touch the behaviour timer: a due *move* processing before it keeps the standard delay, the next one with no destination falls back with 1 tick (`process` is unchanged).

S-0314 revision 73 (with S-0108 revision 60) answers Q-1610 in part: claim 6's processing spacing table gives when a depot meets a lost contact (the processing already scheduled; working is processed every tick). Its interim row is retired; the parts left open are the spec's Open questions, and nothing is built for them beyond the stated rules. Revision 107, with M-0007, answers its questions 1 and 2 for a destroyed vehicle and question 3 for a depot back in *guard* (intake 55b); what stays open (a vehicle that leaves in working or settling, a replacement contact before the pending processing) is the spec's Open questions, so the request is answered as far as the spec goes.

S-0207 revision 29 answers Q-0830 (claim 26): a destination naming an object, whether a building, vehicle, infantry unit or aircraft and moving or not, is not a cell destination; `drive::goal_cleanup` keeps it under cell equality and only the guard row removes it, comparing the retained locomotor point. Its row is retired (tests `claim_26_a_destination_naming_a_unit_is_kept_by_cell_equality` and `claim_26_guard_row_for_a_destination_naming_a_unit_compares_the_retained_point`). S-0504 revision 18, S-0507 revision 8 and S-0215 revision 46 restate it for ships, driving transport passengers and behaviour processing; the same code serves them, with tests added in step 4 ([docs/m6-claims/step4.md](../m6-claims/step4.md)).

S-0314 revision 20 with S-0207 revision 27 answers Q-0794 (claim 17 and S-0207 claim 25). The driving and ship locomotor keeps its own destination point (`DriveState::goal`). An accepted instruction installs it at the destination's assignment (`drive::take_destination`, from `infantry::destination_assigned`; a depot's first contact re-supplies the docking point, `depot::take`). It is not tied to a search or a track. A stop clears it, as does a qualifying final track completion (in the point's cell, height within 208), and a give-up through the destination's removal. The movement report tests its presence (`drive::goal_present`), with an untaken assignment counting at once. Its row is retired. The order-level destination's removal at the goal cell is claim 26's (above).

S-0314 revision 19 answers Q-0790 (claim 17): an empty place 0 fails the pad exchange's distance test; the movement arrival sets the depot's ready mark (S-0215 claim 29); a driving or ship vehicle reports movement by claim 17's table (destination point, or a heading point that differs horizontally), a hover by S-0503 claim 5's *is moving*. Its row is retired.

S-0314 revision 17 answers Q-0681 (claim 21: distant settling), Q-0682 (claims 6 and 25: the departure cell and the failed departure) and Q-0683 (claim 23: the retry after movement, readiness-dependent start); their rows are retired. Q-0680 is answered too, but its tether rule (only at a depot with the pad flag) leaves the ordinary depot's tether unexplained, so its row stays with Q-0674.

S-0314 revision 18 answers Q-0674. Claim 25's locomotion-specific test always gives free for driving, hovering and ship locomotion, which was our interim; every imported foundation word has a present exit list (the 0 × 0 list is present and empty), so `present_exit_list`'s `None` arises only from synthetic data; claim 6's repair-complete announcement is attempted even when the working departure fails, as coded. Its claim 17 also settles the tether (Q-0680's row is retired): only a pad depot's repeated request tethers, no foundation or docking cell does, and the standing refusal is the click's and the queued retry's only; claim 18 states the cell-centre stop. Claim 23's retry is skipped only during chrono phase-in (`teleport::phasing_in`), and the passenger sync of an open-topped transport now runs before it (`tick::object_turn`).

## Simulation (M6b: walls and gates)

Walls and gates in play: S-0112 claim 7, S-0210 claims 4, 8, 13 and 14, S-0215 claim 35, S-0108 claims 2, 4 and 5, S-0406 claims 8 to 11 and S-0408 claim 12. Most of the rules were built in M5a (placement, fill, sale, bits, owners) and M4 (wall damage, S-0303 F4); M6b closes the gaps between them and draws the result. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/walls-gates.md](../m6-claims/walls-gates.md).

- **Wall hits from crushing and towers.** A crushed wall at stage *D* − 2 now deals its neighbour hits through `combat::area::wall_success` (S-0303 F4), so the hit walls change stage or go. A destroyed wall tower deals S-0112 claim 7's four hits of 200 (`objects::remove`, through `combat::area::wall_hit`) after its departure has recomputed the bits; a tower that leaves alive deals none (open question of the claim).
- **Map wall buildings.** A wall-type building of the map's building list is placed as its wall overlay at stage 0 (`objects::place_map_objects`, with `econ::placement::put_wall`); no building remains. Its owner is the building's only initially: S-0210 claim 14 (a) re-owns it with every other map wall before play, even to no owner. The viewer's static pass leaves such buildings out once the cells are loaded, so the overlay is drawn instead.
- **Crush events.** `Effect::OverlayCrushed` and `Sound::Crush` (S-0210 claims 4 and 8); audio plays the overlay type's `CrushSound` and the crushed type's crush sound at the cell.
- **Gates.** The door and the *open* behaviour (S-0215 claim 35, `process::gate_open`, `behaviour::Door`) were built before M6b. The view now draws every gate live, the map's included (`Live::live_gates`), with the door's frame of S-0108 claim 4 (`unload::gate_frame`): *p* of S-0215 claim 35 d1, damaged *p* + *G* + 1, no clamp, no bib, unloading or under-door image while it moves or rests open, and only while *open* is current. With `quirks.gate_reverse_snaps` off, a reversed door reopens over *T*<sub>d</sub> from progress *T*<sub>d</sub> − *a* (d2).
- **Overlays redrawn in play.** `Live::redraw` compares each cell's overlay and data value with the start of the game; a changed cell's static overlay items leave the cell pass and the cell is drawn again from the world (`Painter::live_overlays`). Walls built, sold, crushed, hit or filled, and harvested ore, so show their state. The redrawn items follow the cell pass (our placeholder place; the depth test orders them).
- **Seams.** Gate build-up and selling reversal (S-0108 claim 4) wait for build-up drawing, which the live view does not do for any building. A gate's body tilt is not drawn: the renderer has no building tilt.
- **What play needs.** A wall type is a building type with `Wall=yes` and `ToOverlay` naming an overlay with `Wall=yes`; a gate is a building with `Gate=yes` (and `GateStages` in art). A person builds a wall from the sidebar and places it: the fill closes gaps up to the guard range to the house's own walls. A tank with `Crusher=yes` and movement zone crusher-all drives through enemy walls; a gate opens when the owner's unit drives at it and closes behind it. A wall is sold with the sell-wall order (S-0213 table T1).

Provisional rules of M6b walls and gates: none at present. S-0210 revision 20 answers Q-0684 (claim 14: the code already did this) and S-0215 revision 39 answers Q-0685 (claim 35 d1 to d3: the rest-open image while *open* is current, and the snaps-off reversal); their rows are retired.

## Simulation (M6b: teleport movement)

The teleporting locomotor's own move (S-0207 claims 20 to 22, F9; S-0208 claims 19 and 20) is in `glasnost_sim::teleport`, built before M6b. M6b makes it whole in play. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/teleport.md](../m6-claims/teleport.md).

- **Presentation events.** The two warp animations (`WarpOut`) go through `combat::effects::anim`, so the view draws them; they keep their creation draws (claim 20 i). The departure and arrival sounds are `Sound::ChronoOut` at the old point and `Sound::ChronoIn` at *L*; audio plays the type's `ChronoOutSound` or `ChronoInSound`, else the game-wide one.
- **Arrival effects.** A vehicle's landing has the effects of reaching a track target: the arrival report to a contacted building (S-0402 claim 10, and a tethered vehicle's clear report to its factory, S-0417 claims 7 and 8) and cell-centre crushing (S-0210 claims 3 and 8), then the entered-by raise (claim 22). A teleporting crusher crushes what it lands on. A tethered infantry unit's landing gives its clear report to its infantry factory (S-0417 claim 10), so a teleporting unit that jumps away no longer holds the factory's contact place.
- **Seams.** The airstrike target-retention exception (S-0519, S-0207 Open questions), the electromagnetic pulse (S-0511), *being erased* (S-0514) and a superweapon's hold (S-06xx) have no state in our engine; `teleport::take_destination` and `teleport::jump` mark where they go.
- **What play needs.** A vehicle, infantry or aircraft type with `Locomotor=` the teleporting locomotor (the importer maps it). A person selects it and clicks a cell: `targeting::click` gives *move* to the clicked cell itself (S-0214 claim 11), `orders::issue` applies it, the landing point is fixed and marked at once, and the unit jumps on its next movement update, then phases in for F9's delay. `[General] WarpOut`, the `[General]` keys of F9 (`ChronoTrigger`, `ChronoDistanceFactor`, `ChronoMinimumDelay`, `ChronoRangeMinimum`) and `[AudioVisual] ChronoOutSound` and `ChronoInSound` give the animation, timing and sounds.

Provisional rules of M6b teleport movement: none at present. S-0417 revision 15 answers Q-0697 (claim 10: a tethered teleporting infantry unit reports clear in its jump's arrival update, before the entered-by raise; the code already did this).

## Simulation (M6a: aircraft)

S-0501 (aircraft flight, attack runs and pads) is built in `glasnost_sim::air`. Its game-wide values are `SimRules::air` (`air::AirRules`: the cruise height, the service cadence from the reload rate, the re-approach flag, the attacking-aircraft sight range, the spy-plane camera frames and F4's decimal constants); the per-type keys (flight level, ammunition, landable, airport-bound, fighter, fly-by, fly-back, docks) and the pad keys (helipad, unit reload, docking places) are on the type rules. All are imported by `glasnost_convert`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0501.md](../m6-claims/S-0501.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0501.rs`.

- **Which objects fly.** `air::is_aircraft`: an aircraft-kind object on the fly locomotor that is not a rocket. Other aircraft-kind objects (for example a teleporting type) are untouched.
- **Flight (claims 1 to 10, F1 to F7).** `air::placed` (from `objects::place_with`) sets the placement height by the visiting mark already present; `air::released_docked` (from `econ::release`) stands a new aircraft on its pad. The fly locomotor is `air::tick`: climb, descent, the speed fraction F4, horizontal motion with the map-edge push-back, landing, touchdown with F7's refusal, take-off (which ends the exit tether), and the crash fall F5 (`air::start_crash`, from `combat::death` and `spawn`).
- **Attack runs (claims 11 to 20, T1).** `air::behaviour_turn` (from `process::turn`) runs attack, guard, hunt, move, enter, retreat and the spy-plane behaviours. Ammunition is spent once per run, not per shot (`combat::fire` skips the per-shot spend for flying aircraft). The firing check adds the empty-passenger-carrier and fighter rules (`combat::check`).
- **Pads (claims 21 to 30, F8 to F12).** `air::find_pad`, `air::request_contact`, `air::landing_spot` and the servicing in `air::pad_turn` (from the tick pipeline's building branch): reload every P ticks, repair at a cost, release into guard. Free docks and the pad total are S-0407 claim 16's (`production::dock_free`, `tech`).
- **Pipeline.** In `tick::object_turn`: a dead crashing aircraft gets `air::tick` and `air::end_turn`; a building gets `air::pad_turn` after its events; an aircraft gets `air::end_turn` after its events. Readiness and the fall-back rows are in `process` (`air::ready`, `air::fall_back_early`, `air::fall_back_rows`). The removal marks are in `teleport::removal_marks`.
- **Seams.** A passenger aircraft's drop (S-0522); clearing a cloaked target (S-0516 claim 25, no object cloaks yet); an aircraft sinking in water (claim 9's sinking branch, with claim 37's splash draws).
- **The damage-smoke animation (S-0501 r65 claim 37, Q-2237; M8 A3 intake 67, [intake67-S-0501-S-0113.md](../m8-claims/intake67-S-0501-S-0113.md)).** A shown smoke is created by the simulation: `air::smoke` calls `anims::spawn` with the fixed type `air::DAMAGE_SMOKE` (looked up without regard to letter case; no rules entry names it), at the aircraft's position after its own movement in that tick, not attached, with no loop delay, repeat factor 1 and no reverse request, and pushes `Effect::Anim` for the view. The creation's draws (S-0109 claims 3 and 13) follow *u* in the aircraft's turn. No type of that name: nothing is created and *u* is still taken. Under `quirks.aircraft_smoke_scenario_draw` off neither the draw nor the animation happens. No new state (an ordinary animation in play), so no format change. Tests `claims_s0501_r65.rs`.
- **The edge, the pad contact and the scatter (S-0501 r93, Q-2251, Q-2252, Q-2291; S-0502 r21; M8 A3 intake 76, [intake76.md](../m8-claims/intake76.md)).**
  - Claim 4's push-back takes its sign from the new position's local column (`air::edge_column_low`: *a* = ⌊(*d* + *W*) / 2⌋ − *L*x against *T* = ⌊(*W* + *H* − 1) / 2⌋). Under `quirks.aircraft_edge_hold` off (admitted to the play-fixes layer) an aircraft that may leave the map now is not pushed back and claim 10's removal takes it out.
  - Claims 5 and 6: the exact-cell landing trigger reads the same tick's ordinary target, so the approach replacement never starts a landing, and tests the flight point's cell.
  - Claims 18 and 24: the contact is made when a pad is accepted as destination with *enter* current or queued and no contact held (`air::pad_accepted`, from `destination_taken`); the search after T2's row asks for no contact (`find_pad`). *Enter*'s processing with no contact was a seam (Q-2326), built at intake 80 (below).
  - Claim 49: `scatter::answer` answers every request to an aircraft alike: a fall-back to rest when its current behaviour has the scatter property or it has none, otherwise nothing.
  - Claim 50: an unarmed pad-bound aircraft makes no pad search and hovers near its pad; `quirks.unarmed_pad_bound_hover` off (rejected for the layer) lets a landable type with dock types seek a pad at T2's last in-flight row.
  - S-0502 r21 restates what is built (comments in `lift.rs`). No new state, so the save format stays 92 and the AI game format 55. Tests `claims_s0501_r93.rs`, `claims_s0501_r93_edge.rs`.
- **Enter without a contact (S-0501 r99, Q-2326 answered; M8 A3 intake 80, [intake80.md](../m8-claims/intake80.md)).**
  - Claim 24: every processing of *enter* after its first that finds no contact returns 1 tick (`air::without_contact`). With nothing queued it forgets the destination and falls back to rest, whose pad search gives the pad back with the contact, so the next processing goes on in the approaching stage (AC31 part f; queueing *enter* while it is current with nothing queued starts nothing, claim 18). With a behaviour queued it keeps the pad and *enter*, asks no pad and waits; a destination that is no longer a pad (gone, being sold, cleared at a touchdown) ends the wait with a fall-back.
  - A refusal of the loading request ends the contact; a pad being sold is no longer the destination and the aircraft falls back whatever is queued, any other refusal is followed at once by the processing without a contact (Open question 19 keeps the refusal open).
  - A waiting aircraft landing over its pad is moved toward the docking position without a contact (`air::docking_step`: one docking place, its position; otherwise the foundation centre, S-0404 claim 15).
  - Seam under **Q-2366**: F7 refuses a cell of the pad to an aircraft with no contact, so claim 24's touchdown without a contact comes only off the pad; and what the pad-bound ground rule "comes first" before. No quirk and no new state: save format 93, AI game format 56. Tests `claims_s0501_r99.rs`. Q-2366 was answered by r103 and built at intake 86 (below).
- **The docking stage without a contact, the pad-bound rule first (S-0501 r103, Q-2366 answered; M8 A3 intake 86, [intake86.md](../m8-claims/intake86.md)).**
  - Claim 24's rule before every other (`air::bound_away`, first in `air::enter`): a pad-bound aircraft on the ground by its height whose destination is not the building in its own cell leaves its contact, records a building destination as `heading_pad` and queues *move* with an immediate start, 1 tick. *Move*'s claim 40 selection then seeks a cell off the pad (F7 refuses its cells without a contact).
  - The heading point of a pad (a building of the aircraft's dock types) for an aircraft without a contact is `pad_position_at(w, k, NO_PLACE)`, height included (`air::heading_point_for`), so a high docking position makes claim 6's holding mark.
  - The wait (`air::without_contact`) lasts one processing whenever a behaviour is queued, a cell destination included, and never moves the aircraft; a processing that finds it landing over its pad moves it on to the docking stage. The docking stage without a contact (`air::docking_without_contact`): a docking step while landing, else a fall-back whatever is queued.
  - Claim 7: a touchdown reports nothing; the docking at a touchdown outside *enter* is gone. Claim 31: the processing that moves an aircraft on to the docking stage leaves it not ready until its next processing (`air::ready_mark_cleared`, derived from that processing's record).
  - Seams under **Q-2436** (the approaching stage in contact still gives the pad back as destination; the pad recorded for a cell destination; a touchdown outside *enter*), **Q-2437** (a lost pad ends the wait; the move on to the docking stage needs the pad's cell; the docking stage's fall-back keeps the destination; a sale in the docking stage), **Q-2438** (the derived ready mark when *enter* is left by force). No quirk and no new state: save format 95, AI game format 58 (intake 87's). Tests `claims_s0501_r103.rs`. Goldens: the duel and the naval duel move (the rework of the processing without a contact), the other seven do not.
- **The load request, the run-away notice and the kept ready mark (S-0501 r110, Q-2436 to Q-2438 answered; M8 A3 intake 92, [intake92.md](../m8-claims/intake92.md)).**
  - Claim 24's second rule (`air::enter`, `air::load_request`): an aircraft in flight with no destination asks its contact to be loaded, and so does a pad-bound one in flight in the approaching stage; a switched-on pad confirms, a refusal ends the contact; unconfirmed, a fall-back at once whatever is queued. Confirmed and settled, the pad becomes the destination and *enter* starts again at once.
  - The approaching and docking stages in contact test neither destination nor position: the docking step moves toward the docking position, and the first processing on the ground reports entry wherever it stands (`air::pad_turn`). The report queues *sleep*, then the aircraft queues *guard* (`air::report_entered`). A pad that starts selling sends the run-away notice (`air::sale_started`, from `econ::sell`): contact ended, an F8 landing spot, *move* queued, then *guard*.
  - Claim 8's exception (`air::ignores`), claim 29 at cruise height, T2's *enter* row with only the destination, the pad-bound rule's no-pad record of a cell destination.
  - The ready mark is kept state (`AirState::mark_cleared`), set and cleared where claim 31 lists. Two quirks, both admitted off in play-fixes: `quirks.aircraft_grounded_enter_stall` and `quirks.aircraft_ready_mark_outlives_enter`.
  - Seams under **Q-2506** (the docking stage in contact in flight and not landing), **Q-2507** (the landing point of an aircraft landing where it is) and **Q-2508** (the order of several run-away notices). Save format 97, AI game format 60. Tests `claims_s0501_r110.rs`. All three were answered by r120 and built at intake 96 (below).
- **Landing where it is, the forget at touchdown and the entry report in flight (S-0501 r120 and r121, Q-2506 to Q-2508 answered; M8 A3 intake 96, [intake96.md](../m8-claims/intake96.md)).**
  - Claim 6: an aircraft in flight, moving (it holds a flight point; pitch is not modelled) and not landing that is given no destination gets a new flight point with no destination object (`air::given_no_destination`, `AirState::own_point`): its own cell when F7 and the reservation test accept it, else claim 40's replacement search with its draws; an F8 landing spot with *attack* current and no target; none with *attack* and a target (claim 42). It is called from `econ::assign_destination` (through `air::destination_taken`), from the stop order, and at the next movement update for any other clear. The landing then starts in that cell, and the descent does not steer (the descent move needs a destination). An aircraft with no flight point and no destination starts no landing.

  - Claim 7: the touchdown first forgets the destination and the flight point in the flight point's cell, or when that cell holds its contact (no building, with no contact) (`air::touchdown_forgets`). A refusal rises 10 leptons and starts a take-off that ends at the next movement update with the landing's held height; after a forget nothing moves the aircraft until it gets a destination (`AirState::stranded`).
  - Claim 24: the docking stage in contact that finds the aircraft in flight and not landing reports entry at once (`air::enter`); the run-away notices go in contact-place order and no spot depends on another. Claim 29's unarmed branch is tested. r121 adds T2's in-a-team row for a visiting aircraft on the ground, which our engine (no teams) never takes.

No quirk. Save format 99, AI game format 62. Tests `claims_s0501_r120.rs` (claim 8's exception with a vehicle in the flight point's cell as destination). All four were answered by r132 and built at intake 102 (below).
