One that forgot rests (`air::touchdown` with `forget_dest` false); one that kept its flight point is on the ground by its height, not at rest (`air::untested_kept`). Off, today's tested touchdown (rejected for the layer).

The untested touchdown that kept its flight point slides the same way. Off: ordinary held height at once (rejected for the layer: the slide ends by itself).

(Q-2577): over a cell outside the usable area, not visiting and not free to leave, the selection starts from a local edge cell (`air::stop_start`, `quirks.aircraft_offmap_stop_start`, on; admitted off in play-fixes).

A docking-stage processing that finds an aircraft on the ground by its height and not at rest reports entry as in flight (Q-2649 interim).

Save format 102, AI game format 65. Tests `claims_s0501_r132.rs`. All five were answered by r134 and built at intake 104 (below).
- **The landing check's refusal, the slide's listing and the restarted approach (S-0501 r133 and r134, Q-2646 to Q-2650 answered; M8 A3 intake 104, [intake104.md](../m8-claims/intake104.md)).**
  - `air::tick_flight`'s order is now: sector update, crash, start, the given-no-destination clear, destination and holding mark, the released return, the drop, held height, take-off requests (`air::take_off_requests`), forward step, altitude, write-back, speed fraction and claim 6's triggers, then **the landing step** (`air::landing_step`), then the released climb and guidance. The landing step re-files a landing that started in the update (S-0102 claim 4), makes claim 7's check of the flight point's cell (Q-2646: after the descent and the triggers), and touches down at height 0.
  - Claim 7 (Q-2646): a refused cell (`air::check_refused`) takes off toward cruise height and looks for a free cell with S-0212 claim 25's nearby search around the cell it is over (T5's new row: class track, zone fly, nothing else). Found: the cell's centre is the new flight point and the destination is kept (`AirState::ignored`); above height 0 the take-off ends in that update (`air::end_refusal_take_off`), so it climbs from the next; at height 0 the touchdown follows, untested in both positions of either switch, forgetting against the new point (rest) or keeping it (`air::refusal_touchdown_kept`: on the ground with ordinary flight's held height, no slide). None found: a hit of its current health with the demolition warhead (`damage::apply`, defences ignored, no attacker, no house, distance 0); its flight point is cleared and no touchdown follows (what a survivor does next: Q-2676, design cite).
  - Claim 7: every touchdown sets both speed fractions to 0, the untested ones and the refused touchdown's rise included; `air::touchdown` clears the old flag before it sets the new.

The r132 interim that rested a sliding aircraft whose point had gone (`settle_sliding`) is removed.

(Q-2648): the exact-cell trigger applies while it slides, and that update's landing step touches down at once.
  - Claim 24 (Q-2649): the report on the ground makes no facing request, as r132's interim did. (Q-2650): the pad's answer that restarts entry makes entry's beginning in either stage (`air::restarted`, after both `load_request` calls of the approaching stage and the docking stage's). Claim 26 (Q-2649): the release of a ready aircraft at full health comes before the skip of one flying in or moving (`air::pad_turn`); a skipped one does not count as settled.
  - Requests **Q-2676** (an aircraft that survives the hit: interim, it flies back to the refused point and is hit again, or with no destination climbs and coasts on its fraction) and **Q-2677** (*guard* with a target during the slide, and where it rests: interim as the code does, no cite). No quirk; the C7 entries for the three switches whose scope moved are in [c7-play-fixes.md](../m8-claims/c7-play-fixes.md). Save format 104, AI game format 68. Tests `claims_s0501_r134.rs`. Both were answered by r146 and built at intake 108 (below); the interims are gone.
- **The survivor's origin point and claim 33 at the movement update (S-0501 r146, Q-2676 and Q-2677 answered; M8 A3 intake 108, [intake108.md](../m8-claims/intake108.md)).**
  - Claim 7 (Q-2676): where the refusal finds no free cell, the hit comes as before and the flight point becomes the map's origin point (`AirState::origin`, with the destination kept recorded in `ignored`), the flight unfinished. The hit update's take-off requests aim at the origin point by band (`air::end_refusal_take_off` with `ORIGIN`). While it holds it, `air::flight_target` gives no flight point, so claim 5's calculation, the fixed-full-speed case, guidance and claim 6's triggers stay out; the forward step, the climb and the fraction step go on: it stops in the air or flies on at full speed along the line, off the legal map too (claims 4 and 10 as for any aircraft; nothing added there, Open question 28). A given destination ends it (`air::destination_taken`, `air::give_point`); being given no destination is claim 6's own point (`air::given_no_destination`). A survivor is reachable in tests through S-0302 claim 8's stage 2 rows.
  - Claim 33 (Q-2677): `air::guard_move` queues *move* in each movement update after claim 31's first start point for a living aircraft that is not pad-bound and not crashing, with *guard* current, a destination and nothing queued, on the ground included; `air::guard`'s processing no longer does it (claim 29, "at any height"). So the slide after an entry report ends with *move*'s take-off and a new landing, and the aircraft rests in *enter* (`quirks.aircraft_grounded_enter_stall`). Interim **Q-2726**: *sticky* gets no such *move*.
  - No quirk; the texts of `aircraft_edge_hold` and `aircraft_grounded_enter_stall` gained cases, re-judged in [c7-play-fixes.md](../m8-claims/c7-play-fixes.md). Save format 110, AI game format 75. Tests `claims_s0501_r146.rs`. Goldens (on main e0695d3): duel, duel-diagonal, duel-m6c, duel-naval and skirmish move through the hash only. Lanes (main 01c5688 against the branch rebased onto it, before intake 107; not run again on e0695d3):
    - The sweeps are identical with and without the layer.
    - The arena is byte-identical; it was run on 07d549c, and no code it runs has changed since.
    - In the AI quick lane, 4 of 36 games change and one winner changes (seed 9, map 0, sides 0 and 2). The trace finds claim 33's *move* coming in the tick a destroyer's ASW aircraft gets *guard* with a destination, three ticks before main's.
    - `glasnost-ai --include-ignored` gives 263 passed and 0 failed on both.
- **Revision 7 (answers Q-0630 to Q-0636; Q-0634 is answered).** The firing spot is claim 39's circle search around the target (`air::firing_spot`: radii ρ − 256, ρ − 512, … above 256, facings in steps of 4096, scored by planar distance to the reference; no spot for a short-range weapon). A move destination on an occupied object is replaced by claim 40's ring search (`air::move_destination`, one U(0, 7) draw per radius), and the flight point is the destination's heading point, a pad's docking position for a contacting aircraft. `air::stopped` is claim 40's "no unfinished flight" (pitch is not modelled, so the forward-pitch term is always false). Claim 4 adds the edge push-back fallback position and the 5-lepton descent nudge toward the destination cell's centre; claim 6 the armed flight-point height and the 768-lepton destination-height rule; claim 7 the ground-layer boundary; claim 8 the take-off heading bands (no new heading request at or below trunc(H / 2)). Claim 24's enter is staged: the processing after touchdown recognizes arrival, then docks and reports, then sleeps with guard queued. Claim 36: a released slot aircraft that crashes gets health 0 and keeps its unfinished flight and fraction (`spawn`). Claim 34's edge cell is `air::edge_cell_on` on the house's computed edge, which `air::recompute_edge` sets again whenever a building factory is marked primary (`House::computed_edge`, S-0522 claim 5; the west start row is `quirks.paradrop_west_edge_start_row`). Claim 41 and F8's landing spot use the fixed foot/normal search with the 3 × 3 building footprint, the 1 × 1 own-cell replacement below 256 leptons and the other-object fallback (`air::landing_spot`). F6's wait uses the converted speed; a converted speed of 0 gives a wait of 0, with an importer warning (`glasnost_convert`).
- **Revisions 8 and 9 (answer Q-0650 and Q-0651).**
  - **Speed fraction (claim 5, F4).** The slowdown distance is signed: τ₀ = min(1, *d* / *S*) for *S* ≠ 0, and 1 for *S* = 0 (`air::slowing`). The approach replacement no longer feeds the next tick's calculation. It sets only the target fraction, which governs a tick that skips the calculation.
  - **Guidance (claim 42).** `air::guidance` runs after the movement step in `air::tick`, as the last facing requests of the update. It requests the body toward the flight point. A strafer within 768 leptons skips the body request when the turn would exceed 8192 units. Below 256 leptons the aiming facing (`Object::turret`) is requested toward the target, or toward the landing direction. Otherwise it is requested toward the flight point. There are no requests under the run lock. `air::request` makes a later request in the same tick replace an earlier one without a trace: the facing is snapshotted at the tick's first request (`AirState::request_tick`, `body_was`, `aim_was`).
  - **T1.** An ordinary attacker moves to *firing* when its current fraction is exactly 0, keeping its destination. The firing transitions and the below-16-lepton row have a 1-tick delay. The steering rows request only the aiming facing.
  - **Flight point (claims 12 and 42).** The flight point is kept when the destination is cleared in *attack* (current or queued) with a target (`air::flight_target`). `AirState::face_target` and `replaced` are gone, and so is `air::hovering_still`.
  - **Landing placeholder (claim 41, F8).** With the new `quirks.landing_own_cell_no_result` on (the original), an empty own-cell search below 256 leptons gives the placeholder `Target::Cell((0, 0))`. `air::end_turn` discards it (`air::is_placeholder`), clearing the destination and target, and falls back to rest. Off, the other-object fallback runs.
  - Claim 17 now says strike aircraft, not airstrike aircraft. No aircraft is linked to a strike (S-0519 claims 17 and 20).
  - The save format is 17.
- **Revision 10 (answers Q-0653).**
  - **The 1-lepton step (claim 4, F3).** No fraction of a lepton is kept, so a 1-lepton step at a body facing from 16391 to 32766 (between east and south) changes neither coordinate. The new `quirks.aircraft_unit_step_stall` (on, the original) keeps this. Off, such a step of at least 1 lepton moves 1 lepton along the axis of its larger component, x on equal magnitudes (`air::step`, with `walk::step_components`). An aircraft with converted speed from 10 to 19 whose step falls to 1 lepton outside the halving zone at such a facing hovers there with a positive fraction (claim 5). An ordinary attacker then never fires, and a *move* or a return to a pad stops short of landing. The Q-0653 interim row is gone, and AC24 (b) is flown from its starts b1 to b6.
  - **T1 (claim 42).** The new `quirks.aircraft_zero_step_attack_stall` (on, the original) keeps the zero-fraction test. Off, an ordinary attacker in *approaching* whose positive current fraction gives F3 step length 0 also moves to *firing*, keeping its destination, with a 1-tick delay (`air::step_length`). A 1-lepton step that does not move does not count.
  - Under `quirks.chop_rounding`, two rows of F3's new table differ from S-0208 F3 and S-0205 claim 5 (Q-0654, answered by revision 11, below).
  - The save format stays 17: the switches are rules, not state.
- **Revisions 11 and 12 (revision 11 answers Q-0654; revision 12 restates Q-0650 to Q-0653).**
  - **F3 under `quirks.chop_rounding`.** The round-toward-zero sum keeps the sideways lepton: a 1-lepton step at body facings 32767 to 32772 moves (−1, +1), and at 49148 to 49155 (−1, −1). With the switch off those rows are (0, +1) and (−1, 0). `air::step` already did this (it follows S-0208 F3 and S-0205 claim 5); `f3_unit_step_by_facing` now asserts both tables at every facing. AC24 (b3) drifts west and hovers at a facing near 29778 about 47 leptons west of the column (`claim_5_unit_step_hover_between_east_and_south`); facing 32076 with side 11 is the switch-off result.
  - **F4.** The clamp min(*f*ₕ, *d*) compares numerical magnitudes in leptons, so it changes the fraction only at *d* = 0 (`air::slowing` already did this). The switch-off alternatives are marked unmeasured design rules; the airborne *unload* phases, dock search and waypoint-700 replacement are S-0507 claim 13 (built with S-0507 revision 4).
  - The Q-0654 interim row is retired. No provisional rules of M6a (aircraft) remain.

## Simulation (M6b: hover and amphibious)

S-0503 (hover and amphibious movement) is built in `glasnost_sim::hover`. Its game-wide values are `GeneralRules::hover` (`rules::HoverRules`: hover height, bob, boost, acceleration, brake, dampen and the gravity), imported by `glasnost_convert` from `[General]`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0503.md](../m6-claims/S-0503.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0503.rs` and `crates/convert/src/sim_hover_tests.rs`.

- **Which model (claim 1).** The locomotor field alone chooses it: a vehicle on the hovering locomotor (`rules::Locomotor::Hover`) carries `HoverState` (`Object::hover`) and moves by `hover::tick`, whatever its class or zone. An amphibious type on the driving locomotor drives. The amphibious class only gives land multipliers, and so passability, and the movement zone gives the reach regions.
- **Travel (claims 2 to 7, F1 to F5).** In `hover::tick`, with a heading point: η turns towards it, then come the throttle (F1), the ramp of φ and the boost (F2), the step *d* = trunc(*s* × φ) with the hover's own ρ (`drive::progress_with`), the stalled-start reset, the arrival test on the map plane (no snap), and the straight step along η (`walk::step_point`). Without a heading point, ρ = 1 and the next point is chosen first. Occupancy moves cell to cell as the position crosses an edge. A high-bridge deck is entered at 416 or more above the surface.
- **Next heading point (claims 5 and 12, T1).** `hover::choose_next`:
  - S-0207 claim 19 c (the in-range path discard), then the destination test (its cell, height difference below 208), the fresh search when R has run out, and claim 19 d's arrival stop;
  - then the step check of table T1, through the entry outcomes of `path::step_outcome` and the driving helpers (`drive::blocked_step`, `attack_obstacle`, `give_up_test`, `give_up`, `request_scatter`), now `pub(crate)`.
  - A first check that is not *clear* discards the path, searches at once and checks again. Refused rows zero T, φ and ρ.
  - The body looks one neighbour step beyond the heading point.
- **Height, wake, spin (claims 8 to 11, F6, F7).** These run in the same tick after the travel, in this order:
  - the wake every 10 ticks over water while moving now;
  - the height update (lift, the extra trunc(*g* / 3), gravity, damping, and the bob by the object number);
  - the spin.
- **Switching off.** `econ::power::apply_disabled` (the control-centre rule of S-0405 claim 13) switches a hover off when its flag rises (`hover::switched`):
  - two draws give the spin, unless it is not powered or is in *sleep* or *enter*;
  - the stop request clears the destination and path only when D ≠ P;
  - lift stops.
  - At the spin's end, a hover over water below 104 leptons gets the bomb mark (`hover::bomb_mark`) and starts to fall.
- **Hooks elsewhere.**
  - `infantry::destination_assigned` gives ρ = 1 and, for a powered hover not moving now, chooses the first heading point at once (`hover::take_destination`, claim 5); a hover moving now without a heading point gets T = ρ = 1, the path update (destination-cell completion, a fresh search when R allows) and a choice there. A held hover (`parasite::held`, claim 17) only remembers the destination.
  - `econ::release::send_out` sets ρ = ½ after a factory exit; the hover leaves by its own locomotor towards (x₀ + 4, y₀ + 1).
  - `path::failed` clears a hover's destination like a driving unit's.
  - `occupancy::in_progress`, `path::heading_cell` and the claim 19 count see a hover's heading point and φ.
  - `scatter::vehicle_request` refuses a switched-off hover (claim 16 g) and centres on its heading point's cell.
  - `drive::release_claims` frees its flags at removal.
  - The parasite hold timer (`parasite::held`) is claim 17's hold test: a held assignment makes no choice, and a held choice refuses as *blocked* after clearing the old reservation.
- **Seams.** These are named functions with doc comments:
  - the fall (`hover::start_fall`, S-0511 claim 10);
  - the cloaked reveal (`hover::reveal_cloaked`, S-0516);
  - crate collection (`hover::collect_crate`, built with S-0520);
  - the chase cut (`hover::chase_cut`, S-0212 claim 20);
  - the stop order (`hover::stop_order`, claim 16: the heading point, T, φ and ρ are kept; *D* and the stored path are kept apart from the cleared order-level destination when *D* = *P*, `HoverState::retained`, and the retry timer is made eligible).
  - S-0312 claim 11's low-water death of a marked vehicle is not built.
- **Quirks.** `quirks.hover_brake_undershoot`, `quirks.hover_elevated_heading_point`, `quirks.hover_stalled_start_reset`, `quirks.hover_idle_arrival_reservation` and `quirks.hover_flag_clear_height_only` are implemented.
- **Rendering.** The map view draws every mobile object at its world position, z included (`mapview::live`, `build::height_lift`), so the hover height and the bob show with no new drawing code.
- **Own AI.**
  - Hovering vehicles are army (`scripted::plays`: any locomotor the simulation moves).
  - `Script::reachable` already sends a unit to its own reach region by its movement zone; the amphibious zone's region spans sea and land, so a hover's goal across water stays as ordered.
  - The fixture adds HOVR, a gun hovercraft with the hover class and locomotor and the amphibious zone, built at FACT once the house has a naval yard (NAVY) and a LAB, so only on maps with water; `builds_a_navy_on_a_water_map` checks that one is built.
  - `a_hover_crosses_the_sea_to_a_land_goal` (in `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs`) shows a hover on the sea of `fixture::map_naval` sent by the brain at an enemy beside its base, reaching land.
- **Save format 18.** `Object::hover` is new state; format 21 adds `HoverState::between` (claim 13), format 24 `HoverState::retained` (claim 16), format 25 `HoverState::held` (claim 18).
- **What play needs.** Data and map:
  - a vehicle type whose `Locomotor` is the hovering locomotor's identifier, with the hover or amphibious locomotion class and a movement zone (amphibious for a type that crosses water);
  - land rows whose hover or amphibious multipliers are not 0 on water and on land;
  - `[General]` `HoverHeight`, `HoverBob`, `HoverBoost`, `HoverAcceleration`, `HoverBrake` and `HoverDampen` (absent keys fall back to the binary64 numbers nearest the exact decimal defaults, claim Import mapping).

  A person builds the type at an ordinary (not naval) vehicle factory with `Order::Build`; it leaves the factory by itself. Then they move it by `targeting::click` on any cell of its reach region, water or land, and `orders::issue`. A hover whose control centre is lost (`powered_unit`) spins and settles, and over water it gets the bomb mark.

S-0503 revision 3 answers Q-0690 to Q-0696 (claims 5 and 13 to 16, F6, Import mapping); their interim rules are retired:
- absent hover keys and 2π and 1.1 are the binary64 numbers nearest the exact decimals, and ϱ wraps in signed 32 bits (F6);
- a hover is between steps from creation and each F5 arrival until its next positive advance with a heading point; a clear choice marks the point's cell at once, the first advance clears the actual position's flag, and crossings mark and clear only while between steps (claim 13, `HoverState::between`, save format 21);
- its current speed is ρ ≠ 0 (claim 14);
- a clear choice is a cell-arrival update before the step is used up, and an arrival whose choice leaves no point restores the flag and gives one, except a completion away from the contacted object (claim 15);
- a new hovering locomotor's η is 0, not the body facing (claim 15);
- only a powered hover not moving now chooses at once when given a move (claim 5);
- a stop order keeps the heading point, T, φ and ρ (claim 16).

S-0503 revision 4 answers Q-0675 (claims 5, 7, 13, 15 to 17, two new quirks); its interim rules are retired:
- the arrival runs the initial path update (completion, or a fresh search when R allows, whose failure clears the old reservation) before the choice; a choice is attempted when *D* remains or the contacted object is in the present cell (`hover::path_update`, `hover::choose_next`);
- every non-refusing choice that leaves no point, a deferred or failed search included, restores the actual-position flag and gives a cell-arrival update; an arrival with no choice keeps the old reservation (`quirks.hover_idle_arrival_reservation`; off: the actual position is marked instead, with no update);
- a flag is cleared in the deck by the point's height alone (`hover::clear_layer`, `quirks.hover_flag_clear_height_only`). Our cell records count flags per vehicle, so a clear in the other layer leaves this vehicle's flag standing, as a flag model would when no deck flag is set there;
- held is the parasite hold timer (claim 17), and a person's stop order keeps *D* equal to *P* apart from the order-level destination (claim 16); a search with only a kept *D* searches for its cell.

S-0503 revision 6 answers Q-0679 (claim 18): a cell assigned while held supplies its centre at terrain surface height, with no deck lift, as *D* (`HoverState::held`, `hover::take`). Expiry, searches and point choices leave it; another assignment, a stop, completion or failure clears it. The interim rule is retired.

## Simulation (M6b: transports)

S-0507 (transports) is built in `glasnost_sim::transport`. The type fields (`Passengers`, `Size`, `SizeLimit`, `OpenTopped`, `Gunner`, `TurretCount`, `IsTrain`, `IsDropship`, `Carryall`, and the two transport sounds) are imported by `glasnost_convert`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0507.md](../m6-claims/S-0507.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0507.rs`, `claims_s0507_water.rs` and `crates/convert/src/sim_transport_tests.rs`.

- **State.** Each object has a `TransportState` (`Object::transport`). On a transport it holds the passengers, position 1 first, and the kept count. On a passenger it holds the transport it rides. A vehicle transport's unload phase (table U) is the progress count of its *unload* behaviour.
- **Who may board (claims 1, 2 and 4, F1).**
  - `transport::answer` gives Accepts, Refuses or Neither.
  - A vehicle transport counts sizes, with the load truncated after each addition (`quirks.transport_load_truncated`); an air transport counts heads.
  - Neither is given to a candidate that is not allied, and to one at a building cell.
- **Boarding (claims 3, 5 and 6).**
  - `process_enter` runs the loading exchange each time *enter* is processed.
  - Arrival (`walk`, `drive` and `hover` call `transport::arrival`) boards the unit at position 1 and takes it off the map.
  - A closed transport's passengers leave the update order. An open-topped transport's passengers stay in it, follow its position (`transport::sync`) and take its target.
- **Unloading a vehicle transport (claims 7 to 12, F2, F3).** `process_unload` runs the phases of table U:
  1. settling, with a shore search on water;
  2. the unloading-cell score, then the turn;
  3. letting out from position 1, with a two-round exit search;
  4. the kept passenger of a turret count, then *guard*.
  
  `may_unload` refuses water, bridges, decks, building cells and tunnel cells (`transport::tunnel_cell`: a tunnel-marked cell or a tunnel edge, revision 4's Terms). Map tubes are not imported (`seams::tube_entrance`), so no cell is tunnel-marked yet. An infantry passenger takes S-0208 claim 3's centre-class choice at the serving neighbour; a vehicle passenger takes S-0212 claim 25's nearby search from it.
- **Air transports (claim 13).** `process_air_unload` runs revision 4's five phases (`transport::air_phase`) in the *unload* progress count: selection, approach, stopped check, release and reselect. Release puts position 1 at the aircraft's exact position, facing the first clear neighbour in the order 4, 5, 3, 7, 1, 0, 6, 2, and queues *move* toward it. `air::replacement_search`, `air::landing_test` and `air::cruise_height` expose S-0501 claim 40's pieces to it.
- **Deaths and removal.** `combat::death` and `air` apply S-0312 claims 5, 12 and 16: the passengers die with an exploding or crashing carrier, or get out of a destroyed vehicle, which is left out of their entry tests (list entry and vehicle-present flag). `objects::remove` kills a removed transport's remaining passengers without attacker credit (S-0507 claim 16, `transport::remove_passengers`): a sale, an aircraft killed on the ground, a crashable vehicle on the ground and the end of a sinking.
- **Hooks.**
  - `combat::weapon` gives passengers their weapons (S-0308).
  - `air::passengers` is `transport::count` (S-0501, and the S-0301 empty-carrier answer).
  - `parasite::host_unloading` runs as a passenger is let out (S-0614 claim 12).
  - Hover transports (S-0503) and ship transports (S-0504) use the same code.
- **Seams.** These are named functions with doc comments:
  - mind control (`mind_controlled`, `controls_objects`, `release_mind_control`; S-0601);
  - the gattling spin (`reset_gattling_spin`, S-0609; built in M7a step 3.3, it resets the spin);
  - tubes (`in_tube`);
  - tank bunkers (`tank_bunker`, S-0508);
  - the bomb mark (`bomb_marked`, the special-abilities specs);
  - temporal beams (`temporal_hand_back`, S-0514).
  - A paratrooper leaves its plane through `transport::drop_out` (S-0522, in its own section below).
- **Quirks.** `quirks.air_unload_reselects_clear_destination`, `quirks.unload_facing_gap_no_wrap`, `quirks.unload_second_round_skips_rear`, `quirks.unload_failed_exit_gunner_handover` and `quirks.transport_load_truncated` are implemented, each on by default.
- **Own AI.**
  - The brain ferries idle army infantry in its own transports (`Brain::ferry`). It builds the cheapest suitable type if it has none, and needs an amphibious hover when the goal lies across water.
  - It loads infantry by clicking the transport, drives to land near the goal, and unloads with the deploy click. It uses only orders, through `targeting::click` and `orders::issue`.
  - The fixture adds TRUK (wheeled) and LIFT (amphibious hover), both for four with size limit 2, and `fixture::map_strait`. The test `ferries_infantry_across_a_strait` checks that infantry is let out on the far shore.
- **Gate.** Each sim-gate duel asserts at least one load and one unload. Since M7b AI round 2 the diagonal duel has its own seed, `DUEL_DIAGONAL_SEED` = 8: on `DUEL_SEED` house 0 won it at tick 19 270 with its first ferry loaded and not yet unloaded; seed 8 played to tick 26 867 with 18 loads and 18 unloads, and after the economy-defence change of the same round to tick 28 947, again with 18 and 18 (the straight duel now ends at tick 22 579 with 16 loads and 15 unloads). It saves and loads at the first tick with a passenger aboard.
- **Save format 20.** `Object::transport` is new state, and it is hashed.
- **Not built.** The boarding and leaving sounds are imported but not played. (The transport pips of S-0111 claims 9 to 12 are drawn since M8 step A3 intake 46.)
- **What play needs.** Data: a vehicle or aircraft type with `Passengers` above 0 and a `SizeLimit` at least the passengers' `Size`.

  To load, a person selects infantry (or a vehicle) and clicks a friendly transport that shows the enter cursor (`targeting::click`, then `orders::issue`). The unit walks to the transport and boards it.

  To unload, they select the transport and click it with the deploy cursor. A vehicle transport stops, turns its rear to the chosen cell and lets the passengers out one by one, last in first out.

  There is no deploy cursor with no passengers. A vehicle transport also has none on water, or on a bridge, a deck or a tunnel cell.

  An air transport lets its passengers out once it has landed, or once it has stopped in the air with no destination.

M6b transports have no provisional rules left. S-0507 revision 5 answers Q-0672 (claim 13: an air transport releases infantry above the surface and every vehicle at its exact position and height, with no standing-position choice, and the own cell must be clear for both kinds) and Q-0673 (an undefined waypoint 700 supplies cell (0, 0), the search still runs and its failure keeps that centre); their rows are retired. The Q-0700 to Q-0707 rows were retired before: S-0507 revision 4 answers Q-0700 (claim 10 placement), Q-0701 (claim 8 shore search), Q-0702 (claim 16 removal cleanup), Q-0703 (claim 9 alliance object), Q-0704 (the tunnel edge of Terms and claim 7), Q-0705 (claim 13 phases) and Q-0706 (claim 11 delay, already built), and S-0312 revision 26 answers Q-0707 (claim 12: the dying vehicle is left out of the escape entry tests, now with its vehicle-present flag as well).

## Simulation (M6b: bridges)

S-0511 (bridges) is built in `glasnost_sim::bridges` and `glasnost_sim::fall`. The theater's ten bridge positions and the general bridge fields (`BridgeExplosions`, `MetallicDebris`, the fall rates, `RepairBridgeSound`) are imported by `glasnost_convert`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0511.md](../m6-claims/S-0511.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0511.rs`.

- **Attempts (claims 1 and 2).** Every destroy attempt goes through `bridges::attempt`, which picks a low strip, a section or a pier. Its sources are:
  - area damage (`bridges::area_attempts`, from `combat::area::detonate`), gated by the bridge-destruction setting and the warhead's wall flag;
  - the trigger-action seam `bridges::cell_attempts` (actions 32 and 63 are not built);
  - hut demolition.
- **High bridges (claims 3 to 8 and 12).** `bridges::high` holds:
  - section states (the overlay data), notices, losses and pier states (the tile);
  - the span check, with its band raises of event 31 (`triggers::bridge_destroyed`);
  - the link change of the named cell (`high::name_cell`).

  A collapsed pier's earlier levels are kept in `Cells::pier_levels`. The ramps' alternate look is `Cells::alternate`.
- **Blow-ups and falls (claims 9 to 11).** `bridges::blow` does three things:
  - it kills the ground layer with the demolition warhead;
  - it drops the deck layer into a fall with the bomb mark (`fall::start`);
  - it takes F2's explosion draws.

  After an attempt, units heading onto a lost deck are hit. `tick` runs `fall::turn` in place of the locomotor step while an object falls.
- **Low bridges (claims 13 to 15).** `bridges::low` holds the strip attempts, the neighbour re-evaluation and the kill check.
- **The hut (claims 16 to 21).** `bridges::hut` holds:
  - `demolish`, called at the delayed-kill expiry in `combat::damage`;
  - `repair`, with F4 on the map-generator stream `World::map_rng`;
  - `repair_offered`, the cursor test of S-0214 row I3 in `targeting`.
- **Seams.** These are named functions with doc comments:
  - `bridges::hut::engineer_entered_hut`, an engineer entering a hut (S-0509 is not built);
  - `bridges::cell_attempts`, trigger actions 32 and 63;
  - `bridges::hut::demolish`, also for timed bombs (S-0519).
- **Quirks.** All ten are implemented, each on by default:
  - `quirks.bridge_cursor_unrelated_link`;
  - `quirks.bridge_event_band_short`;
  - `quirks.bridge_family_by_block`;
  - `quirks.bridge_span_cap`;
  - `quirks.collapsed_pier_span_search`;
  - `quirks.low_bridge_wrong_axis_recheck`;
  - `quirks.low_strip_regress`;
  - `quirks.pier_loss_notice_skips_chipped`;
  - `quirks.pier_repair_partial_height`;
  - `quirks.repair_consumes_engineer_always`;
  - `quirks.repair_walk_unbounded`.
- **Save format 21.** `Object::fall`, `Cells::alternate`, `Cells::pier_levels` and `World::map_rng` are new state, and they are hashed.
- **Save format 24.** `Cells::section_self` and `Cells::covered_by` (claim 2's responsible section) are new state, and they are hashed.
- **Save format 25.** `Track::height` (S-0207 claim 24, the heading point's stored height) is new state, and it is hashed. A landing keeps the bomb mark (claim 10).
- **Save format 28.** `DriveState::goal` and `DriveState::for_dest` (S-0207 claim 25, the locomotor destination point) are new state, and they are hashed. `superweapons::Protection::tint` (S-0513 claim 29, the envelope phase up to its draw) replaces `tint_due`.
- **Not built.**
  - The own AI does not repair bridges, because it needs S-0509's entry.
  - The bridge-repaired and repair sounds are effects, but audio does not play them.
  - Row IC2 (fog of war) is not built.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=bridge-destroyed,bridge-repaired cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` picks the first offered map, by content, that has a hut within 3 cells of a high bridge. It demolishes that bridge through `hut::demolish` and writes `local-lane/m6b/bridge-destroyed.png` 30 ticks later. Then an engineer enters the hut through the seam, and it writes `bridge-repaired.png` 10 ticks later. On the first run on the user's install:
  - the demolition left 22 of 30 high-bridge sections and 3 of 4 links enabled, with repair offered;
  - the repair brought back 30 sections and 4 enabled links, with no repair offered;
  - the first shot shows 40 animations created and 39 drawn.

  The screenshots show the deck gone over the water with its explosions, then the deck whole again.

Provisional rules of M6b bridges, each tied to a request:

None at present.

S-0207 revision 27 answers Q-0793 (claim 24, S-0417 claim 5, S-0511 claims 9 and 11). The factory exit track's heading point stores world height 0 (`drive::begin_exit_track`). The successful exit instruction also sets the locomotor destination point to the apron centre at height 0. S-0511 claim 9's reservation clearing and claim 11's stored-height test therefore use 0, while claim 11's track test still uses the current height. Its row is retired.

S-0511 revision 5 with S-0207 revision 26 answers Q-0676 (claim 11 and S-0207 claim 24: a track's heading point keeps the height at its start, `Track::height`), Q-0677 (claim 10: the bomb mark is kept on landing) and Q-0678 (claim 7: a collapsed pier never qualifies as a start piece and, with `quirks.collapsed_pier_span_search`, not as a far piece either); their rows are retired.

S-0511 revision 4 answers Q-0710 and Q-0712 to Q-0718, and S-0511 claim 22 with S-0202 claim 13 answer Q-0719 and Q-0711; their rows are retired.

- **Map-generator stream (S-0202 claim 13, Q-0711).** `map_rng` starts every game at seed 0, independently of the game seed. A game is a fresh session in our engine; the stream state an earlier game of the same session would leave is not carried over (a seam).
- **Centre-cell target loss (claim 22, Q-0719).** `bridges::drop_cell_targets`: a mobile object whose attack target is the centre cell reverts a suspended behaviour first (restoring target and destination), then a still-equal target is cleared; an aircraft in *attack* restarts its run stage (`air::restart_run`); projectiles aimed at the cell lose their target. Destinations naming the cell stay.
- **Drops (claim 9, Q-0712 and Q-0713).** A drop keeps target, destination, queued destinations, path and movement state. Its marks move at once (`bridges::blow::move_marks`): a stationary unit's standing-position mark or present flag goes from the deck record to the ground record; a moving one's reservation at its heading point is cleared at that point's height. The deferred transfer is gone.
- **Falling (claim 10, Q-0713; S-0301 revision 14).** The fall step and any landing hit run before the behaviour processing in `tick::object_turn`. A unit still falling after it does not advance and cannot fire: the firing check answers *illegal* at rank 4 for a falling firer. A surviving landing lifts both in the same turn.
- **Heading hits (claims 11 and 15, Q-0718).** `bridges::blow::heads_to` compares movement points with P, the cell's centre at floor + 416 (claim 11) or at its floor (claim 15). Walk and hover use their heading point's cell and stored height within 104 leptons; drive and ship use their current track's end cell with the track's stored start height (S-0207 claim 24) or the unit's height. An object destination is not excluded, and a destination alone is not a heading point.
- **Kill check (claim 15, Q-0714).** "Could not enter" is the unit's own-kind entry outcome of S-0209 claims 4 and 5 being *blocked* (`bridges::low::blocked`), occupants and overlays included.
- **Responsible section (claim 2, Q-0717; S-0211 revision 17, claim 15 (a)).** `Cells::mark_high_bridge` records history per cell: `section_self` when an overlay marks the cell as its own section cell, `covered_by` for the last overlay that marked it as a covered cell. `Cells::responsible` gives the cell itself, else the last coverer; nothing searches nearby overlays. An attempt on a high-bridge cell (`bridges::qualifying_section`) takes the bridge family first, then the wooden one, by the responsible overlay or a non-collapsed pier tile of the family at the attempted cell. With a pier supplying the family over a replaced overlay, claim 3 acts on the responsible cell's retained state (`pieces::state_in_table`), and a state outside the table changes nothing. A deck cell with no responsible section writes a note and does nothing. The claim 1 high test uses the same responsibility.
- **Pier tiles and spans (Q-0710).** A pier's state replacement acts on the eight-neighbour-connected component of cells with the reference cell's exact pre-change tile number (`pieces::pier_component`); a collapse lowers exactly those cells. A ramp's alternate look keeps the placed tile's rectangle (`pieces::tile_cells`). A position in both pier ranges reads x states 0 to 3, then y states 0 to 3, then the collapsed states (`pieces::piece_of_q`); the span searches and repair read the pier along their own axis (`pieces::pier_along`). `high::span_check` starts from a named loss cell: the first neighbour in N to NW order with a deck or body mark, its responsible or body-rule section (`pieces::section_of`, shared with the hut), then the nearest start ramp or non-collapsed pier toward the start side up to the map edge. A direct pier collapse names its start-side neighbour, then its far-side neighbour. A collapsed pier never qualifies as a start piece, and with `quirks.collapsed_pier_span_search` the far-end search passes over it too, so both checks select the combined outer-ramp-to-outer-ramp extent (claim 7, S-0511 revision 5).
- **Hut and cursor (Q-0715, Q-0716).** The hut cell is the cell of the hut's position, its top-left foundation cell (Terms), and the engineer's own cell is the repair's start (claim 18), as `bridges::hut` already did. The repair cursor (`repair_offered`, claim 21) takes the last qualifying cell of the hut's 5 × 5 block, in increasing y and then x: a tile of either bridge role, otherwise a low piece. A low candidate decides by its own row's run. A high candidate's role position picks a probe and a direction from the claim's table, and the test follows enabled links whose end is the traversal cell until it finds a disabled link (repair), no link (none) or, with `quirks.bridge_cursor_unrelated_link`, an enabled link with neither end there (repair). A repeated traversal cell refuses repair; the warning the Edge cases ask for is not written, because the cursor test is a read-only query.

## Simulation (M6b: cloak and stealth)

S-0516 (cloak and stealth) is built in `glasnost_sim::cloak`. The general cloak fields, the per-type cloak, sensor, detector and disguise keys, and the weapon and warhead keys are imported by `glasnost_convert`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0516.md](../m6-claims/S-0516.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0516_*.rs`, on the invented rules of `tests/cloak_common/mod.rs`.

- **The machine (claims 1 to 18 and 41 to 43).** `cloak::machine` holds the four states and their stages. `machine::turn` is the ordinary cloak update (claim 42): a mobile object's runs in `tick::object_turn` after its due behaviour processing and before locomotion and the separate firing; a building's runs at the end of its turn, after its behaviour. `machine::late_turn` (aircraft target checks, disguise) follows at the end of every object's turn. `machine::uncloak` is the one call of every uncloak cause. Hooks:
  - movement for the cells entered; the cell-arrival update (`machine::cell_movement_completed`, the sensor disc move, even within the same cell, and the adjacent-sensor reveal) comes from the locomotor by claim 22's table: `drive` at a track target, `walk` at an arrival and snap, `hover` at S-0503 claim 15's non-refusing choices, `teleport::jump` in the jump tick (after a vehicle's arrival reporting and crushing, before the entered-by raise), the superweapon warp at the member's arrival-effects update (`superweapons::warp::arrive`, after the fate test, for survivors only; its removal and appearance leave the disc at the old recorded cell), `fall::turn` at a completed landing (after falling ends, before the parachute is detached); ordinary flight and rocket flight have none (`machine::flight_cell_crossed`, `quirks.flying_sensor_disc_stationary`); a jumpjet's touchdown, underground emergence, mechanical walking and drop-pod descent are seams of unbuilt locomotors, and entering play (unloading included) adds the disc with no adjacency test;
  - `combat::damage` for hits, `combat::fire` for firing, `crush` for crushing;
  - `econ::events` for entering and leaving the map and owner changes.

  The delay `CloakDelay` is read in single precision (F3), so the test rules' delay of 0.01 minutes is 8 ticks, not 9. An underwater vessel is submerged while it is not uncloaked (`naval::submerged`, claim 41).
- **Coverage (claims 19 to 23 and 40).** `cloak::field` grows and shrinks cloak fields ring by ring per house, in increasing Y then X, stopping at own building centres, the generator's own included (`quirks.cloak_field_building_interrupt`). Each generator records whether growth or shrinking is pending (`cloak::FieldPhase`) and whether it was in operation at its last turn (`Generator::was_on`): a new generator is idle at radius 0, and the turn that detects a change in operation applies claim 20's table (`field::turn`; staying in or out of operation requests nothing, pending growth has no power gate). An accepted manual power-off makes shrinking pending even at radius 0, the radius becoming the type radius first (`field::switched_off`, called from `econ::power::switch`; `quirks.cloak_generator_zero_radius_shutdown`). Only an idle one in operation, at any radius, receives a neighbour restart. Removal and capture are immediate collapses that run the neighbour-restart test, also for a type radius of 0 (strict reach 16 square cells); a captured generator grows for its new owner only if in operation; `cloak::sensor` keeps per-house, per-cell sensor and disguise-detector counts (`World::cloak`).
- **Hidden objects (claims 24 to 29).** `cloak::hidden_from` is the game-state test, used by `combat::check`, `combat::acquire`, `air` and `targeting::selectable_by`. `cloak::view` holds the presentation queries (`look`, `shadow`, `pickable`, `deselects`, `on_radar`). They take a viewing house and are never read by the simulation.
- **Disguise (claims 30 to 40).** `cloak::disguise` holds the disguise state (`Object::disguise`), disguising by a hit, permanent disguise, disguise when still, loss, and `appears_disguised`. `appears_disguised` feeds targeting, threat, the walker rows of S-0209 and the radar colour (`econ::minimap`).
- **App, audio and AI.**
  - The live view draws through the local player's eyes (`mapview::live`, `set_viewer`, `apply_cloak`). The "rippling" look is drawn as 50 % translucent.
  - Cloak events take objects out of the selection (claim 26).
  - Audio plays the game-wide cloak sound (claim 15).
  - The own AI does not see a cloaked enemy it does not sense.
- **Seams.** These are named functions with doc comments in `cloak/mod.rs`:
  - C4 planting (S-0519) and the special-weapon causes call `machine::uncloak`;
  - cloaking crates (S-0520, built) call `machine::grant_own_flag`;
  - undeploying (S-0517) calls `disguise::clear`;
  - a house leaving (S-0414) calls `disguise::house_left`;
  - the crush response to a hit (S-0210 claim 11, S-0215 claim 39; `crush::respond_to_hit`) reads `appears_disguised`;
  - seizing (S-0607), paralysis (S-0214), the chrono warp (S-0514), infiltration (S-0510), mind control (S-0515), and bunkers and occupied buildings (S-0508, S-0509).
- **Quirks.** All fourteen are implemented, each on by default:
  - `quirks.building_sensor_ring_owner_only`;
  - `quirks.cloak_field_building_interrupt`;
  - `quirks.cloak_field_overlap_loss`;
  - `quirks.cloak_flag_ground_mobile_only`;
  - `quirks.cloak_generator_zero_radius_shutdown`;
  - `quirks.flying_sensor_disc_stationary`;
  - `quirks.cloak_zero_speed_stall`;
  - `quirks.heal_reveals_cloaked`;
  - `quirks.mirage_adjacent_check_cadence`;
  - `quirks.mobile_sensor_removal_centre`;
  - `quirks.sensor_array_removal_radius`;
  - `quirks.sensor_building_activation_once`;
  - `quirks.sensor_count_negative`;
  - `quirks.uncloak_from_cloaking_full_duration`.
- **Save format 22.** `Object::cloak`, `Object::disguise` and `World::cloak` are new state, and they are hashed. Format 28 adds `cloak::Generator::phase` (S-0516 claim 20: growth or shrinking pending). Revision 8 adds `cloak::Generator::was_on` (`serde(default)`; the format number is not yet bumped).
- **Not built.**
  - The app does not draw the disguise image (claim 37), and the cursor, insignia and hover name of claim 36 are not wired into the status drawing.
  - Buildings drawn as static map scenery get no translucency.
  - The importer does not warn about an empty mirage disguise list.
  - The importer warns about a `CloakingStages` of 0 or below (imported as 1) and a negative `CloakRadiusInCells` (no field), claims 20 and 43.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=cloaked-as-owner,cloaked-as-enemy cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` places a cloaking vehicle near the local start, with a plain vehicle beside it. It uses a driving vehicle type with the cloak flag if the ruleset has one, else it gives a plain vehicle its own flag through the crate seam.
  - `cloaked-as-owner.png` is the local house's own vehicle, fully cloaked after 36 ticks. It is drawn 50 % translucent.
  - For an enemy house's vehicle, the lane uncloaks it and waits for the fade back. `cloaking-as-enemy.png` shows it translucent mid-fade, 21 ticks later. `cloaked-as-enemy.png` shows it fully cloaked 14 ticks after that, when it is no longer drawn.

  On the first run on the user's install, the scenes took 10 s in all.

S-0516 revision 8 answers Q-0795 and Q-0796, and their rows are retired. `cloak::machine::arrival_source` is gone: no cell-arrival update comes from the cloak turn any more; each source above calls `cell_movement_completed` itself. Unbuilt locomotors (S-0502's jumpjet touchdown and the other modes) are seams. The generator's phases follow claim 20's table, edge-triggered on the recorded operation state.

S-0516 revision 7 answers Q-0791 (claim 22's table of cell-arrival updates by locomotor, `quirks.flying_sensor_disc_stationary`, same-cell relocation) and Q-0792 (own-centre interruption on the first radius-1 update; idle means no growth or shrinking pending, whatever the radius). Their rows are retired. It also lets uncloaking at stage 0 complete with speed 0 (`quirks.cloak_zero_speed_stall` stalls only a positive stage).

## Simulation (M6b: superweapons)

S-0512 (charge and readiness) and the S-0513 effects of the Allied and Soviet kinds are built in `glasnost_sim::superweapons`. The kinds are the nuclear strike, the weather storm, invulnerability (iron curtain), the teleport (chronosphere), the force shield, the paradrop and the spy plane. The ledgers are [docs/m6-claims/S-0512.md](../m6-claims/S-0512.md) and [docs/m6-claims/S-0513.md](../m6-claims/S-0513.md); the revision 7 and 10 changes are classified in [docs/m6-claims/step2-sw.md](../m6-claims/step2-sw.md). The tests are `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0512.rs`, `claims_s0512_words.rs` and `claims_s0513_{nuke,storm,protect,warp,planes}.rs`, on the invented rules of `tests/sw_common/mod.rs`.

- **Words (S-0512 claims 23 and 24).** The twelve kind and link words and the aim-action vocabulary are `glasnost_rules::sim_econ::SUPERWEAPON_KIND_WORDS` and `SUPERWEAPON_ACTION_WORDS`. The importer (`convert::sim_econ`) keeps the previous layer's kind or link for a word outside the vocabulary and keeps the previous action for an empty one; a nonempty word outside the action vocabulary becomes the explicit word `none`. An icon with no aim action fires at cell (0, 0) without aiming (`Play::cameo_click`).
- **Records and charge (S-0512).** `superweapons::charge` holds one `Record` per house and type in `World::sw`. Grants, suspension, loss, the one-time and permanent trigger grants, set and reset charge, drain and manual modes, the flash window and the countdown rows all run in the house update (`charge::house_update`, `charge::after_tech`). A fire order (`orders::Order::FireSuperweapon`) is carried out by `launch::fire`, which consumes the charge and dispatches the kind's effect.
- **Nuclear strike (claims 7 to 13).** `superweapons::nuke`. The silo is the building owned longest. It launches an upward missile on its next update and stays busy until guard would be queued, 8 ticks after it accepted the launch (claim 47); the missile-maker burst creates the falling payload (F8). A one-time record drops the payload from 20 000 leptons (F2). The warning marker is an animation. The payload leaves radiation like any radiation-weapon detonation (`radiation::detonation`, M6c: radiation fields).
- **Weather storm (claims 14 to 21).** `superweapons::storm`. The storm states of T1 run in the weather update at row 3 of the tick pipeline, before the turn pass. It covers the countdown and warnings, the radar outage of the other houses, the centre and scatter clouds (F5, F6), strikes with the bolt, area damage and debris, and clearing. A cloud plays as an ordinary forward one-pass animation (claim 46): its stage delay comes from the creation draw, a constant random-rate delay or the `Rate` field, and it is tested against the frame count of its image and the canvas height of the first bolt image, both imported from the sprites (`AnimationType::frame_count`, `canvas_height`, `rate`, `normalized`). Playback fields beyond the rate (start, end, loops, reverse, ping-pong, shadow, follow-up) are a seam (S-0109 claim 28). The strike witness (claim 18) is the ground-listed candidate nearest the cell's top corner. Trigger actions 44 and 45 start and stop a storm; action 109 is the waypoint curtain.
- **Invulnerability and the force shield (claims 22 to 32).** `superweapons::protect`. The protection timer is `Object::sw.protection` (F7). Its consumers are the hit table, the small-detonation nullification, the firing answers, crushing and the occupancy row. The curtain releases a parasite through `parasite::host_curtain_started`. The force shield blacks out the launching house and shields allied buildings within F3's radius. PLAN.md lists the force shield under M7; it is built here because S-0513 specifies it with the others.
- **Teleport (claims 33 to 41).** `superweapons::warp`. A source pick emits the player-local marker event. The warp walks the source block (F1, claim 4). For each examined object it first releases a naval parasite with 500 ticks of suppression (`parasite::superweapon_teleport`). It then kills organic objects (claim 35), leaves the excluded ones in place, and makes the rest members (`Object::sw.warp`). Each member's stages (T2) run at the start of its own living turn (`warp::turn`, from `tick::object_turn`): leaving, the 60-tick wait, departure, the landing check and redirect, placement, arrival fate, and the arrival hold. `teleport::phasing_in` and `being_erased` read the member's stage. The departure does not release a drone parasite (S-0614 claim 12, P7), so it does not use `objects::take_inside`. The chrono weapons of S-0514 are the seam `warp::chrono_seam`.
- **Paradrop and spy plane (claims 42 to 45; S-0522 claims 1 to 3).** `superweapons::planes`. Planes are sent from the house's edge cell. Paratroopers board the plane. The passes and the drop are S-0522, in the section below. The spy plane's camera reveals through `econ::shroud::camera` in its approach and overfly.
- **Quirks.** Every switch of S-0512 and S-0513 is implemented and on by default.
- **Save format 26 (changed since by step 2).** The records, the storm, the protection timers, the warp members and the held delay are new state, and they are hashed. Step 2 adds the silo's busy-until tick (`ObjSw::silo_busy_until`) and changes the meaning of `Cloud::delay` (the effective stage delay); the format number is not bumped here. `warp_state_survives_save_and_load` saves mid-warp and checks the hash.
- **Own AI.** The brain (`glasnost_ai::brain`) builds, one after the other, the cheapest building it can build and place that gives a kind of superweapon it has no building for yet (M6c step 2b: every Allied and Soviet kind, that is the nuclear strike, the storm, the iron curtain, the teleport, the force shield, the spy plane and the two paradrops; the Yuri kinds stay unbuilt, `launch::yuri_seam`). The kinds come from the buildings' superweapon fields and the types' kind words in the imported rules (`Brain::giving_kinds`, `fired_kind`), no building name is in the code. It does so once its harvesters are kept and while it is not selling out. A giving building that the tech tree still blocks is reached through the building that clears the blocker (the tech climb's `unblocker`, depth `CLIMB_DEPTH`), as a person climbs to a superweapon, and the cheapest of the buildable giving buildings and these clearing buildings is built next. The teleport's first stage wants two armed vehicles in a block (the curtain still three, `TELEPORT_MIN`). What the local lane showed (`ai-sides` on map 8, seed 7, one game per pair of sides, counts in `local-lane/m6c/ai-sides-map8-b.txt`): the paradrop (American), the spy plane and the teleport were built and launched (7, 5 and 1 launches in one game), the force shield was built in all three games and ready for 16 000 to 57 000 house-ticks with one launch in one game (its cell needs two own buildings in a block with a seen enemy near and nothing of anyone else, which a house that wins away from home rarely has), the missile, the storm and the curtain were never built in these games: both houses of a game have near zero credits for most of the game (three army lines spend them), so a costly building waits; two houses had a placeable giving building and no credits. Not changed: the AI does not hold back its army to save for a superweapon. This build comes before the tech climb but after a building the plan wants for power, a refinery or a factory. Each tick, it fires every granted, ready and not suspended missile or storm record. It fires through `Order::FireSuperweapon`, at the seen enemy cell with the most seen enemies within 3 cells and none of its own objects (`Brain::strike_cell`). A storm is fired only while none is under way. The other kinds (M6b2, `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai_sw.rs`; the building of each is built since M6c, `ai_sw_build.rs`) are fired when ready and the house owns the giving building: an iron curtain on the block (a cell and its eight neighbours, S-0513 claim 4) with the most of its armed non-organic vehicles, at least 3, with a seen enemy within 10 cells and none of its infantry (claim 23) and nothing of anyone else in the block (`Brain::curtain_cell`); a force shield on the block with the most of its buildings, at least 2, with a seen enemy within 12 cells and nothing of anyone else in it (`shield_cell`); a spy plane at the cell the next wave would attack; the teleport's first stage on a vehicle block like the curtain's but with no fight needed, only while no source is held, and its second stage at the first cell 4 to 9 cells around the enemy base whose whole block is free usable land (`group_cell`, `landing_cell`). The numbers are tuning of our own opponent. Tests: `fires_a_ready_superweapon_at_seen_enemies` (debug) and `builds_and_fires_a_superweapon` (release) in `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs`; `curtains_a_fighting_group_of_vehicles`, `shields_buildings_with_an_enemy_at_them`, `sends_the_spy_plane_at_the_enemy` and `teleports_a_group_to_the_enemy_base` in `ai_sw.rs`.
- **Fixture and gate.** The synthetic skirmish (`glasnost_ai::fixture`) adds NUKE (the missile kind, charge 1 minute = 900 ticks), its silo SILO (needs LAB), the upward weapon LIFT and the falling NukePayload. Every gate duel saves and loads at the first tick with a granted record still charging. The straight and the diagonal duels must launch a superweapon (`SwEvent::Launch`). The naval duel is short of credits and never reaches LAB, so it neither charges nor launches, and it still does not fly a rocket. Its `flies` flag stays off.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=sw-nuke,sw-storm,sw-iron-curtain,sw-chronosphere,sw-force-shield,sw-paradrop-plane,sw-spy-plane cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` readies the local house's first type of each kind. It places the building that gives the type, with power plants until the house has enough power, sets a full charge and aims through the client (`Tool::Superweapon`, then a left click). It writes `local-lane/m6b/sw-*.png`. On the run after merging main, on the user's install (map 0):
  - nuclear strike: the launch event came 1 tick after the order, and the strike 399 ticks after it; it damaged all three enemy vehicles and destroyed none;
  - weather storm: the storm started 251 ticks after the order, with the first lightning at 285 ticks;
  - iron curtain: 3 of 3 vehicles were protected 1 tick after the order;
  - teleport: the source pick armed list position 4 (S-0513 claim 33), and the destination click warped 3 of 3 vehicles, which stood at the destination 66 ticks later;
  - force shield: the shield started 1 tick after the order, aimed at an own building;
  - paradrop and spy plane: no type found (the `paratroopers-dropping` scene of S-0522 sends the plane without a type). The types at list positions 4, 5, 6, 8 and 9 (5 of 12) had a kind word that the interim words did not recognise. Revision 7 of S-0512 (claim 24) gives the words, so these types resolve now; the lane has not been run since, and its fallback through `planes` stays in case a type is not found.

  The screenshots show the explosion, a bolt under the clouds, the curtain's invoke animation, the group landed with the source markers fading, and the shielded building. The iron-curtain and force-shield shots have no tint (not built: S-0513 claim 29's brightness phases and F9, S-0102 claim 34).
- **Not built.**
  - The Yuri kinds (S-0610, S-0611, S-0612) are `launch::yuri_seam`.
  - Tank bunker links (S-0606) do not exist, so the warp has none to end.

Provisional rules of M6b superweapons, each tied to a request: none are open (Q-0870 and Q-0871 answered by S-0513 revision 14).

S-0203 revision 32, S-0301 revision 18 and S-0215 revision 48 (with S-0513 revision 14 claim 51) answer Q-0870 (step 6a, [docs/m6-claims/step6a.md](../m6-claims/step6a.md)): the warp update is the whole member turn from the first pending update through arrival effects (`tick::object_turn`), the reload countdown still elapses (`combat::fire::reload_elapsed`), and the expected-health bookkeeping and the protection envelope's phases are ordinary work that resumes after the hold-end test. Its row is retired.

S-0512 revision 8 and S-0513 revision 11 (step 3, [docs/m6-claims/step3-sw.md](../m6-claims/step3-sw.md)) answer Q-0738 (claim 48) and Q-0741 (claims 33 and 49); the rows above keep only what those claims mark unmeasured, under the new requests Q-0850 and Q-0851. S-0513 revision 12 answers Q-0800 (step 4, [docs/m6-claims/step4.md](../m6-claims/step4.md)): `storm::cell_state` measures a building witness at its foundation centre (`World::reference_point`, remainders modulo 256 compared with (0, 0)) and a building is listed in every actual foundation cell; its row is retired. Q-0850 and Q-0851 stayed open until revision 13 (step 5, [docs/m6-claims/step5.md](../m6-claims/step5.md)), whose claims 50 and 51 are the engine rules `warp::*` now builds: the fallback cell (0, 0) for an off-map destination and a failed search, an illegal landing cell is blocked and never placed, a refused placement returns the member to the landing retry (alive, no cap, no timeout), the first fallback and the first refusal per member and warp are reported as world notes, launch clears the path, the queued destinations and the walking, driving and hover steps, and target and destination are dropped in the first update. Their interim rows are retired; Q-0870 and Q-0871 are new.

S-0512 revision 7 answers Q-0730, Q-0731 and Q-0737, and S-0513 revision 10 answers Q-0732, Q-0734, Q-0735, Q-0739 and Q-0740 (the lead closes the request files); their interim rows are gone. Revision 10 also answers Q-0733 fully (claim 29 for buildings, the force shield and missed turns) and part 1 of Q-0741 (claim 33: the source marker takes its creation draws on every pick).

S-0513 revision 9 answers Q-0733 (claim 29): the draw R(−5, 5) is taken on entering Initial dimming, in the turn *q* + 10 for an object with uninterrupted turns, *q* being its first turn while this application protects it. A launch applied after the turn pass in tick *t*₀ therefore draws in tick *t*₀ + 11. `protect::tint_turn` advances `Protection::tint` (before the first turn, Initial rise of 6 ticks, Bright hold of 4 ticks) by at most one phase per turn and takes the draw on entering Initial dimming. The later phases, their brightness factors and F9 are presentation and not built. The request file still reads open; the lead closes it. Tests: `claim29_tint_draw_in_turn_q_plus_ten`, `claim29_launch_after_the_turn_pass_draws_at_t0_plus_eleven`, `claim29_reapplication_restarts_the_envelope`.

## Simulation (M6b: parachutes and drops)

S-0522 is built in `glasnost_sim::paradrop`, on the plane sending of S-0513 (`superweapons::planes`). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0522.md](../m6-claims/S-0522.md). The tests are `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0522.rs`, with the launch side in `claims_s0513_planes.rs` and the arrival edge in `claims_s0501.rs`.

- **Passes (claims 6 to 8).** `AirState` holds the pass counter (`passes`, 5 at sending and after each successful drop) and the paradrop flag. `air::process` dispatches *paradrop approach* to `paradrop::approach` (every 3 ticks) and *paradrop overfly* to `paradrop::overfly` (every 5 ticks). A plane that gives up retreats to its edge; there its paratroopers are removed silently before the plane (claim 8).
- **Drop attempt (claims 9 and 10).** `paradrop::attempt` takes one round, picks the side by the ammunition's parity, and steps 128 leptons sideways to the drop point. The entry outcome is tested first and takes no draw; then the standing position is selected (its draw), then the usable area, the deck-entry mark and the nearby-cell qualification. On success, `transport::drop_out` places the paratrooper at the plane's height, facing 32768, on the deck layer when it is at or above the deck height over a bridge cell and on the ground layer below it. The fall starts, the parachute sequence is requested, and the parachute animation's draws are taken. The `SwEvent::Parachute` and `SwEvent::Chute` events are emitted. On failure, the round is given back.
- **Shot drop (claim 11).** In `combat::fire::shot`, an aircraft carrying passengers makes a drop attempt in place of the whole ordinary shot: no delay or draw, burst advance, report, muzzle animation, spray draw or projectile. A successful drop expires the rearm countdown now; a refused one changes nothing, and both report a shot, so the run's pending round is made.
- **Fall and parachute (claims 12 and 13).** `FallState::chute` marks an attached parachute. While it is set, `fall` uses the parachute recurrence, and the landing surface is the deck's for deck-layer objects. The chute is cleared on landing. `infantry` refuses other sequences while a paratrooper falls with its parachute sequence, and requests stand once it has landed (S-0107 claim 6). For a held parachute type both settings of `quirks.paratrooper_any_animation_slow_fall` give the same result, because the parachute is the only animation the simulation attaches. With no type held, the setting on gives the parachute recurrence once in the release-tick update (`FallState::chute_once`) and then the no-parachute one; off gives the no-parachute one from the first update. A name the rules give that no type lists was created as an animation type by the importer and is attached normally.
- **Losses (claims 14 and 15).** A paratrooper killed in descent is removed like any infantry. A shot-down plane kills only the passengers still aboard.
- **Drawing.** The app attaches the parachute animation to its paratrooper on the `SwEvent::Parachute` event (`Live::attach_parachute`, S-0109 claim 22). The animation follows the unit in the ground layer, ordered by the ground sort key plus its sort adjustment, with the carrier's house-colour unit palette and the cell light sampled at attachment (claim 18), and disappears with the unit. When the unit lands, its current pass becomes its last (`Live::follow_owners`). The chute sound event is not played, because the app plays no superweapon sounds yet.
- **Revision 5 (step 3, [docs/m6-claims/step3-sw.md](../m6-claims/step3-sw.md)).** A falling infantry unit is not ready to change (S-0215 claim 11, `process::ready`), so a fresh paratrooper's queued rest starts only after landing and its due processing in the air chooses 450 ticks (S-0215 claim 4, scenario B44). The release adds the sensor disc at once and the landing is a cell-arrival update (already built, tested in `claims_s0522.rs`). The S-0301 revision 17 drop-attempt rules (no ordinary delay, burst step or draw; a round spent at once; not a void shot) are tested there too.
- **Quirks.** `quirks.paradrop_fallback_point`, `quirks.paradrop_west_edge_start_row` and `quirks.paratrooper_any_animation_slow_fall` are implemented, each on by default. No switch is new.
- **Save format 27 (changed since by step 2).** The pass counter, the paradrop flag and the chute mark are new state, and they are hashed. Step 2 adds the one-update chute mark (`FallState::chute_once`); the format number is not bumped here. `save_load_mid_descent_round_trips` saves mid-descent and checks the hash.
- **Own AI.** The brain fires every ready paradrop or American paradrop record at a cell 3 to 8 cells around its home: the first usable cell with no object on it (`Brain::drop_cell`). Infantry types listed in a paradrop payload count as army when they are armed (`scripted::of_role`), so landed paratroopers join the army. Test: `fires_a_paradrop_whose_troops_join_the_army` in `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs`. The brain does not build a building for a paradrop.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=paratroopers-dropping cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` sends a plane to a cell near home. No type had a paradrop kind on the user's install (Q-0730), so the scene sent it straight through `planes::paradrop`. It writes `local-lane/m6b/paratroopers-dropping.png` and, 60 ticks later, `paratroopers-descending.png`. On the user's install (map 0):
  - one plane was sent, and the first drop came 398 ticks after the order;
  - 3 paratroopers were descending at 433 ticks, and 4 at 493;
  - 6 were dropped, and all 6 stood on the ground 1055 ticks after the order;
  - at both shots, every parachute animation had art and was drawn.

  The second screenshot shows four paratroopers under their parachutes and the plane leaving.
- **Seams.**
  - Parachute reinforcements from triggers (S-0011 claim 30) are not built.
  - The not-human-type exception of claim 14 keeps S-0107 claim 17's open outcome.
  - The simulation has no animation clock (S-0109 claim 28), so the chute mark lasts until landing.
  - The falling-damage multiplier is not applied by `fall`; the magnetic-lift impact applies it in `lift` (S-0607 F2), for lifted objects only.
  - Once landed, a paratrooper is ordinary infantry, so the M6c infantry features (garrisons S-0508, capture S-0509, infiltration S-0510, deployers S-0517) need nothing from `paradrop`. A falling paratrooper is on the map in the ground or deck layer, so M6c area effects (chrono weapons S-0514, radiation S-0525) and M7 effects reach it through the ordinary object lists.

Provisional rules of M6b parachutes and drops, each tied to a request: none are open since S-0522 revision 4 (Q-0736, Q-0750 to Q-0754 are answered and their interim rows are gone).

## Simulation (M6b: multiplayer triggers)

S-0524 is built in `glasnost_sim::mp_triggers`, on the trigger runtime of S-0011 (`triggers`). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0524.md](../m6-claims/S-0524.md). The tests are `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0524.rs`.

- **Tag carriers of the S-0011 runtime (S-0011 r29 claim 2, S-0312 r54 claim 29; Q-2716 answered; M8 A3 intake 113, [intake113.md](../m8-claims/intake113.md)).** Being destroyed, leaving play and being removed detach no tag: under `quirks.removed_carrier_keeps_tag` (default on, the original) `objects::remove_with` leaves the object's entry in `TriggerState::objects` and its place in the tag's carrier count, and nothing gives the place back when the object stops existing, so an all-carriers tag that lost a carrier this way does not fire at its last carrier's raise (claim 10, scenario T6 variant H). The stale entries are harmless: every reader of `objects` filters on `World::obj`, and object numbers are never reused. Off: the removal detaches the tag (`triggers::detach_object`), as before intake 113. An infantry unit showing its death keeps its tag through its removal in the same way; a leftover carries its tag to the defeat sweep (S-0203 claim 13 (g)). Tests: `claims_s0011_r29.rs`.
- **Import.** The converter reads each action's fourth to seventh items into `Action::items` (code 40's rectangle) and the trigger's field 8 into `TriggerDef::carry_over`. The demolition warhead's `Bright` and `CombatLightSize` are read into `WarheadRules::bright` and `light_size`.
- **Storm, strike and nuclear actions (claims 1 to 3).** Code 102 requests a storm through `storm::request`, owned by the trigger's owner house. Code 90 makes a cloud through `mp_triggers::lightning_strike`, which records the waypoint for `quirks.repeated_lightning_strike_suppressed`. Code 95 launches the payload through `nuke::waypoint`.
- **Demolition blast and flash (claims 5 and 6).** An undefined marker acts at the stand-in point (128, 128, 0) and makes no bridge attempt. `demolition_blast` makes five area detonations with the demolition warhead and no source or house, then calls `bridges::cell_attempts`. `light_flash` pushes an `Effect::Trigger` with code 66, the waypoint point and the flash-size level as its number; the presentation does not draw it yet.
- **Ambient (claim 7).** `TriggerState::mp.ambient` holds the base and target values code 73 set; the renderer does not read them yet.
- **Local area (claim 8).** Shrinking touches no object (it does not remove, move, stop or switch off buildings or units; an excluded power source and an excluded building still count; an ordered attack on an object outside is still an attack). `resize_local_area` sets the footprint's local area, recalculates every legal cell, recomputes the passability categories and rebuilds the reach regions.
- **Restoration (claims 9 and 13).** The record is in creation order with top-left cells; a building of the type and house that covers the recorded cell with any foundation cell is healed; otherwise a fresh building is placed by ordinary during-play placement (construction queued; claim 15, Q-0840 answered: the ready mark is clear at entry, the placed case of S-0406 claim 17, even without a build-up sprite) and the ground-layer occupants of its foundation are re-placed in reverse lifting order by the 1 x 1 nearby search from their old cells, facing 0, removed when no cell is found. `record_starting_buildings` runs in `load::new_game` after the game-start terrain pass. `restore_starting_buildings` heals a recorded building that is still there, and otherwise replaces it, displacing mobile ground units.
- **Entries (claims 10 to 14).** An infantry unit arriving at a transport raises its tag once before the acceptance test (`transport::arrival`); vehicle boarding, depot and refinery docking and pad landing raise nothing (tested in the owning claim files); capture makes no ownership-change raise; `vehicle_like_entry_seam` is new. `carry_over` moves a flagged tag to the captured or hijacked object. `capture_seam`, `hijack_seam`, `consuming_entry_seam` and `retaining_entry_seam` hold the raise order for S-0509, S-0312 claim 13's hijack, S-0510 and S-0508 when those are built.
- **Quirks.** `restoration_failed_return_removes_unit` (on), `repeated_lightning_strike_suppressed`, `trigger_crate_respects_setting` and `trigger_crate_expiry_while_disabled` (off: off is the original) are new (443 with the upgrade switches of S-0215). The two crate switches are not consulted, because crates (S-0520) are not built.
- **Save format 28.** The trigger definitions' new fields, the warhead's flash fields and `TriggerState::mp` are new state, and they are hashed.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=trigger-coverage cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` starts every offered map and counts its trigger events and actions as implemented, seamed or outside the subset (`mp_triggers::event_coverage`, `action_coverage`).
- **Seams.**
  - Code 108 (crates) places a crate through `crates::place_trigger` (S-0520).
  - Engineer capture (S-0509), hijacking (S-0312 claim 13), garrisons (S-0508), infiltration (S-0510) and sabotage call nothing yet; their seams are in `mp_triggers`.
  - Teams, computer targeting, change owner, destroy attached objects, explosions, cheering and the waypoint animations stay as S-0011 left them.

Provisional rules of M6b multiplayer triggers, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_triggers::resolve_house` | position values A to H take the house at that start position; S-0009 claim 29's rule for header counts of 9 or more is not applied | — |

Aircraft shadow anchor (S-0105 claim 40, Q-0799 answered): `mapview::build::vehicle_voxels` draws an aircraft shadow at the projection of its horizontal position at the terrain height there (`Mobile::shadow`, from `live::shadow_anchor`; a map-only scene reads the terrain from the map), 60 pixels higher over a qualifying high bridge, whatever its flight height. Q-0841 is answered by S-0501 revision 15, claim 43: an aircraft counts as marked on a deck only by its own deck mark (`AirState::deck`, read by `live::shadow_anchor`), never by its layer or its listing. The mark is set by a descent that reaches height 0 above a high-bridge deck or a landing that finishes at deck height, cleared by an ordinary climbing tick or a refused touchdown, and left alone by horizontal flight (`air::tick`, `air::touchdown`). Altitude calculations use the deck as their reference for a marked aircraft, and for an unmarked one at least 416 leptons above a high-bridge cell (`air::height`); `air::listing_height` and `air::sync_listing` list a flying aircraft in the ground layer, the deck layer or neither (S-0209 claim 13). Vehicle shadow anchors stay as the body point (open in S-0105). The paradrop plane's flight height (1500 leptons in the lane) is drawn. The pitch of a rocket body and the pitch and bank of a flying aircraft are drawn since M6c step 1, section *Rendering of aircraft attitude (M6c)* below.

Save format 29 to 31 (M6b2): one bump for the step-2 to step-4 state changes: the silo's busy-until tick (`ObjSw::silo_busy_until`), `FallState::chute_once`, `Cloud::delay` meaning the effective stage delay, and the cloak generator's `was_on` phase mark. Format 31 adds the warp member's `entry_ok` (the claim 50 placement check). Format 30 adds `AirState::deck` (the aircraft deck mark, S-0501 claim 43) and the warp member's `fell_back` and `refused` marks (S-0513 claims 50 and 51). Format 28 is the multiplayer trigger and driving-destination state.

## Simulation (M6c: garrisons)

S-0508 (revision 7) is built in `glasnost_sim::structures` (`garrison`, `entry`, `ownership`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0508.md](../m6-claims/S-0508.md), the tests are `claims_s0508.rs` and `claims_s0508_fire.rs` (fixtures in `structures_common`). New state is `Object::structure` (`StructState`: a building's occupants in order, the shared occupant/muzzle index, an occupant's building); save format version 32.

- **Entry (claims 1 to 6).** `walk::tick` calls `structures::entry::arrival` at the arrival update, after the boarding test and before the arrival raise of S-0208 claim 22: an infantry unit in *capture* whose destination is a building that stands in its cell. An occupier or assaulter type makes the garrison attempt (`garrison::attempt`: the occupation test of S-0508 claim 1, `targeting::may_garrison`, shared with the cursor of S-0214 row I8; one entry raise; the unit leaves the map through `objects::take_inside` and is appended to the occupants; or the refusal of claim 4, `entry::refuse`). The announcement is `Effect::Sound::Garrisoned`, which the audio crate plays for the entering unit's house (`BuildingGarrisonedSound`, `EVA_StructureGarrisoned`).
- **The building's turn (claims 7 to 9).** `econ::events::turn` calls `garrison::turn` for a civilian garrison building (garrisonable, tech level exactly −1): a badly damaged one evicts, the owner follows the first occupant while it belongs to the civilian house, an empty one returns to the civilian house (`ownership::change`, which runs `econ::events::owner_changed`) with `Effect::Sound::Abandoned` for the owner it lost it from (`BuildingAbandonedSound`; the announcement only when the *garrison abandoned* radar event is created, F7).
- **Eviction (claims 10 to 13).** `garrison::evict` with the options of `Evict` (`PLAIN`: deploy order, forced evacuation, destruction, the map's action 111; `SALE`: the inside fallback; `HUNT`: the house-wide hunt action 6).
